# Crawler v2 progress

One entry per work package, appended after its checks pass (spec: `full-crawler-spec.md`, "Reporting"). Nothing here claims a check that was not run.

## WP1. Sites from survivor reports (app) — 2026-11-17

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- `CrawlSource` gained `origin` (`ADMIN` default, `REPORTED`, `DISCOVERED`), `hops` (default 0), `discoveredFromId`, `render` (`HTTP` default, `BROWSER`, `AUTO`), `lastCrawledAt` and `pagesCrawled` (default 0). Additive migration `asmita/prisma/migrations/20261117000000_crawl_source_origins/`; existing rows read as `ADMIN`.
- New `CrawlBlockedHost` table (host unique, reason, addedBy, createdAt). The admin API for it is WP2; the table and its matching rule are live now.
- New `asmita/src/lib/crawl-never.ts`: the built-in never-crawl list (major social platforms, search engines, app stores, webmail, banks and payment sites, government `*.gov.*` and `*.nic.in`, news wires, Asmita's own domains) with the "equals or subdomain" rule. `*.gov.*` is read fail-safe as "any hostname with a label `gov`", so `whitehouse.gov` (where `gov` is the last label) is covered too.
- `ensureCrawlSourceForReportedHost` (`asmita/src/lib/crawl-sources.ts`): creates one enabled `REPORTED` source (host only, never the link or the case, no approver) with the env defaults, idempotent per host, race-safe (unique `domain`; ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING on Kysely, P2002 catch on Prisma). Skips never-crawl hosts: the built-in list first, then the `CrawlBlockedHost` table (host and every parent domain, one indexed lookup per name). Writes a `CRAWL_SOURCE_REPORTED` audit entry on the new source only — no actor, no case.
- Hooks: `addUrlsToCase` and `registerCaseWithUrls` (`asmita/src/lib/case-ops.ts`) and final finding approval (`asmita/src/lib/crawl-findings.ts`). A failure while creating a source never fails the survivor's action (fixed error code only, never her host).
- Env defaults, read only in `asmita/src/lib/crawler-flags.ts`: `CRAWLER_REPORTED_PAGE_BUDGET` (2000), `CRAWLER_DISCOVERED_PAGE_BUDGET` (200, used from WP5), `CRAWLER_DEFAULT_DELAY_MS` (1500). Unset or invalid falls back to the default; out-of-range clamps to the admin bounds. Documented in `asmita/.env.example` and the runbook.
- `GET /api/crawler/sources` now returns `origin` (lowercase `admin`/`reported`/`discovered`), `hops` and `render` (`http`/`browser`/`auto`) per the v2 contract, on the memory, Kysely and Prisma paths. The admin sources view (`CrawlSourceView`) carries the new fields too; the admin UI columns are WP2.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/crawl-never.test.ts` (38), `tests/unit/crawl-reported-sources.test.ts` (12, memory), `tests/unit/crawl-reported-sources-prisma.test.ts` (11, mocked Prisma), `tests/unit/crawl-reported-sources-kysely.test.ts` (7, recording Kysely fake in the style of the other crawler tests). 68 new tests.
- Existing tests changed only where the contract changed on purpose: `crawler-service.test.ts` (sources-route shape gained `origin`/`hops`/`render`), `admin-crawler-ui.test.ts` (its `CrawlSourceView` fixture gained the new fields). Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 18 test files / 248 tests all passed, build ok. `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 124 test files / 976 tests all passed. No `crawler/` source was touched.

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the file is protected agent-instruction file and the write needs the owner's approval, which did not come. Intended row addition (apply on approval): after the existing crawler-row sentence about `CRAWLER_SHOW_FINDINGS_TO_SURVIVOR` — "Crawler v2 WP1: a source records its `origin` — when a survivor's submitted link or an approved finding names a host, the app creates an enabled REPORTED source for that host (host only, never the link or case, idempotent, nothing linking it to her after erasure); hosts on the never-crawl list (`src/lib/crawl-never.ts`, `CrawlBlockedHost`) are never auto-created". The runbook, `crawler/README.md` and ADR 006 were updated instead.
- Migrations still exercised only against mocks and the memory store; not applied to a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook open item, unchanged). The new migration is additive only.
- The Worker smoke with the crawler step (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) was not run (pre-existing open item).
- The admin UI does not yet show origin/hops/last-crawled columns or a never-crawl tab — WP2.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- `registerCaseWithUrls` is hooked in addition to the spec's `addUrlsToCase`: on the Worker the Kysely backend stores a survivor's submitted links at case creation only (`addUrlsToCase` there is the memory and Prisma paths), so the spec's "all three backends" is otherwise unreachable. Every path that stores a survivor link now creates the source; nothing else changed.
- A new audit event `CRAWL_SOURCE_REPORTED` (enum value added) instead of no audit: the repo convention audits every source change; the entry names the source, its host and budget only — no actor, no case — so it adds no link to the survivor.
- `*.gov.*` is matched fail-safe as "any label `gov`" (so `x.gov` matches too); the literal reading would leave bare `.gov` hosts unblocked.
- ADR 006 received a dated note in "Decisions recorded since acceptance" saying WP1 supersedes its N4 in part; the spec's doc list does not name the ADR, but leaving N4 as written would contradict the accepted spec.
- Environment flake, not a code issue: the first crawler `npm run check` in this container failed with vitest fork-pool crashes ("Worker exited unexpectedly", 8 errors, counts truncated); an immediate rerun was fully green and so was the final `check.sh`.
- Repository history quirk: the WP1 code, tests and docs were committed by a concurrently running session in this same worktree before this progress entry was written (commit "WP1: commit remaining work after passing checks"); the WP1 progress entry and the ADR 006 note follow in a second commit. Same author line, same work; nothing else was included.

## WP2. Bulk import and never-crawl list (app + admin UI) — 2026-11-17

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- `POST /api/admin/crawl-sources/import` (`crawler:manage`, CSRF-checked, 503 `crawler_disabled` while `ENABLE_CRAWLER` is off): up to 1000 entries per request, bare hosts or full URLs (the host is extracted, then validated exactly like a single add). One result per entry — `created`, `exists` (already a source, or a repeat within the request), `blocked` (built-in never-crawl list or a `CrawlBlockedHost` row, including parent domains), `invalid` — so duplicates and blocked hosts are reported, never errors. Omitted `pageBudget`/`delayMs` default to the WP1 env knobs (`CRAWLER_REPORTED_PAGE_BUDGET` / `CRAWLER_DEFAULT_DELAY_MS`, 2000/1500); `enabled` defaults to false, like the single add.
- `importCrawlSources` (`asmita/src/lib/crawl-sources.ts`): a fixed handful of queries whatever the list size — one read of existing sources, one read of blocked rows over every host and every parent, one bulk insert with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING (a concurrent import of the same host cannot error or double-create; on the Prisma path one read settles the created rows and their ids) — so 1000 hosts import within the Worker's per-request subrequest limits. Built-in never-crawl hosts are filtered before any query.
- `writeAuditLogs` (`asmita/src/lib/audit.ts`): a batched audit writer — one LOCK, one previous-hash lookup, one insert of all rows, in one transaction, with the same hash chain and row shape as one-by-one writes. `writeAuditLog` now delegates to it with a single event; behaviour is unchanged and no existing test needed to change. Each created source gets one `ADMIN_ACTION` entry, action `crawl_source_imported`, like a single add.
- `GET/POST/DELETE /api/admin/crawl-blocked-hosts` (`crawler:manage`): list, add (host plus reason; the host is validated like a source host), remove by id. Reads work with the crawler off, changes return 503. Every change is audited (`crawl_blocked_host_added` / `crawl_blocked_host_removed`). New module `asmita/src/lib/crawl-blocked-hosts.ts` with memory, Kysely and Prisma paths; no schema change (`CrawlBlockedHost` came with WP1's migration, so no new migration).
- Admin "Crawler sites" page: paste-a-list import box (optional budget and delay, an explicit "switch on immediately" tick) with a results table of one row per entry; new columns origin, hops, pages crawled, last crawled; a filter by origin; and a "Never crawl" tab managing the blocked-host table (add with a reason, remove). Addresses stay plain text, never links (tested).
- No `crawler/` source was touched; the service contract in `crawler/README.md` is unchanged by this WP.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/crawl-import.test.ts` (13, memory path plus the route), `tests/unit/crawl-import-kysely.test.ts` (11, recording fake; includes the bounded-query proof — 1000 hosts are five statements plus one LOCK fragment — and the batched hash-chained audit insert), `tests/unit/crawl-import-prisma.test.ts` (7, mocked Prisma: createMany with skipDuplicates, the concurrent-creation race, the batched audit insert inside the locked transaction), `tests/unit/crawl-blocked-hosts.test.ts` (9, CRUD, routes, and that a blocked host and its subdomains never become a REPORTED source), plus 7 new assertions in `tests/unit/admin-crawler-ui.test.ts` (import box, results table, origin columns and filter, never-crawl tab, text-not-links, crawler-off disablement). 47 new tests; the new admin UI files are also in that test's static no-fetch/no-link file list. Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 18 test files / 248 tests all passed, build ok (unchanged by this WP). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 128 test files / 1023 tests all passed (976 before this WP, +47).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was again **not** updated (protected agent-instruction file; the write needs the owner's approval, which did not come — same as WP1). Intended line addition (apply on approval): in the architecture list, extend "`/api/admin/findings*` and `/api/admin/crawl-sources` for analysts" to "`/api/admin/crawl-sources` (single add plus WP2 bulk import at `/import`) and `/api/admin/crawl-blocked-hosts` (WP2 never-crawl list) for analysts". The runbook (section 2) documents both new routes instead.
- The Worker (Kysely) path still has no test against a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook open item, unchanged): the Kysely coverage here is a recording fake. The 1000-host bound is proven on the fake, not on a deployed Worker.
- The admin UI was verified by static-render tests only, not by a human or an E2E run; the existing e2e crawler journey uses the unchanged single-add route and was not extended.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The spec does not say what an omitted `pageBudget`/`delayMs` in the import body defaults to; the WP1 env knobs (reported-source defaults) are used, so one deployment setting covers every automatically created source and the import, and the UI leaves the fields blank with "server default".
- A repeated entry within one request reports `exists`: the host is on the list once the first entry created it. The spec lists the status words but not which applies to repeats.
- Import entries without a scheme are taken as bare hosts, so "example.com/x" is reported invalid rather than repaired into "example.com": the spec says "hosts or full URLs", and silently rewriting half-URLs invites mistakes on safety-relevant input.

## WP3. App-backed frontier (app + crawler) — 2026-11-17

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- App side: new `CrawlPage` table (additive migration `20261117010000_crawl_page_frontier/`): one row per page URL per source — `urlEncrypted` (encryptField) with `urlDigest` unique per source, `depth`, `kind` (page/sitemap/feed, for WP4), `etag`/`lastModified`, `status` (PENDING/FETCHED/NOT_MODIFIED/GONE/BLOCKED/ERROR/DEAD), `claimedUntil`, `lastFetchedAt`, `nextDueAt`, `failCount`. New module `asmita/src/lib/crawl-pages.ts` with memory, Kysely (Worker) and Prisma paths, aligned as required; memory store extended (`crawl-store.ts`).
- `POST /api/crawler/frontier/claim` (strict body, 256 KiB, rate-limited, 503 while `ENABLE_CRAWLER` is off): due rows of one source, selected `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` on both database paths (Kysely query builder; `$queryRaw` on Prisma) and marked claimed for 30 minutes, so two crawler instances never get the same URL. A source's first claim creates its seed row (`https://<domain>/`). A claim never crosses the source's remaining budget for the UTC day (pages with `lastFetchedAt` today), so a source stops handing out URLs for the day once `pageBudget` pages were fetched. Unknown or disabled sources claim empty, never an error. Validators are returned only when present (optional, not nullable).
- `POST /api/crawler/frontier/report`: per-page strict validation (a malformed entry is skipped without losing the others), scheduling per the spec — first visit as soon as possible; after `fetched` revisit in 24 h; after `not_modified` double the previous interval up to 14 days; after `error` back off 1 h / 6 h / 24 h and dead after 5 failures; `gone` dead — plus `blocked` rechecked after 24 h (the spec gives it no schedule). New in-scope links are stored at the reporting page's depth + 1, deduplicated per source on a keyed digest with the crawler's tracking-param rule (the URL as reported is kept, as findings do), and the in-scope check the crawler does is repeated by the app on every link (host equals the source domain or is a subdomain; credentials, non-default ports and non-http(s) refused, exactly the findings rules). Visited pages move `CrawlSource.pagesCrawled`/`lastCrawledAt`. `accepted` counts the page rows the report moved; DEAD/GONE rows are never resurrected by a late or retried report.
- Retention: `purgeExpiredCrawlPages` (url-retention.ts) runs with the hourly cron alongside the findings purge. Frontier rows follow the same 30 days; a row is operational state, not a record, so it is deleted, not blanked — except rows still due for crawling, which keep their pending work.
- Crawler side: the in-memory frontier (queue, seen set, queuePeak/seenPeak/linksNotQueued) is removed. `src/crawler.ts` claims batches (`FRONTIER_CLAIM_LIMIT`, new config knob, default 50, 1..200), fetches each page with `If-None-Match`/`If-Modified-Since` when the app has validators, maps outcomes (304 → `not_modified`, 404/410 → `gone`, robots or 401/403 → `blocked`, anything else → `error`) and reports results in body-bounded chunks under the server's 256 KiB limit; the report never carries more links per page than the contract's 500 even if `MAX_LINKS_PER_PAGE` is configured higher. The in-scope check stays in the crawler (claimed URL, every redirect hop, post-fetch belt-and-braces). 304 handling, extra request headers and response validators (`etag`, `lastModified`) were added to `src/net/safe-fetch.ts` — the only fetch path, unchanged in every other respect. A page the run was stopped on mid-way is not reported, so it is redone whole after the claim window: a killed run loses at most its claimed batch. New summary fields: `pagesNotModified`, `batchPeak` (the crawler's whole frontier memory is one batch plus its report). Claim/report failures count their error codes; 503/401 stop the run like the other API calls, and a failed report only breaks the source's loop (the claimed pages recover on their own).
- `CrawlDeps.seedUrl` and `runMain`'s `seedUrl` option are gone: seeds are the app's now. The test mock gained a faithful frontier (same rules, written independently) with a fixture `seedUrl`.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run diagram, bounded-frontier row, the two routes, `FRONTIER_CLAIM_LIMIT`, run summary), the runbook (section 2 frontier, env table, go-live migration list), ADR 006 dated note. `crawler/.env.example` gained `FRONTIER_CLAIM_LIMIT`.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New app tests: `tests/unit/crawl-frontier.test.ts` (17: seeds, 30-minute claims, killed-run recovery, daily budget, link validation/dedup/depth, every schedule branch, retention, both routes), `tests/unit/crawl-frontier-kysely.test.ts` (5: FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED recorded on the claim, claimedUntil set, one ON CONFLICT link insert, 200-page report = 203 bounded statements, 700 links chunked 500+200), `tests/unit/crawl-frontier-prisma.test.ts` (6: locked raw claim, seed P2002 race, budget gate, report writes, dead rows never resurrected, retention filter). 28 new tests.
- New crawler tests: `tests/integration/frontier-app.test.ts` (3: a second run claims nothing while pages are scheduled and then 304s instead of refetching — request headers asserted; two crawler processes against one mock never fetch the same URL and together find every planted image; a killed run loses only its claimed batch, which becomes due again after 30 minutes and is redone whole). `tests/integration/frontier.test.ts` rewritten for the flood site: crawler frontier memory stays at one batch (batchPeak <= claim limit), the app's frontier grows by at most MAX_LINKS_PER_PAGE per fetched page, the daily budget stops the source, the planted image is still found.
- Existing tests changed only where behaviour changed on purpose: `frontier.test.ts` (in-memory queue metrics are gone; the app-backed bounds replaced them), `lifecycle.test.ts` (page_not_in_source run pre-seeds its pages because the domain override now also gates the frontier; the created/duplicate run advances the mock clock a day because a second immediate run now correctly claims nothing), `url-robots-extract.test.ts` (FetchResult gained two fields), `url-retention-cron.test.ts` (the cron route now runs the third purge), `run-fixture.ts`/`fixture-entry.ts` (seedUrl moved from the crawler to the mock). Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 19 test files / 251 tests all passed, build ok (18 files / 248 tests before this WP). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 131 test files / 1051 tests all passed (128 / 1023 before).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval in the UI and the prompt timed out without a response (as for WP1 and WP2; silence is not consent). Intended row addition (apply on approval): in the architecture list, the crawler bullet's route list becomes "`GET /api/crawler/pool`, `GET /api/crawler/sources`, `POST /api/crawler/findings`, `POST /api/crawler/frontier/{claim,report}` (crawler v2 WP3: the app owns the page frontier — `CrawlPage` rows, URLs encrypted at rest and deduplicated per source on a digest, each claimed batch locked to one crawler for 30 minutes, 30-day retention as findings; the crawler service claims batches and reports per-page outcomes, so runs resume where they stopped and a killed run loses only its claimed batch) (service key from `scripts/issue-crawler-service-key.mjs`, only its sha256 stored); `/api/admin/findings*`, `/api/admin/crawl-sources` (single add plus WP2 bulk import) and `/api/admin/crawl-blocked-hosts` (WP2 never-crawl list) for analysts" — the same extension WP1/WP2 intended, plus the frontier routes.
- The Kysely (Worker) path still has no test against a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook open item, unchanged; the Kysely coverage here is a recording fake, the Prisma path a mocked client). The `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claim has therefore never actually run against Postgres, only been compiled and recorded.
- The Worker smoke with the crawler step (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) was not run (pre-existing open item, unchanged); it exercises pool/sources/findings, not the new routes.
- Frontier claim/report traffic is not audited, on purpose (see deviations); if the owner wants an audit trail of crawler activity beyond the key's `lastUsedAt`, that is a follow-up decision.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- `blocked` (robots.txt disallow, or a 401/403 answer) is rechecked after 24 h: the spec gives `gone`, `error` and `not_modified` schedules but none for `blocked`. Robots blocks are usually long-lived but can change, so a bounded daily recheck was chosen over killing the page.
- Every `fetched` page is scheduled 24 h ahead, not only fetched pages with new links (the spec names "after `fetched` with new links"); nothing in the contract distinguishes a fetched page without links, and a 24 h revisit is the defined baseline the `not_modified` doubling starts from.
- The daily budget is enforced at claim time as "pages with a `lastFetchedAt` within the UTC day < `pageBudget`", and a claim is also capped at the remaining budget; `error`/`blocked` pages do not count (they were not fetched). This is the reading of "stops handing out new URLs for the day once `pageBudget` pages were fetched that day" that cannot exceed the budget even when runs are killed mid-batch.
- The 30-day purge deletes `CrawlPage` rows instead of blanking them like findings: a frontier row without its URL can never be crawled again, and unlike a finding it is not a record of anything. Rows still due (not DEAD/GONE and past their `nextDueAt`) are kept, as the spec requires.
- A page the crawler is stopped on mid-way is not reported, so it is redone whole after the claim window — the strictest reading of "a killed run loses at most its claimed batch" (its already-hashed findings still go out; the server dedups them on the redo).
- Claim and report are not audited: they are per-page service traffic (orders of magnitude more rows than findings), they carry no admin action and no case link, and the audit log's hash chain would grow by one entry per page crawled. Source changes, findings and reveals stay audited as before.
- A report the server applied but whose answer was lost is re-applied on retry: schedules only move forward, links are deduplicated by digest, so the retry is safe; `pagesCrawled` may count such a page twice. Documented in `crawl-pages.ts`.
- The report caps a page's links at the contract's 500 even when `MAX_LINKS_PER_PAGE` is configured higher (the server would refuse the whole report entry otherwise).
- The mock's frontier seeds fixture sources with `http://<host>:<port>/` (production seeds `https://<domain>/` per spec); the mock also does not apply the app's non-default-port refusal to reported links, because the whole fixture site lives on a port — the port rule itself is covered by the app-side tests.

## WP4. Deep coverage of a site (crawler) — 2026-11-17

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- Sitemaps, end to end on the WP3 frontier: a source's robots.txt `Sitemap:` lines (up to 50, parsed independently of robots-parser) ride on the source's first frontier report as `kind: "sitemap"` links; sitemap indexes and urlsets are `CrawlPage` rows claimed, fetched and scheduled exactly like page rows — there is no second fetch path, and they cost `pageBudget` like any page. A `.gz` body (sitemap.xml.gz served without Content-Encoding) is inflated in memory under the same `MAX_HTML_BYTES` cap. The document kind is sniffed from the root element (`<urlset>`, `<sitemapindex>`, `<rss>`, `<feed>`), never from the URL or the Content-Type, which sites routinely get wrong.
- New `crawler/src/crawl/xml-docs.ts`: a bounded event scanner (no DOM, no new dependency, no temp file) with entity and CDATA handling; the parsers stop at `MAX_SITEMAP_URLS` taken addresses — page `<loc>`, child-sitemap `<loc>`, `<image:loc>` and feed entries all count against it — and at a hard markup-event cap, reporting truncation. Image sitemap `<image:loc>` entries are hashed during the sitemap's own fetch, attributed to their page (the `<loc>`), which must be in scope and not obvious non-content like any page; the normal image caps, budgets and off-scope-host rules apply unchanged.
- RSS and Atom feeds linked from pages (`<link rel="alternate">` with an rss/atom type, and anchors to `.rss`/`.atom`) are reported as `kind: "feed"` rows; their entries become page rows. RSS `<item><link>` text and Atom `rel="alternate"` (else the first rel-less link) are taken; channel-level links and `rel="self"` are not.
- Pagination: `rel="next"` anchors, `<link rel="next">` in the head, and anchors whose text or class marks next/older/page-N are collected into their own bounded bucket and reported first, so a link flood cannot push them past the per-page cap. Pagination-marked links skip the query-variant cap.
- More image candidates (`crawl/extract.ts`): CSS `background-image` in inline styles (every `url()` of background/background-image declarations), `<video poster>`, `<link rel="image_src">`, JSON-LD `image`/`images` (string, array or object with `url`/`contentUrl`; `@graph`-walked with depth and per-block size caps, malformed or oversized blocks counted, never fatal), `<noscript>` fallbacks (bounded re-parse when the parser kept the content as raw text), and the full-size target of a thumbnail link: `<a href=*.jpg><img></a>` now takes the href and suppresses the wrapped thumbnail (its src is a thumbnail of the same picture).
- Trap skipping: login, cart, search-result and calendar URLs are never reported or fetched, whatever markup marks them (exact path segments incl. `wp-login.php`, date-shaped paths `/YYYY/MM…` and `/YYYY-MM-DD`, date query keys, WordPress `?m=YYYYMM`, `?q=`/`?s=`/keyword search keys, `add-to-cart`) — a rel="next" link into an infinite calendar is skipped like any other calendar link. New `crawl/traps.ts` also adds a per-source `QueryVariantGuard`: at most `MAX_QUERY_VARIANTS_PER_PATH` distinct query variants of one path are reported (page-number parameters — `page`, `paged`, `pg`, `start`, `offset`, `from` — and tracking parameters do not count); its state is bounded 16-byte hashes (at most 4096 paths), never a URL.
- Configuration (Zod, fail closed) and docs: `MAX_SITEMAP_URLS` (default 5000) and `MAX_QUERY_VARIANTS_PER_PATH` (default 8) in `src/config.ts`, `.env.example`, `crawler/README.md` (run flow, a new "Deep coverage" guarantee row, config table, run-summary fields, the bounded-state note) and the runbook's frontier section. `run_summary` gains `pagesXml`, `sitemapDocs`, `sitemapUrlsTaken`, `sitemapImages`, `feedsParsed`, `feedEntriesTaken`, `docsTruncated`, `linksSkippedNonContent`, `linksSkippedQueryCap`, `jsonldSkipped`, `noscriptSkipped`. No `asmita/` code changed.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/xml-docs.test.ts` (13: sniffing incl. gzip magic and XHTML non-matches, entities/CDATA, urlset with image entries, index children, per-document caps, truncated and tag-bomb documents, RSS and Atom entry choice, robots.txt Sitemap lines), `tests/unit/traps.test.ts` (8: the non-content URL families and their near-misses, the variant cap, page-number and tracking exemptions, bounded path map), `tests/unit/deep-extract.test.ts` (12: every new image candidate kind, feed discovery, pagination marking and flood priority, nofollow, the non-content skip and the thumbnail rule), `tests/integration/deep-coverage.test.ts` (7: the spec's done-when — a sitemap index, a gzipped sitemap, an Atom feed, 30 paginated listing pages and lazy/background/poster/JSON-LD/noscript/thumbnail images fully covered within budget with every planted image found exactly once, and the infinite-calendar and query-trap fixture staying inside its caps, plus the log scan). 40 new tests; no existing test file was changed, deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 23 test files / 291 tests all passed (19 / 251 before this WP), build ok. `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 131 test files / 1051 tests all passed (unchanged by this WP).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval, which did not come (as for WP1–WP3; silence is not consent). Intended row addition (apply on approval): extend the "Deep coverage" wording in `crawler/README.md` to the CLAUDE.md crawler row — "crawler v2 WP4: the crawler follows robots.txt Sitemap: lines, sitemap indexes, gzipped sitemaps, image-sitemap entries and RSS/Atom feeds through the same frontier as pages; pagination links get priority; login, cart, search and calendar URLs and query-string traps are skipped". The runbook and `crawler/README.md` document all of it instead.
- RSS 0.90/RDF (`<rdf:RDF>`) feeds and JSON Feed are not parsed: the spec names RSS and Atom, and RSS 2.0 plus Atom cover what sites publish today; an RDF root is not sniffed.
- A sitemap or feed document with more addresses than one report entry's link cap (min of `MAX_LINKS_PER_PAGE`, 500) contributes its first 500 per visit; the remainder stay for later runs, because the frontier contract caps one page entry's links at 500. Documented in `crawler/README.md`.
- Sitemap fetches were exercised against the local fixture site only; no real site was crawled (rule 7). The Docker image build, CI on Linux and the Worker smoke remain unrun (pre-existing open items, unchanged).

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The query-variant cap is not applied blindly: standard page-number parameters (`page`, `paged`, `pg`, `start`, `offset`, `from`) do not count as variants and pagination-marked links skip the guard. The spec's own done-when — "30 paginated listing pages … fully covered" — is impossible for a query-paginated listing under a blind cap (eight variants would cut it at page 8), so an ordered page sequence is treated as pagination, not a trap. Both exemptions stay bounded by the same caps as everything else (per-page link cap, depth, budgets).
- Image sitemap entries are hashed at the sitemap's own fetch, attributed to their `<loc>` page, rather than waiting for that page's row to be claimed: the sitemap row is the in-scope fetched page, the image is a candidate extracted from it, and attributing the finding to its real page (in scope, else the image is skipped) is what "image candidates for their page" requires. The usual dedup means the same image is never hashed twice if the page later lists it too.
- "`<a href=*.jpg><img></a>` takes the href" is implemented as: the href becomes the image candidate and the wrapped `<img>` is suppressed. Taking both would hash a thumbnail and its full-size copy of the same picture, doubling fetches for no finding.
- The non-content URL patterns are deliberately narrow — exact path segments, date-shaped paths, specific query keys — so nothing ambiguous is skipped; "obvious non-content" only. `?p=` (WordPress post ids) and bare numeric id parameters are content addresses, not traps, and are kept.
- Robots.txt `Sitemap:` discovery is attached to a source's first *reported page entry* of the run (whatever its status) rather than fetched outside the frontier: keeping sitemap fetches inside the claim/report path preserves every WP3 guarantee (two crawlers never fetch the same URL, a killed run loses only its batch, budgets count it), at the cost of sitemaps being discovered a claim later than a direct fetch would manage.

## WP5. Automatic discovery of new sites (crawler + app) — 2026-11-17

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- Crawler: the out-of-scope hosts of links on in-scope HTML pages are reported to the new `POST /api/crawler/discoveries` — hostnames only, never a URL, deduplicated per run, at most 200 per request, capped per run by the new `MAX_DISCOVERY_HOSTS` (default 1000; 0 switches discovery reporting off). The set is collected where links are already read (`processPage`'s admit), reported once per claimed batch in chunks of 200, best-effort: a failed report only delays those hosts to a later run (503/401 stop the run like every other API call). New summary fields: `discoveryHostsSeen`, `discoveryHostsReported`, `discoveryHostsCapped`. Off-scope sitemap/feed entries are NOT collected (see deviations). The crawler itself never requests a discovered host, not even robots.txt, until a later run's `GET /sources` lists it.
- App: new module `asmita/src/lib/crawl-discoveries.ts` with the discovery rules, on the memory, Kysely (Worker) and Prisma paths, aligned as required. `POST /api/crawler/discoveries` (bearer key, 503 while `ENABLE_CRAWLER` is off, 256 KiB, strict body, rate-limited like the other service routes). Per reported host: skipped when on the never-crawl list (built-in or `CrawlBlockedHost`, host and every parent) or already a source (whatever its origin); otherwise a `DISCOVERED` source is created with `hops = parent.hops + 1`, `discoveredFromId`, the `CRAWLER_DISCOVERED_PAGE_BUDGET` budget and the default delay. It is auto-enabled while `hops <= CRAWLER_MAX_DISCOVERY_HOPS` (default 2) and within `CRAWLER_MAX_NEW_SITES_PER_DAY` (default 200) auto-enables per UTC day; everything else is stored disabled with a reason. `accepted` counts the hosts stored, enabled or not.
- Daily-cap exactness: on both database paths the count of today's enabled DISCOVERED sources and the insert share one transaction holding `CrawlSource` exclusively (`LOCK TABLE ... IN EXCLUSIVE MODE`, the audit-writer pattern), so two concurrent reports cannot both spend the last slot. One discovery report is a fixed handful of queries whatever the host count: parent read, one existing-source read, one blocked-host read, one count, one `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` insert, one batched audit write.
- Promotion: `recordCrawlFindings` promotes a discovered site on any accepted finding (new or merged sighting — both prove the site hosts a match): `hops` reset to 0, budget raised to `CRAWLER_REPORTED_PAGE_BUDGET` (never lowered), idempotent, wrapped so a failure can never lose the finding. Audited (`CRAWL_SOURCE_PROMOTED`, actor the service key).
- Pause: the hourly retention cron (`/api/cron/purge-expired-urls`, which already runs the crawler retention sweeps) now also runs `sweepDiscoveredCrawlSources`: it resumes expired pauses first, then pauses every enabled DISCOVERED source whose page budget was spent on each of the last three UTC days (today counts as the third) with not one finding on it in that window — switched off for 30 days (`pausedUntil`, `pauseReason "no_match_budget_spent"`), resumed by the same sweep. A paused source is simply disabled, so the frontier and the crawler's source list needed no new rules; days paused fetch no pages, so a resumed site cannot be re-paused until it spends its budget on three fresh days. The sweep is bounded: one candidates read, one grouped count per day of the window, one distinct findings read, one update each way, one batched audit write. New `CrawlFinding` index on `sourceId` (additive) carries the findings read.
- Schema (additive migration `20261117020000_crawl_discovery_pause/`): `CrawlSource.pausedUntil`, `pauseReason`, `disabledReason` ("max_hops" | "daily_cap"), four new audit event types, the `CrawlFinding_sourceId_idx` index. An admin switching a paused or stored-disabled site on clears the automatic state (`updateCrawlSource`); from there the sweep never touches it.
- Admin page: the "Crawler sites" table gains a "Found via" column (the discovered site's parent domain, resolved in `listCrawlSources` from rows already loaded — no query per source) and a "State" column saying why a discovered site is paused or stored disabled; addresses stay plain text, never links.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run flow, "Discovery never fetches" guarantee row, bounded-state note, the new endpoint, `MAX_DISCOVERY_HOSTS`, run-summary fields), the runbook (section 2 "Discovered sites", env tables both sides), `asmita/.env.example`, `crawler/.env.example`, ADR 006 dated note.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New app tests: `tests/unit/crawl-discoveries.test.ts` (17, memory path plus the route: the A→B→C→D hop chain, never-crawl and already-a-source skips, the daily cap and its day reset, the parent-must-be-enabled gate, the audit entry, promotion via `recordCrawlFindings` incl. idempotence and an untouched admin source, every pause/resume branch, admin-enable clearing, the admin-page fields), `tests/unit/crawl-discoveries-kysely.test.ts` (8, recording fake: the fixed-statement proof for 200 hosts, the count inside the lock, ON CONFLICT, both LOCK fragments, promotion and sweep statements, the batched audit), `tests/unit/crawl-discoveries-prisma.test.ts` (8, mocked client: locked transaction shape, cap from the count, settle-read race, promotion, resume/pause updates, findings-in-window guard). Plus 3 new tests in `tests/unit/admin-crawler-ui.test.ts` (parent column, pause/disable sentences, the state label). 36 new tests.
- New crawler tests: `tests/integration/discovery.test.ts` (3: the spec's done-against — the a.test→b.test→c.test→d.test fixture crawled across four runs against one mock whose source list grows: b and c crawled automatically once listed, d stored disabled and never fetched, the never-crawl host neither stored nor fetched, a match on c promotes it, the per-run cap holds with `MAX_DISCOVERY_HOSTS=2`, the daily cap holds with one auto-enable per day, and the log scan stays clean), plus one client test in `tests/unit/client-findings.test.ts` (never an empty or oversized discovery batch, strict response shape). 4 new tests.
- Existing tests changed only where behaviour changed on purpose: `url-retention-cron.test.ts` (the cron route gained the sweep: mocked, asserted, and the response fields), `admin-crawler-ui.test.ts` fixtures gained the new `CrawlSourceView` fields. The mock's `GET /sources` now returns configured plus enabled discovered sources, mirroring the app; existing fixtures are unaffected (verified by the full suite). Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 24 test files / 295 tests all passed (23 / 291 before this WP), build ok. `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 134 test files / 1087 tests all passed (131 / 1051 before).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was again **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval, which did not come (as for WP1–WP4; silence is not consent). Intended row addition (apply on approval): extend the crawler architecture bullet's route list with `POST /api/crawler/discoveries` (crawler v2 WP5: the crawler reports the out-of-scope hosts its pages link — hostnames only, deduplicated per run, capped per run — and the app's rules decide which become DISCOVERED sources, auto-enabled within a hop limit and a daily cap, promoted on a match, paused for 30 days after three budget-spent days without one; the crawler never fetches a discovered host until the app lists it). The runbook, `crawler/README.md` and the ADR 006 note document all of it instead.
- The Kysely (Worker) and Prisma paths still have no test against a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook open item, unchanged): the Kysely coverage is a recording fake, the Prisma path a mocked client. The `LOCK TABLE "CrawlSource" IN EXCLUSIVE MODE` fragment has therefore never actually run, only been compiled and recorded. The new migration is additive only but has not been applied to a real database.
- The Worker smoke with the crawler step (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) was not run (pre-existing open item, unchanged); the Docker image build, CI on Linux and the E2E crawler journey were not run either (pre-existing open items, unchanged).
- The admin UI was verified by static-render tests only, not by a human or an E2E run.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- `accepted` counts the hosts stored as sources, enabled or not. The contract says only `{ accepted: number }`; the disabled-but-stored hosts are part of what the app did with the report, and the crawler logs the count as `discoveryHostsReported` either way.
- The reporting parent must exist AND be enabled at report time (the spec is silent). A disabled or paused source must not grow the crawl list; a source disabled mid-run already handed out its claimed batch, and losing those few hosts to the next run is the safe direction.
- Discovery hosts come from anchors on fetched HTML pages only — the same candidates already read for links — not from off-scope sitemap/feed document entries. The spec says "linked from in-scope pages"; sitemaps are machine-generated lists where cross-host entries are usually noise, and treating them as discoveries would let a hostile sitemap amplify source-row creation 5000-fold per document. The app's never-crawl/existing filters and the daily auto-enable cap would still bound crawling, not row creation.
- A run-side cap `MAX_DISCOVERY_HOSTS` was added (the spec names none). Everything else the crawler keeps is bounded; without it a hostile approved page could grow the run-wide host set to pages × links. It bounds reporting, never crawling.
- The pause criterion is read as: pages fetched >= `pageBudget` on each of the last three UTC days (today as the third) and no finding with `lastSeenAt` inside the window. "No match after its budget is spent three days running" is ambiguous about repeats and window edges; `lastSeenAt` (any sighting, new or repeat) is the generous reading for the site, the day-bucketed count matches the daily budget the claim gate already enforces, and a promoted (hops 0) site stays subject to the rule — it is still a discovered site, and the no-finding-in-window check exempts it exactly when it still matches.
- The daily cap counts DISCOVERED sources that are enabled and were created today, so an admin enabling a same-day discovered site consumes a slot. The invariant the spec asks for ("at most N auto-enabled per day") still holds — the count can only be reached sooner; distinguishing admin enables would need another column for a corner the lock already makes safe.
- Pause/disable reasons are stored on the source (`pauseReason`, `disabledReason`, `pausedUntil`) rather than derived. The admin page must show "why they are paused or disabled" (spec) also after restarts and across the sweep's 30-day window; the reasons are fixed codes, not free text.
- The sweep rides the hourly URL-retention cron, not the maintenance cron: that route already runs the crawler's retention sweeps and is documented as the crawler's hourly job; the maintenance route is deliverability and alerting. The route's response gained `discoveredSourcesPaused` / `discoveredSourcesResumed`.
- Promotion fires on any accepted finding, merged sightings included: a repeat sighting still proves the site hosts a match, and promotion is idempotent, so the only cost of the wider trigger is one cheap read per accepted finding.

## WP6. Continuous running and parallel sources (crawler) — 2026-11-17

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- `node dist/main.js run --loop` (`src/cli.ts`): pass after pass across all enabled sources until stopped. A pass that ends 0 starts the next one, sleeping the new `LOOP_IDLE_SECONDS` (default 60 s, 1..3600) whenever it claimed nothing; SIGTERM ends the current pass cleanly and exits 0; any non-zero exit code ends the loop with that code, so an always-on deployment's restart policy is the retry cadence. A source switched on in the app mid-run is picked up on the next pass's `GET /sources`; a crawler switched off on the server (503, exit 0) does not exit but polls every idle interval until the switch returns. The argv parser now accepts `run` and `run --loop` only — anything else exits 64 — and without `--loop` the one-shot path is unchanged.
- Source-level concurrency (`src/crawler.ts`): sources run in parallel up to the new `CRAWLER_SOURCE_CONCURRENCY` (default 8, 1..32) instead of `GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY`. Each source stays sequential; every request still passes the `GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY` gate, robots.txt and the per-host delay, so a slow or failing source occupies only its own worker slot. No other crawl path changed.
- Summary and progress: `run_summary` gains `sourcesActive` (sources that claimed work), `pagesClaimed` (every page the app handed out; `pagesAttempted` remains the ones actually processed) and `newUrls` (link entries the app's frontier accepted). A new periodic `run_progress` line (every `PROGRESS_LOG_SECONDS`, default 60 s) carries sources active, pages claimed/fetched/not-modified, new URLs, new hosts reported, images hashed, matches and the pool size — counts only, through the same sanitising Logger, whose field allowlist gained the new counters. A `loop_idle` line marks each idle sleep.
- Runbook (`asmita/docs/ops/crawler-runbook.md`): section 4 step 5 explains running it continuously — a VM container with a restart policy (the always-on form) or the existing Cloud Run job on a 15–30-minute Cloud Scheduler cadence (near-continuous with no new moving parts; frontier claims are exclusive for 30 minutes by WP3). A Cloud Run *service* is deliberately not offered: Cloud Run requires a service's container to listen on a port, the crawler listens on nothing by design, and adding a listener only to pass a health check would grow its attack surface for no crawl benefit. The env table gains the three knobs, section 5 says what each code means for a loop deployment, and the kill-switch wording covers stopping one. Updated alongside: `crawler/README.md` (intro, run flow, exit codes, a new guarantees row, run summary, config table, deployment notes), `crawler/.env.example`, and a `crawler/Dockerfile` comment (the image serves both modes; the command line decides — the ENTRYPOINT/CMD split needed no change).
- No `asmita/` code changed; the service contract is unchanged by this WP.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/integration/continuous.test.ts` (5) — the spec's done-when, end to end. Twenty fixture sources of mixed speed (100–300 ms pages, 4.1 s of pure serial page time) finish within a third of the serial bound, which only concurrent source crawling can meet (measured ≈0.8 s with the default 8 workers), every source active, `pagesClaimed`/`pagesFetched`/`newUrls` asserted exactly. One hanging source (a home page that never answers, ended by the request deadline) does not delay the others: all 20 fast pages fetched and reported while it hung, its error report is the last one the run sent, and the whole run stays under half the serial-plus-hang time; the periodic `run_progress` line is asserted there too (present while the hang was pending, every field a count). `run --loop` (the real `runMain`, stopped with an AbortController) sleeps when nothing is due (its first idle pass is the second one, `loop_idle` logged), picks up a source switched on in the mock mid-run on a later pass, and exits 0 on SIGTERM; the loop ends with code 2 when the mock revokes the key between passes, after exactly two source listings; the command line keeps `run` and rejects every other argv with 64, and `--loop` without a configuration stops before any network activity. The log scan (no hostnames, no URLs, no hex runs) covers every new line. Supporting additions: a `buildParallelFixture` (20 mixed-speed hosts plus one hanging host, no images) and a `statusAfter` option on the mock API (a route answers a status once more than N calls reached it). No existing test file was changed, deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 25 test files / 300 tests all passed (24 / 295 before this WP), build ok. `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 134 test files / 1087 tests all passed (unchanged by this WP).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the write was attempted and is blocked by the platform as a protected agent-instruction file; the approval prompt timed out without a response (as for WP1–WP5; silence is not consent). Intended row addition (apply on approval): in the architecture list, extend "`crawler/` (outside `asmita/`) is the service itself." to "…is the service itself; it runs one pass per invocation (`run`) or continuously (`run --loop`, crawler v2 WP6: sources crawled concurrently under a global request cap, an idle sleep when nothing is due, any non-zero exit code ends the loop and the deployment's restart policy is the retry)." The runbook and `crawler/README.md` document all of it instead.
- The concurrency bound proves ≥3 parallel sources (the assertion is a third of serial time); asserting all 8 workers per wave would be flaky on shared runners, and the hang test separately proves a stuck source holds nothing but its own slot.
- Loop mode was driven in-process through the real `runMain` (an AbortController standing in for SIGTERM), not as an OS process receiving a real signal; the real-signal wiring is the same path `process.test.ts` covers for the one-shot command, which is unchanged.
- The runbook's continuous-running procedures (VM container, short-cadence job) are documentation only; nothing has been deployed (pre-existing open item). The Docker image was not rebuilt in this WP (comment-only change), and the Cloud Run port/health-check claim was checked against Google's public documentation and support threads, not against a live deployment. CI on Linux and the Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged).

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The loop ends on **any** non-zero exit code, not only the unambiguously fatal ones: exit codes are the operator contract ("a run exits 0 only when nothing needs an operator's attention"), so looping through 3 (findings undelivered) or 5 (pool stale) would swallow the signal; an always-on deployment's restart policy provides the retry. Exit 0 — including SIGTERM, the wall-clock limit, the budgets and the crawler being switched off — keeps the loop running, so a disabled crawler polls quietly instead of exiting.
- "Sleeps when nothing is due" is implemented as "a pass that claimed nothing sleeps; a pass that did work starts the next immediately" — that next pass then finds nothing due and sleeps, so every idle gap is still bounded by one interval.
- The always-on forms are the VM container and the short-cadence Cloud Run job, not a Cloud Run *service*: the spec offers "Cloud Run service or a VM container with restart policy", and a Cloud Run service requires its container to listen on a port (deployment fails otherwise); the crawler deliberately listens on nothing, and an HTTP listener added only to pass a health check would grow its attack surface for no crawl benefit.
- Two new config knobs beyond the spec's named `CRAWLER_SOURCE_CONCURRENCY`: `LOOP_IDLE_SECONDS` (the spec says "sleeps when nothing is due" without an interval) and `PROGRESS_LOG_SECONDS` (the spec asks for periodic progress logs without a period). Both Zod-validated, fail closed, documented in `.env.example`, the README and the runbook.
- `sourcesActive` is the cumulative count of sources that claimed work during the pass — the same counter in the summary and the progress line — not a live in-flight gauge: one metric, no divergence between the two lines, and `source_done` already logs each source's live duration.
- `pagesClaimed` is added beside `pagesAttempted` rather than replacing it: the app's claim and the crawler's attempt can differ (a run stopped between a claim and its first page), the loop's idle test asks whether the app had anything due, and the summary now answers both questions.
- The Asmita API client's connection pipeline (2) was deliberately not raised to the source concurrency: claim/report/findings calls are small and queue politely at the app's single origin, while crawl fetches are unaffected (they use the SafeFetcher's own agent); raising it would only shift load onto the Worker.

## WP7. Pages that need a browser (crawler) — 2026-11-17

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- Rendering rule, exactly as specified: a source with `render = BROWSER` has every HTML page rendered; an `AUTO` page is rendered only when its HTML carries a script tag and the static extractor finds no image candidates; `http` (the default) never launches a browser, and a run with no browser sources never spawns the worker (it is started lazily on the first render).
- Playwright Chromium, headless, in its own child process (`src/browser/browser-worker.ts`, bundled as `dist/browser-worker.js`, `playwright-core` pinned to 1.60.0 — the same version as the app's `@playwright/test`, so both share one Chromium 1223). One page at a time, always: the parent-side renderer (`src/browser/browser-renderer.ts`) serialises renders, so the spec's "one page at a time per source" holds a fortiori across sources. The worker gets a scrubbed environment (TMPDIR/HOME = the memory temp dir, `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` only — never a key, config or fingerprint), runs under Node's permission model (reads: its own bundle, the node_modules listing and `playwright-core`, the browser directory, read-only system metadata; writes: only the memory temp dir; child process allowed for Chromium; no addons, no worker threads) and has stdout/stderr discarded like the decoder's. The IPC protocol (`src/browser/protocol.ts`) is validated strictly on both sides and any unexpected shape, field or id kills and replaces the worker — the decoder rule.
- All browser traffic goes through an in-process forward proxy (`src/net/browser-proxy.ts`): a SOCKS5 server, because with a SOCKS proxy Chromium hands the proxy the hostname (remote DNS), so the proxy sees every destination before it is contacted and no HTTP absolute-URI parsing is needed. It applies the same `NetPolicy` object safe-fetch uses: the port must be allowed (checked for both schemes, since SOCKS carries none), the hostname is resolved through the policy's resolver and refused when ANY address is blocked, and the dial goes to the validated address — no rebinding window. A literal destination (Chromium sends literal IPs as domain-type strings) is checked directly against the blocklist. Chromium is launched with `--proxy-bypass-list=<-loopback>` so even loopback destinations go through the proxy (and are then refused by the production policy). Per render the parent sets a byte account: all tunnels summed must stay under `BROWSER_MAX_PAGE_BYTES` or the proxy destroys them, the render aborts and the page reports `error`.
- Nothing on disk: no persistent profile (a fresh context per render; Playwright's throwaway profile lives under the worker's TMPDIR, which the parent points at `BROWSER_TMP_DIR`, a tmpfs — `/dev/shm` in the container, `--shm-size=1g` documented), disk cache disabled, downloads refused, service workers blocked. Image bytes are taken from intercepted responses in memory, capped per image in the worker and re-checked by the parent, and go through the same size caps and the same decoder child process as every other image (the shared decode→match→report tail in `crawler.ts` is one function for both paths). The no-disk-write test now covers the browser path as far as Node can observe it (below).
- Honest User-Agent: Chromium's own default with `CRAWLER_USER_AGENT` appended, read once from a blank page (no network). No stealth plugins, no fingerprint spoofing. robots.txt is still honoured: every request the page wants to make — documents and subresources — is intercepted in the worker, counted against a per-page request cap, kept inside the source's scope for navigation requests, and waits for the parent's verdict (the parent owns the robots cache; a URL the network policy itself would refuse is denied without a robots fetch). The page itself is also robots/delay-checked before the render (the HTTP pre-fetch's beforeHop).
- Time and resource caps per page: `BROWSER_PAGE_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s deadline, then abort, then SIGKILL), `BROWSER_SCROLLS` bounded scroll steps to trigger lazy loading, `BROWSER_MAX_REQUESTS_PER_PAGE` (excess aborted before the network), `BROWSER_MAX_PAGE_BYTES` at the proxy, and the usual image caps. All through `src/config.ts` (Zod, fail closed) and `.env.example`.
- Crawler flow: every page still does the WP3 HTTP pre-fetch (conditional GET, 304 → `not_modified` and no render, 404/410 → `gone`, robots/401/403 → `blocked`, non-HTML → no render), so a rendered page is fetched twice — once by safe-fetch, once by Chromium; the pre-fetch's redirect scope, robots and delay checks all still run. A successful render reports the page `fetched` with links extracted from the rendered DOM (in scope, depth-gated, capped) and no validators (see deviations). Images are the intercepted responses processed as they arrive: scope/robots/off-site-host caps/dedup/budget apply as in the HTTP path, findings carry the page as claimed and the image as the browser fetched it. A BROWSER page whose render fails reports `error` (the app backs off); an AUTO page falls back to its static result.
- `GET /api/crawler/sources` fields `origin`/`hops`/`render` (in the app's contract since WP1) are now required in the crawler's `SourcesSchema` and read; 12 test/support Source literals gained the three fields.
- New error codes (`browser_launch_failed`, `browser_failed`, `browser_aborted`, `browser_timeout`, `browser_protocol`), new `summary.browser` counters (renders, failed, images), new run-summary fields documented.
- Container: the runtime image is now `node:22-bookworm-slim` (was distroless) with Chromium and its system libraries installed by playwright-core's own CLI (`npx playwright-core install --with-deps chromium`), so the browser revision is pinned by the lockfile; non-root `node` user; `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/app/browsers`; `BROWSER_TMP_DIR=/dev/shm`; the unwritable `TMPDIR` for the main process is kept (the browser worker gets the tmpfs instead). CI (`crawler-ci.yml`): the checks job installs the pinned Chromium with its deps; the image job's entrypoints moved from the distroless path to `node`, and a new smoke proves Chromium renders on a read-only filesystem with its profile in tmpfs.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run diagram, two new guarantee rows, the "Browser rendering" section including what the no-disk test cannot observe, config table, run summary, deployment notes, known limits — JS executes only for BROWSER/AUTO now), `crawler/.env.example`, the runbook (section 3 env row, section 4 deploy and hardening), ADR 006 dated note (the WP7 guarantees against N1/N3, and the `--no-sandbox` hardening item joining the decoder's).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/browser-proxy.test.ts` (8: tunnel through the validated address, private literal ATYP 1, literal-as-domain ATYP 3, hostname resolving to a metadata address, blocked port, BIND command, greeting without no-auth, and the per-render byte account destroying over-budget tunnels), `tests/unit/browser-renderer.test.ts` (13, against a fake worker over the real IPC: lazy spawn, image streaming, verdict round-trip, scrubbed environment and argv, wrong-render-id and oversize-image and unknown-field protocol kills with worker replacement, deadline timeout and recovery, aborted render, worker death and recovery, serialised renders, fail-closed verdict hook, launch failures giving up for the run, close), `tests/integration/browser.test.ts` (9, real Chromium end to end: the JS-gallery page with infinite scroll yields the planted image — it only appears after the second scroll; each image requested exactly once (bytes from the intercepted response, never re-fetched); links read from the rendered DOM; a robots-disallowed image inside a rendered page never requested; a script's two private-address requests both refused with the render surviving; AUTO renders the script page (2 document requests) and not the static one (1); exactly the three expected findings; log scan clean).
- Extended: `tests/integration/memory-only.test.ts` gains a second crawl — a browser-rendered source under the same patched-to-throw fs write APIs, findings found and no write attempted.
- Existing tests changed only where behaviour changed on purpose: the 12 `Source` literals named above (the v2 contract fields are required now); `asmita/tests/integration/crawler-client-contract.test.ts` expects `origin`/`hops`/`render` in the client's parsed sources (the crawler's schema used to strip them, so the contract test had never seen them). Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 22 test files / 282 tests all passed, build ok (19 files / 251 tests before this WP, +31). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 131 test files / 1051 tests all passed (unchanged counts; one assertion updated as above).

**Container verification** (run here, not part of check.sh)

- `docker build -f crawler/Dockerfile` succeeds with Chromium; the image runs `--read-only --network none`: the sharp in-memory decode smoke passes; Chromium launches and renders with its throwaway profile in `/dev/shm` (`--shm-size 1g`) on the read-only filesystem; `run` without configuration exits 1 with only the sanitised `config_invalid` log. A full browser-source crawl inside the container against a local fixture is impossible by design — the address policy refuses local and private addresses, which is the guard doing its job — so the in-container proof stops at the Chromium render smoke; the full crawl is covered by the test suite in Node 22.

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated again (protected agent-instruction file; the write needs the owner's approval — same as WP1–WP3). Intended row addition (apply on approval): after the frontier sentence — "crawler v2 WP7: sources an admin sets to `render = BROWSER` (and AUTO pages with scripts and no image candidates) are rendered by headless Chromium in a child process whose every request leaves through an in-process proxy with the same address policy as safe-fetch; nothing reaches disk (the throwaway profile lives in a tmpfs), robots.txt is honoured per request, and image bytes are taken from intercepted responses in memory through the same decoder child". README, runbook and ADR 006 document all of it instead.
- The browser worker does not get the decoder's `--disallow-code-generation-from-strings` flag: Playwright's driver was not audited for code-generation use and a false failure there would be opaque (the worker's stderr is discarded). The flag list it does get is in `browser-renderer.ts` and the README.
- A full crawl inside the built container was not run (see above, by design); the Chromium-launch path in the container is proven by the read-only render smoke and the CI smoke added for it.
- The n22 test container needed Chromium present under the mounted Playwright cache with its system libraries loadable; that is one-time environment setup outside the repository (the same cache the app's e2e uses). CI installs both per run (`npx playwright-core install --with-deps chromium`).
- WP4, WP5, WP6 and WP8 are untouched, as instructed.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The forward proxy is SOCKS5, not an HTTP CONNECT proxy: with SOCKS5 Chromium sends the hostname (remote DNS), so the proxy decides every destination and no absolute-form HTTP request rewriting is needed; the port rule is checked for both schemes since the SOCKS handshake carries no scheme. The spec asked for "a forward proxy that applies the same address policy" — SOCKS5 is that, with the same resolve/check/dial guarantee.
- "A script that requests a private address is refused by the proxy": with the robots verdict running before every request, such a request is refused one layer earlier — the verdict consults the same policy (a literal blocked address is denied without even a robots fetch), so the proxy never sees it. The page observes both refusals (the integration test's marker link appears only when both fetches fail), the proxy's own refusal is proven directly by the unit test driving raw sockets at it, and no-direct-egress is proven by the fixture hostnames existing only in the test policy's DNS (a successful render means the proxy resolved them). The outcome the spec wants — a private address requested by a rendered page is never fetched — is what is tested.
- Every request, not only documents, waits for the robots verdict. Parity with the HTTP path, where every image fetch is robots-checked before the network; a hostile page referencing many unknown hosts causes at most one robots.txt fetch per origin, bounded by the per-page request cap.
- Rendered pages report no ETag/Last-Modified, so a browser page is never 304-suppressed: the browser's GET cannot be conditional, and validators from the pre-fetch would let a later 304 on unchanged HTML skip the render — but a page whose HTML is unchanged may still render differently. Browser pages revisit at the 24 h base interval. (The pre-fetch itself still stores validators for AUTO pages that fall back to the static path.)
- BROWSER sources fetch every page twice (safe-fetch pre-fetch + the browser's own GET). Kept because the pre-fetch gives WP3 its conditional-GET, gone/blocked and non-HTML handling for free, and it is one extra document request per rendered page; the alternative — sending validators into a browser navigation — would make a 304 a navigation error Chromium cannot classify.
- In browser mode, image candidates are the intercepted responses only; static candidates from the pre-fetched HTML are not re-fetched separately. The spec says image bytes are taken from intercepted responses, and re-fetching DOM candidates the browser chose not to load would double requests for images already downloaded.
- Rule 4's "no proxies" is read as no third-party or anonymising proxies; the spec's own WP7 mandates this in-process policy proxy, which adds no anonymity (same source IP, one honest User-Agent, no rotation).
- The runtime image left distroless. Chromium needs its shared-library set, and copying dlopen-loaded libraries into distroless without a package manager is not maintainable; `node:22-bookworm-slim` with playwright-core's pinned install keeps the read-only, non-root posture. `--no-sandbox` is passed to Chromium (no setuid helper on a read-only root filesystem) and documented with the decoder's deployment-hardening items; the browser worker runs as the job's user.
- `BROWSER_TMP_DIR` defaults to `/dev/shm` and browser sources fail closed where it does not exist (macOS dev machines); the container flags are documented in the README and runbook.

- `asmita/src/lib/crawl-stats.ts` — `getCrawlerStats()`, three aligned paths (memory, Kysely Worker, Prisma) like every crawl module. No schema change, no migration: everything reads tables WP1/WP3 already created. The aggregation runs in the database (`groupBy`, `count`, `max` — a fixed 10 statements, 11 when a findings run exists), so no page, finding or source row is ever loaded into the app and no address column is selected (asserted on the Kysely recording fake and the mocked Prisma client).
- The aggregates: sites by origin (admin/reported/discovered) and state (on/off) with totals; pages crawled today (the UTC day, exactly the window a source's daily `pageBudget` counts, so the dashboard and the budget cannot disagree); the frontier queue — live total split into pending (never visited), due now, claimed (30-minute window), scheduled, dead; discovered sites today (UTC day); matches awaiting review (CANDIDATE) plus approved/dismissed; page outcomes by the code the crawler's report left (error, blocked, gone, dead, fetched, not_modified, pending); the last run the app saw — the newest run that reported a match (run id, first/last seen, finding count) plus the last activity of any kind (a page fetch, a source visit, a finding).
- Admin "Crawler overview" page `/admin/crawler` (`findings:review`; `asmita/src/app/(admin)/crawler/page.tsx` + `CrawlerOverview.tsx`): the above rendered read-only — five stat cards, an origin × state table, the queue split, an errors-by-code table and a last-run panel. No addresses (the aggregates carry none), no links, no buttons, no forms. The page calls the lib directly like every admin page; the API route exposes the same data for dashboards and checks. Nav item "Crawler overview" in the Work group, gated by `findings:review` (SUPER_ADMIN and CASE_REVIEWER, like Findings); the nav-permission test's page map covers it.
- Docs: the runbook's analyst section gains a "Crawler overview (crawler v2 WP8)" paragraph (route, permission, what each number means, and the two honest limits), and the go-live migration item now lists "crawler stats" among the paths to exercise on a real Postgres. `crawler/README.md` untouched: the service, its contract and its behaviour did not change, and no `crawler/` source was touched.

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was again **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file was blocked by the approval gate (as for WP1-WP3; silence is not consent). Intended addition (apply on approval): in the architecture list, extend the crawler bullet's admin-route list with "and the address-free `GET /api/admin/crawler/stats` behind `findings:review`, which feeds the read-only `/admin/crawler` overview page (crawler v2 WP8; counts, timestamps and one run id only — never a host, URL, fingerprint or case reference)". The runbook documents the page and route instead; the WP1-WP3 additions remain pending for the same reason.
