- "Last run" is the newest run that reported a match plus the last activity of any kind — the app cannot name a run that found nothing (see deviations). When WP6's continuous loop lands, a long-lived run will appear as one run id with a growing finding window; if that reads poorly, the fix is a run-summary report contract, which no WP specifies.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "Page renders from a new address-free `GET /api/admin/crawler/stats`": the page renders from the same new aggregate the route serves (`getCrawlerStats()`), not by fetching its own API. The repo's no-fetch lint rule and the admin-crawler-ui static test forbid a page calling fetch, and every existing admin page calls its lib directly; the route exists as the address-free API. This is the reading that satisfies both the spec and the unchanged rules.
- "Last run summary" and "errors by code" are reconstructions from what the app already stores: no WP (including this one, which is reads only) adds a contract for the crawler to report its run summary or its finer error codes to the app. The app can name a run only through `CrawlFinding.runId`, and the only per-page error codes it holds are the frontier report statuses. Both limits are printed on the page itself and in the runbook, so the console never overstates what it knows.
- "Queue size" is rendered as the live total plus a split into pending, due, claimed, scheduled and dead: one number hides what an operator needs (what a run would take now, what is out with a run, what is waiting). The live total is the headline.
- Sites' "state" is enabled/disabled, the only state a source has at WP3; WP5's paused state will extend the same table.
- Discovered-sites-today counts DISCOVERED sources by `createdAt`, which no code path creates until WP5: the aggregate is built and tested with directly seeded rows and reads 0 in production until then. Building it now is cheaper and safer than growing the route in WP5.
- The route is not rate-limited: it is a cheap, bounded, authenticated read beside the findings list route, which has none either; the spec's "rate-limited like the existing routes" sentence governs the `/api/crawler/*` service routes, and this is not one.

**Attempt-2 verification (2026-11-17).** Attempt 1's session detached mid-run on the protected-file approval prompt and kept working in parallel with attempt 2, the lane's second pass over the same incomplete work (check 1 had failed only on the asmita type-check: `vi.unstubAllEnv()` → `vi.unstubAllEnvs()` in `crawl-stats.test.ts`, a one-word fix both sessions applied idempotently). It could not be stopped — its agent loop lives in the shared gateway that also serves the other lanes — so the decision was to let it converge, audit everything it committed file by file against the spec, and re-verify independently rather than duplicate or revert its work. The full checks were then re-run from scratch by attempt 2: **ALL CHECKS PASSED** (`check.sh`, Node 22) — crawler lint, type-check and build with 19 test files / 251 tests; asmita lint (0 errors), type-check, `prisma validate` and 134 test files / 1073 tests. The entry above is attempt 1's, kept as written; the working tree it left was clean and unchanged by the re-run.

## Review fixes — 2026-11-17

A review of the app side of crawler v2 found eight defects. Each is fixed in its own commit on `v2-app-fix`, with a test that fails without the fix.

- **Audit writes on the Prisma path (high).** `persistAuditLogs` passed `data: null` to `auditLog.createMany`; Prisma refuses a plain null for a `Json?` column, so every Prisma-path audit event without data threw and was swallowed by `console.error`. The row now omits `data` when there is none (SQL NULL); the hash computation is unchanged. Test: `tests/unit/audit-prisma-persist.test.ts`.
- **Reported sources no longer created in the survivor's request (privacy).** WP1 created the `CRAWL_SOURCE_REPORTED` entry right after her `CASE_CREATED` / `URL_SUBMITTED` entries, linking her to the host through adjacent sequence numbers and timestamps, past URL purge and account erasure — the spec says nothing may link the source to her. `addUrlsToCase` and `registerCaseWithUrls` no longer touch `CrawlSource`. The hourly retention cron (`purge-expired-urls`) runs `createReportedSourcesFromSubmittedUrls`: up to 1000 links with `SubmittedUrl.crawlSourceChecked = false` (new column, migration `20261117030000_submitted_url_crawl_source_checked`: existing rows read true, new rows default false), hosts deduplicated and sorted, never-crawl, blocked and existing hosts skipped as before, sources created with `createdAt` = sweep time and audited in one batch with no actor and no case, links then marked checked. Approved crawler findings still create their host's source at approval (an analyst action, not hers). The WP1 tests on all three paths now run the sweep after storing links and assert nothing is created inline; deviation: expect up to an hour between her report and the source.
- **An admin's "off" survives the pause sweep.** Only `enabled: true` cleared `pausedUntil`/`pauseReason`, so an admin disabling a paused discovered site had it switched back on by the sweep after 30 days. `updateCrawlSource` now clears the pause fields on any change to `enabled` (switch-on still also clears `disabledReason`), and the sweep resumes only rows that are still disabled and still paused, on all three paths. Tests: new cases in `crawl-discoveries.test.ts`; the Prisma and Kysely sweep tests assert the narrower resume filter.
- **Daily budget holds across crawler processes.** The remaining budget was computed outside the claim transaction and ignored rows claimed but not yet reported, so two processes could each claim the full budget. `claimDue` now locks the source row (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, which serialises claims of one source only), re-reads budget and switch, and subtracts both pages fetched today and rows with `claimedUntil > now` not fetched today, all inside the transaction (Prisma, Kysely; memory is single-threaded). Tests: new cases in `crawl-frontier.test.ts` (two "processes" share one budget; a killed claim frees its slot after 30 minutes), `crawl-frontier-prisma.test.ts` and `crawl-frontier-kysely.test.ts` (lock before counts, outstanding rows subtracted). The Prisma mock gained `$executeRaw`.
- **"Budget spent three days running" counts real fetches.** The pause read `CrawlPage.lastFetchedAt`, which every revisit overwrites, so earlier days looked idle and the pause almost never fired. New table `CrawlSourceDay (sourceId, day, pagesFetched)` (migration `20261117040000_crawl_source_day`, additive); the frontier report adds the visited pages for the UTC day (Kysely upsert `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, Prisma `upsert`, memory map), and the sweep reads the three days in one query and prunes counters older than seven days. The daily claim budget still counts `lastFetchedAt` within today, which revisits cannot distort and which keeps the dashboard's "pages crawled today" equal to it. Tests: a memory case that revisits the same two pages three days running and is paused (fails on the old code), counter pruning, and the Prisma/Kysely sweep and report tests now assert the counter reads and writes instead of three `CrawlPage` counts.
- **Blocking a host stops crawling it.** `addBlockedCrawlHost` did not touch existing sources, so claims kept handing out the host's URLs and the sweep could resume it. Blocking now switches off every source on the host or a subdomain (any origin; `disabledReason: "blocked_host"`, pause cleared, ids recorded in the audit entry as `disabledSourceIds`); `claimCrawlPages` refuses any source whose host is on the never-crawl list (built-in or `CrawlBlockedHost`, parent domains included) before seeding; the sweep filters blocked hosts out of resumes. Deviation: the WP2 note that the list "stops the automatic paths only" no longer holds — an admin can still add a blocked host, but it is not crawled while blocked. Tests: new cases in `crawl-blocked-hosts.test.ts`, `crawl-import-prisma.test.ts`, `crawl-import-kysely.test.ts`; the Kysely sweep sequence gains the blocked-host read, the Prisma frontier mock gained `crawlBlockedHost.findFirst`.
- **Frontier retention and size are bounded.** Rows never visited are due at epoch 0, so the "still due" keep-clause kept them forever, and rows of disabled or paused sources stayed too. `purgeExpiredCrawlPages` now also deletes, after the normal 30 days, the rows of any switched-off source (admin, block or pause), due or not (Prisma relation filter, Kysely subquery, memory lookup); rows of enabled sources keep the WP3 rule. New links are capped per source at `CRAWLER_FRONTIER_MAX_PAGES_PER_SOURCE` (default 50,000 = 25 days of the largest default budget, near the 30-day retention; clamped 1000..1,000,000; documented in `.env.example` and the runbook; `crawler/README.md` left untouched as this lane does not edit `crawler/`): the report locks the source row and counts its rows before inserting, and drops links beyond the cap. Because links are trimmed before the digest dedup, the cap can bite a few rows early, never late. Tests: memory (a disabled source's due rows are purged; reports of 499 links per page stop at a cap of 1000 rows), Prisma and Kysely cap tests; the Prisma purge test's `OR` list gains the disabled-source clause. Not done: the Kysely purge subquery is not observable in the recording fake, so it is covered by type-check and the shared Prisma/memory semantics only.
- **Bulk inserts only claim their own rows.** On the Prisma path, `insertImportedSources` (WP2) and `insertDiscoveredSources` (WP5) re-read by domain after `createMany({ skipDuplicates })`, so a row another request created for the same host was reported as created and audited as this admin's import or this run's discovery. Both now generate the ids, insert them, and re-read by those ids only (the new reported-source sweep does the same). Kysely already used `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING`; memory is single-threaded. Tests: a concurrent-row case each in `crawl-import-prisma.test.ts` and `crawl-discoveries-prisma.test.ts`; their existing re-read assertions now expect the id filter.

Verification after all eight fixes: `npm run lint` (0 errors, 3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), `npm run type-check`, `npm run prisma:validate` and `npm run test` all pass: 137 test files passed and 1 skipped, 1131 tests passed and 3 skipped (the skipped file is the pre-existing, environment-gated `tests/integration/crawler-client-contract.test.ts`, untouched). Not verified: the two new migrations and the new queries have run only against mocks, the recording Kysely fake and the memory store, not a real Postgres (the runbook's go-live item now lists both migrations).

## Watch

### WP34. One watch-mode branch, with its e2e failures fixed — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22), and the full Playwright suite was run here too (see below).

**What was built**

- The branch: `feat/watch-mode` (cut from `origin/feat/watch-w1`, WP22) now carries all three lanes. Merge 1: `origin/feat/watch-w2` (WP23, WP24) — five conflicts resolved keeping both sides' intent: `CrawlConsent` carries the WP22 watch variant (`watch` prop, `onNowOn`) and WP24's re-upload view (`caseOpen`), with w2's local `watch` variable renamed `reupload` so the two meanings never collide; the approval path notifies a watch (WP22 `notifyWatchCopyFound`) and a re-upload watch (WP24 `notifyReuploadIfWatching`), which are disjoint (an open watch vs a closed case inside its re-upload window); `CrawlConsentState` carries both `scope` (WP22) and `watchUntil` (WP24); the watch view passes `caseOpen` so a closed watch shows the re-upload wording; the WP22 watch view keeps its own CrawlConsent instance. Merge 2: `origin/master` (video fingerprints, PR #73, and the Quick exit fix) — additive conflicts only; QuickExit (`data-armed="true"`) and quick-exit.spec.ts come from master as they are, so `quick-exit.spec.ts:45` needed no work and was not weakened. `crawler/README.md`'s pool line carries master's VPDQ note verbatim. All five migrations in the branch are additive and in timestamp order: `20261119000000_link_verification`, `20261119000000_video_fingerprints`, `20261119000000_watch_mode`, `20261119120000_crawl_consent_watch_until`, `20261119130000_audit_content_still_online` (below). Nothing was regenerated by hand.
- A gap the merge surfaced: the WP23 `CONTENT_STILL_ONLINE` audit event was in the Prisma schema and written by `link-verification.ts`, but **no migration had added it to the Postgres `AuditEventType` enum** (the WP23 migration created `LinkVerificationOutcome`, not this value), so the first still-online audit write would have been rejected by the database. New additive migration `20261119130000_audit_content_still_online` adds the one enum value.
- **The reported e2e failure, and the cause in the product.** `watch-journey.spec.ts:60` and `:90` both failed with `locator.check: Clicking the checkbox did not change its state`. Playwright's `check()` clicks once and then reads the DOM state immediately (a `NonRecoverableDOMError`, no retry — verified in playwright-core's `_setChecked`), and the tick was a controlled checkbox bound only to the server's consent state (`checked = on`): React re-rendered it unchecked and disabled the instant she clicked, while the switch-on POST was still in flight. The VM failure snapshots show exactly that moment — box disabled, "Switching on...", request pending, no error shown. A fast human reader sees the same flicker, so the product was wrong, not the test. Fix: the tick now follows her click at once — a `pendingOn` state decided by the new pure `tickChecked()` in `crawl-consent-logic.ts`; the box stays ticked (and locked) while the request runs, the server state takes over on success, and a refusal unticks it with the reason shown (changed text) or hides the control (unavailable, as designed). The stop path already un-checked synchronously. **Both spec tests are otherwise untouched.**
- **Four more product defects the same e2e exposed** once the tick worked and the tests could reach further than they ever had (each fixed in its own commit, none of them test changes):
  1. The watch page **never rendered**: the watch view passed `badge={(status) => …}` from the server page to `WatchFoundList`, a `"use client"` component — Next.js refuses functions across the server→client boundary ("Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components"), crashing every watch page render. `StatusBadge` moved to its own shared component (identical markup and keys); the client list renders it directly and takes only serialisable props.
  2. `AddMoreImages`' hidden file input carried the **same accessible name as its visible button** ("Add more images"; `<input type=file>` is a button to assistive tech), so the page offered two identically-named controls. The chooser now uses the existing "Choose images to fingerprint" string (en + hi); no new i18n keys.
  3. After "Yes, stop watching" her fingerprints are deleted, so the consent section correctly hides itself — but the watch header then showed only the generic "Your watch is off" line, **hiding from her that her fingerprints were deleted**. The watch view now shows the withdrawal record (`watch.state.withdrawn`) whenever the consent is withdrawn.
  4. After a successful tick on /watch, `switchOn()` called `onNowOn()` (router.push to her watch page) **and then `router.refresh()`** — the refresh's RSC fetch raced the pending navigation and could leave her stranded on /watch with "Watching is on". The refresh now runs only when no navigation follows (the case flow, unchanged).
- One test-side fix: the spec's last locator ("Nothing is sent to any platform unless you choose.") matched two paragraphs that both make that true promise (the tick's on-panel copy and the Add-more-images hint — both deliberate WP22 copy, never rendered together until now). The assertion is scoped to the tick's own section; it is not weakened.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

## Watch and verify

### WP23. Check that removed content stayed down (app) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- New `LinkVerification` table (additive migration `asmita/prisma/migrations/20261119000000_link_verification/`, with the same RLS rule as every other table and a CHECK that keeps `attempt` within 1..3): one row per scheduled re-check — `submittedUrlId`, `dueAt`, `attempt`, `outcome` (PENDING/GONE/STILL_ONLINE/UNKNOWN), `checkedAt`, plus `enqueuedAt` (deviation 1 below). **No URL is stored in it**; a check works from the SubmittedUrl it references (asserted on the schema, the migration and the rows).
- Scheduling: when one of her links becomes REMOVED — `markUrlResolved` (she marks it) or the admin platform-reply route (a reply of REMOVED) — and only on a real transition (re-marking an already-removed link schedules nothing), three checks are scheduled at +1/+3/+7 days. Best-effort and unlogged beyond a fixed error code; it can never fail her action.
- The hourly sweep `sweepDueLinkVerifications` (new `asmita/src/lib/link-verification.ts`, memory/Kysely/Prisma aligned) rides the existing `/api/cron/purge-expired-urls` — no new trigger. Its response gained `verificationsEnqueued`/`verificationsResolved`/`verificationsStillOnline`. Step 1 queues each due check into the crawler frontier as a priority row (due now, ahead of every scheduled revisit) through one new exported function in `crawl-pages.ts` (`ensurePriorityCrawlPage` — the only change to that file, ~55 lines, called from the new module), creating the host's REPORTED source through the existing rules when none exists; a never-crawl host, a link whose address the retention already removed, or an address the crawler would refuse (credentials, non-default port) resolves UNKNOWN with no source and no row. Step 2 resolves the enqueued checks from the crawler's own reports: gone/blocked page → GONE; fetched + no finding + her fingerprints live in the pool (exactly `crawl-pool.ts`'s inPool rule, via a new `caseHasPoolFingerprints` there) → GONE; fetched without pool fingerprints, or a not-modified answer (images not re-hashed) → UNKNOWN; DEAD → UNKNOWN; a fetched page waits two hours after the report before deciding; a finding for that page address on her case since the check was queued wins as STILL_ONLINE at any moment. On STILL_ONLINE the link returns REMOVED → NOTICE_SENT (re-arming the existing escalation sweep), the episode's remaining checks are dropped, a `CONTENT_STILL_ONLINE` audit entry is written (new enum value), and she is emailed **her case reference and a sign-in prompt only** (`sendLinkStillOnlineNotification` in `email.ts`; new legal text created unreviewed — **counsel must read it before real use**, and the whole re-check behaviour is on the spec's "For legal sign-off" list; both are stated in the runbook).
- UI: her case page shows "Verified removed (checked N times)" after three GONE results and "Still online: we have followed up" after a still-online check (i18n `case.verification.*` in `en.json` + `hi.json`, listed as machine translated in `hi-review-status.json`; no accent stripes). The admin case page's link cards show the removal-check history (`listLinkVerifications`, one bounded read per case, no address columns).
- Purge and erasure: the 30-day URL blanking drops the still-pending checks of a purged link (completed rows stay as history); account erasure deletes a case's checks with its links on all three paths (memory: `eraseCrawlMemoryForCases`; Prisma/Kysely: inside the erasure transactions, counted as `linkVerifications`).
- Docs: the runbook gained section 10 "Removal re-checks (watch-and-verify WP23)" plus a go-live checklist item. `crawler/` was not touched (rule 8: the service contract is unchanged — a check is an ordinary frontier row); root `CLAUDE.md` was not written (protected agent-instruction file, as for WP1–WP8).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `asmita/tests/unit/link-verification.test.ts` (25, memory path end to end through the real store, case-ops and frontier report paths: scheduling on removal via `markUrlResolved`, idempotency, enqueue and the priority row, the fresh-fetch reset per attempt, never-crawl/blank-address/non-crawler-shaped → UNKNOWN, every outcome rule incl. the grace and the 7-day give-up, STILL_ONLINE flip + audit + reference-only email + a fresh episode on re-removal, three GONE → the verified-removed summary, erasure, no address in the table), `tests/unit/link-verification-kysely.test.ts` (11, recording fake: bounded statement shapes, the conditional updates, the NOTICE_SENT flip, the pending drop, the audit and email args, no address ever selected or sent), `tests/unit/link-verification-prisma.test.ts` (17, mocked client: the createMany, the upsert's reset values, every resolve branch, the lost-race flip, the full STILL_ONLINE transition), `tests/unit/link-still-online-email.test.ts` (4). 57 new tests.
- Existing tests changed only where behaviour changed on purpose: `url-retention-cron.test.ts` (the cron route gained the sweep and three response fields), `url-retention.test.ts` and `crawler-work-and-retention.test.ts` (the retention purge now also drops pending checks), `admin-cases.test.ts` (the detail's verification read, asserting its address-free column list), `admin-notice-response-route.test.ts` (the removal reply schedules re-checks; an acknowledgement or a lost race does not), `account-hard-delete.test.ts` (the new table in the erasure fake's FK list, seed and counts), `crawl-consent-ui.test.ts` (the FoundByAsmita JSX was reformatted for the note prop), `crawl-reported-sources-prisma.test.ts` (erasure mock gained the new delegate). Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests all passed, build ok (unchanged by this WP). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 147 test files / 1230 tests all passed. (A direct `npx vitest run` outside `check.sh` skips the environment-gated `crawler-client-contract.test.ts`: 146 files / 1227 tests passed, 1 file / 3 tests skipped, as before this WP.)
- The Kysely and Prisma test files, the email test and the runbook section were produced by parallel subagents of this session and re-read and re-verified here (statement shapes, outcome rules and privacy assertions checked against the module).

**Not done or not verified**

- The migration and the new queries have run only against the memory store, the recording Kysely fake and the mocked Prisma client, not a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook go-live item, which now also names the removal re-check sweep).
- The admin case page's verification history and her case page's notes are covered by the summary values, the address-free read assertions and the existing static page tests, not by a human or an E2E run; the E2E crawler journey was not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged).
- The Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) was not run (pre-existing open item, unchanged).
- ADR 006 was not extended: WP23 changes no crawler behaviour (rule 8) and adds no new data flow to the service; the legal sign-off items are tracked in the watch-and-verify spec's own list and the runbook.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- One column beyond the spec's list: `enqueuedAt`. The spec's own resolution rule ("a crawler finding for that page since the check was queued") needs the queue moment, and the enqueue must happen once per attempt — without it, a DEAD/GONE frontier row would be re-resurrected every hour. Still no URL in the table.
- "A crawler finding for that page" is read as a finding matched under **her** fingerprints (her case's finding for the same page address, compared by the same `parseSubmittedUrl` urlHash as the finding-approval path). The check asks whether *her* image came back; another survivor's image on the same page is not her link being still online, and the per-case read stays bounded.
- A due check resets an existing frontier row to a fresh pre-visit state (PENDING, failCount 0, validators cleared, lastFetchedAt cleared) and thereby re-enables a DEAD or GONE row for that one purposeful visit. Without the reset, the most common case — the platform's own 404 having marked the page GONE — would resolve all three attempts from one stale report and the feature would verify nothing. Reports still never resurrect a row; only this explicit app-side enqueue does, and it never touches another row.
- A finding that arrived since the queue decides STILL_ONLINE immediately, without waiting out the two-hour grace: the grace exists to wait *for* findings, not to delay acting on one that has arrived.
- "Triggers the existing automated follow-up/escalation" is implemented as the status flip alone: the link returns to the notice-sent state, and `escalation-engine.ts` (untouched) does what it already does on its own hourly schedule. For a link whose platform reply said REMOVED, the engine excludes the notice by its `removedAt` record of the platform's answer — rewriting that record was not an option. For crawler links the existing representative-notice exclusions keep applying.
- The sweep does nothing while `ENABLE_CRAWLER` is off (checks wait as PENDING); the spec is silent about the switch.
- A check whose page was never answered (source off, budget spent, page erroring) resolves UNKNOWN seven days after it was queued — the spec gives no bound and PENDING forever helps nobody. Bounded at 200 enqueued and 200 resolved per hourly pass.
- Scheduling covers both link sources ("one of her links"): her own and crawler links she marked removed when findings are visible. The never-crawl and address rules make the crawler-link case safe; representative-notice behaviour is untouched.
- The still-online email is English-only with the locale plumbed, like the L2/L3 escalation emails it sits beside (the i18n rule covers page strings; `case.verification.*` is in both `en.json` and `hi.json`). The email wording is new legal text created unreviewed — counsel must read it before real use, as the runbook and the spec's sign-off list state.
- The memory store's link records gained the encrypted address (optional, dev/test only) and NOTICE_SENT in their status union: the memory path could not queue a check without the address it had never kept.

### WP24. Watch for re-uploads after the case closes (app) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- New nullable column `CrawlConsent.watchUntil` (additive migration `asmita/prisma/migrations/20261119120000_crawl_consent_watch_until/`) and a new audit event `CRAWL_WATCH_STARTED` (enum value in the same migration). Nothing else in the schema changes; existing rows read as "no watch started", and erasure needs no new rule — the column lives on the consent, which erasure already deletes.
- New `asmita/src/lib/crawl-watch.ts`: `sweepReuploadWatches` rides the existing hourly `/api/cron/purge-expired-urls` (no new trigger; its response gained `reuploadWatchesStarted`) and starts the 12-month window on every live authorisation whose case is RESOLVED or whose every non-rejected link is verified removed (three GONE re-checks, the same rule as her case page — counts only, never an address). The start is one conditional update (`watchUntil IS NULL`), so the first trigger wins, a later resolution moves nothing, and two sweeps can never double-start or double-audit; each pass is bounded at 200. A `CRAWL_WATCH_STARTED` entry records the consent id and the window's end only.
- The pool rule (spec: fingerprints stay while the consent is live and either the case is open or `now < watchUntil`): `crawl-pool.ts` gained a private `poolCoverage` — the existing live-consent read (which now also selects `watchUntil`) plus one case-status read by id — used by `buildCrawlPool`, `resolvePoolFingerprint` and WP23's `caseHasPoolFingerprints`; a resolved case outside its window is dropped exactly as a withdrawn authorisation is, its pool id resolves to nothing, and a crawler report under it is refused (`fingerprint_not_accepted`). "Open" is OPEN, PARTIALLY_RESOLVED and ESCALATED (the admin list's active statuses). `fingerprintStillConsented` / `findingStillConsented` keep their live-consent-only meaning, so notice coverage is unchanged.
- A re-upload found and approved by an analyst: the finding becomes a CRAWLER link as before (it shows under "Found by Asmita" and follows the existing representative-notice path — her full consent covers it), and at final approval, while the case is no longer open and the window is still running, she is emailed **her case reference and a sign-in prompt only** (`sendReuploadFoundNotification` in `email.ts`, idempotency key `reupload-found:<findingId>`, one email per approved finding). New legal text created unreviewed — **counsel must read it before real use**; the 12-month watch itself is on the spec's "For legal sign-off" list too. Best-effort by contract: a failure logs `reupload_email_failed` and never fails the approval.
- Her dashboard: `CrawlConsent` gains a `caseOpen` prop (from the case status) and, while the authorisation is live, the case is closed and a window is set, renders "Watching for re-uploads until {date}" with a "Stop watching" button and watch-worded stop confirmation — stopping withdraws the authorisation through the existing DELETE route with all its existing effects. `getCrawlConsentState`/the consent GET gained `watchUntil`. Strings in `en.json` + `hi.json` (`crawl.watch.*`), listed as machine translated in `hi-review-status.json`; no accent stripes (the existing pill and button styles only).
- Nothing else: no source is created or changed for her (the sources from her reported links stay as they are), `crawl-pages.ts` was not touched at all this time, `crawler/` was not touched (rule 8 — the service contract is unchanged), and the admin Findings/Cases pages are unchanged (WP24's spec asks nothing new there).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `asmita/tests/unit/crawl-watch.test.ts` (16, memory path end to end through the real store, pool, findings and review code: the sweep's triggers and idempotence, pool inclusion for open / resolved-in-window / resolved-expired / resolved-without-window / withdrawn, the STILL_ONLINE-and-UNKNOWN checks not counting as GONE, REJECTED links not blocking, `caseHasPoolFingerprints` following the window, the consent state's `watchUntil`, stop-watching withdrawal emptying the pool, the approval email with its exact reference-only args, no email on an open case / a first review / a dismissal / a window that ended between find and approval), `tests/unit/crawl-watch-kysely.test.ts` (14, recording fake: bounded statement shapes, the conditional update and the lost race, the audit args, the pool's consent+status reads, the email and locale, a no-address sweep over every recorded statement), `tests/unit/crawl-watch-prisma.test.ts` (15, mocked client: every branch incl. the `status: { not: "REJECTED" }` link read and the exact pool query shapes), `tests/unit/reupload-found-email.test.ts` (5, email copy: reference + dashboard link only, no site or platform, English while Hindi awaits native review). 50 new tests.
- Extended: `crawl-consent-ui.test.ts` (+3: the watch view with its date and Stop-watching button, the open case keeping the ordinary copy, the `reuploadWatchView` truth table), `url-retention-cron.test.ts` (the sweep in the cron route and the new response field), and — because the pool now reads the case status and the consent state gained a field — the mocked clients and fixtures of `crawler-prisma-path.test.ts` and `link-verification-prisma.test.ts` (new `case.findMany`/`crawlConsent.findFirst` delegates, an OPEN fixture case, the consent select's new `watchUntil`), plus `watchUntil: null` in the `ConsentState`/`CrawlConsentState` fixtures of `crawl-consent-ui.test.ts` and `crawler-routes.test.ts`. Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests all passed, build ok (unchanged by this WP). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 151 test files / 1283 tests all passed (147 / 1230 before this WP, +4 files, +53 tests).
- The Kysely and Prisma test files were produced by parallel subagents of this session and re-read and re-verified here (statement shapes, outcome rules and privacy assertions checked against the module), then re-run green inside the full check.

**Not done or not verified**

- The migration and the new queries have run only against the memory store, the recording Kysely fake and the mocked Prisma client, not a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook go-live item, which now also names the re-upload watch).
- The watch view on her case page is covered by static render tests and the state's `watchUntil` assertions, not by a human or an E2E run; the E2E crawler journey was not extended (pre-existing open items, unchanged).
- The Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) was not run (pre-existing open item, unchanged).
- Root `CLAUDE.md` was not written (protected agent-instruction file, as for WP1–WP23); the runbook (new section 11 plus a go-live checklist item) documents the feature instead. `crawler/README.md` is unchanged: the service, its contract and its behaviour did not change.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "When a case is resolved": no code path in the app sets a case to RESOLVED today (the status exists in the enum and the admin filter, but nothing transitions it), so there was no action to hook. The window is started by the hourly sweep instead, within an hour of resolution, whatever resolved it — an admin action in a future route, or a direct status change. This also backfills cases resolved before this WP shipped, whose fingerprints would otherwise have left the pool the moment the pool rule learned about `watchUntil`.
- First start wins: the spec gives no rule for a case resolved again later, and a sliding window would let a resolved-again-then-resolved case watch indefinitely; the first resolution fixes the date.
- "Every link on it is verified removed" is read as every link a takedown was ever about (REJECTED links excluded) being REMOVED with three GONE re-checks — WP23's own "verified removed" rule; a case with no link never matches this trigger, because nothing was verified.
- The sweep runs whatever `ENABLE_CRAWLER` says, unlike WP23's re-check sweep: starting the window is a case-record transition, not a crawl action, and the pool applies it whenever the crawler is switched back on.
- The email fires at final approval while the window runs, once per finding; an approval after the window ended still stands (the authorisation is live, so the representative-notice path still covers it) but sends nothing — the spec ties the email to the re-upload watch, not to the approval.
- The re-upload email is English-only with the locale plumbed, like the WP23 still-online email it sits beside (the i18n rule covers page strings; `crawl.watch.*` is in both `en.json` and `hi.json`).
- Memory-store consent rows gained `watchUntil` as an optional field so no unrelated test fixture had to change; every path normalises it to null.

## Video

### WP20. Video frames in the crawler pool and matcher (app + crawler) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- App, `/api/crawler/pool` (`src/lib/crawl-pool.ts`): an approved, consented VPDQ submission is served as its own entry `{ id, kind: "video", frames }` — the pool id, and the kept frame hashes decrypted from the stored frame list (the JSON of `{ hash, quality, t }` the WP17 route validated), reduced to plain 64-hex hashes (qualities and timestamps are never served). Image entries keep their pre-WP20 shape exactly. All three backends select `videoFramesEncrypted` beside `algorithm` (memory, Kysely, Prisma), and the Kysely/Prisma tests assert the column is asked for, so a stale select cannot skip or empty a video entry. If the stored frame list is gone or unreadable the entry falls back to the stored best frame's hash — the hash annex's own rule (WP19), so an entry never silently vanishes; erasure clears the pool id in the same statement as the list, so the fallback is defence, not a live path — and a row with no valid hash anywhere is skipped. The pool `version` covers video entries (an image-only pool hashes exactly as before, pinned by test). `resolvePoolFingerprint` resolves a video's pool id like an image's, so a video finding reaches the unchanged analyst review path.
- App, the consent gate: the crawl authorisation is now offered for a case whose only fingerprint is a video. `hasSignableImageFingerprint` became `hasSearchableFingerprint` (PDQ or VPDQ; `crawl-consent.ts` + `case-hash-annex.ts`), flipping the gate whose own comment named WP20 as the switch — until it flipped, a video-only case could never authorise the search, so the pool could never have served a video entry at all. The memory crawl store's erasure twin clears `videoFramesEncrypted` with the pool id, like the database statement.
- Crawler, the frame index (`src/match/video-index.ts`, new): multi-index hashing. Each 256-bit frame hash is split into 16 chunks of 16 bits, each chunk indexed by a counting-sorted table (~128 bytes per frame all told); a query within Hamming distance t of a frame must sit within floor(t/16) bits (1 at the capped threshold 31) on at least one chunk — otherwise the 16 chunks would already carry more than t errors — so probing every chunk table with its 17 neighbourhood keys and verifying the candidates with the Matcher's full SWAR popcount finds every frame within t while touching a fraction of the pool. A BK-tree was rejected: at radius 31 of 256 bits its search visits most of the tree, degenerating towards the linear scan the spec says to stay fast without. Lower thresholds keep the pigeonhole guarantee (sub-radius 0: fewer than 16 differing bits must leave an exact chunk). Benchmarked (`npm run bench:video`, Node 22 container): **0.23 ms per query at 100,000 frames** (build 159 ms) and 0.43 ms at 1,000,000 — beside the brute-force image matcher's 0.9 ms at 100k image fingerprints.
- Crawler, one matcher for the whole pool (`src/match/pool-matcher.ts`, new): `PoolMatcher` holds the unchanged brute-force `Matcher` for image entries and the frame index for video entries behind the same interface — `has`, `idSetView`, `withdraw` and the one pool-age gate cover both kinds with no second path, `match()` returns every pool hit best-first tagged `image`/`video`, and the tag never leaves the process. The finding contract is untouched: the fingerprint id is the video submission's pool id, `distance` the best frame distance, and every hit queues for human review exactly like an image hit.
- Match rule (Phase 2): one preview image within the pool threshold of any frame of a survivor video is a match for that video — the video spec's thumbnail clause. The 3-frames clause needs a crawl that samples an online video, which Phase 2 never does; no video bytes are fetched anywhere (the integration test plants a `<video src>` on the page and asserts it is never requested).
- Counting (`metrics.ts`, `log.ts`): the run summary and the `run_progress` line gain `videoMatches` beside `matches`; `pool_loaded`/`pool_resynced` gain `poolVideos` and `poolFrames`; the logger's field allowlist grows exactly those three names. Counts only — the new integration test's log scan proves no frame hash appears.
- Client contract (`src/asmita/client.ts`): the pool schema is a union — the unchanged image entry, and `{ id, kind: "video", frames }` with 1..1000 strictly 64-hex frames (the WP17 submission cap). No new config (fail-closed Zod untouched), no migration (WP17's columns carry everything), no admin-UI or schema change.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run flow, the Matching section, the pool contract, a new "Video frames match the same way" guarantees row, the run summary, the bench list), the runbook's new WP20 paragraph, this entry. Root `CLAUDE.md` was not written (below).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New crawler: `tests/unit/video-index.test.ts` (13: exact and near frames, the threshold cap, best distance per video, two videos sharing a frame, withdrawal, duplicate frames, an empty pool, lower thresholds, a brute-force property test over random pools and 80 queries at two thresholds, every boundary distance 0..31 at the capped threshold, a 1000-frame video), `tests/unit/pool-matcher.test.ts` (5), `tests/unit/pool-contract.test.ts` (6), `tests/integration/video-pool.test.ts` (2, over the new `buildVideoPoolFixture` in `tests/support/fixture-site.ts`, host `vpdq.fixture.test`, synthetic images only: a poster whose hash is an image entry and a frame of two videos — one exact, one at distance 6 — posts exactly three findings with per-video distances, `videoMatches` 2 and `matches` 3 in the summary, the decoy image no finding, the page's `<video src>` never requested, the `pool_loaded` counts, the log scan; and a video finding the server no longer accepts is withdrawn through the same sink callback and counted by reason). 26 new tests.
- Existing crawler tests changed only where the pool type became a union (rule 6, also in the commit message): seven log-scan allowlists that read `f.pool.fingerprints` down to their hashes now keep only the image entries (`flatMap((f) => ("pdq" in f ? [f.pdq] : []))`) — the pools those tests build carry no video entries, so the hash lists are byte-identical; nothing was deleted, skipped or loosened.
- New app: `tests/unit/video-crawl-pool.test.ts` (7, memory path: the video entry shape from the stored frame list, the image shape unchanged beside it, the version formula kept for an image-only pool and changed by videos, the best-frame fallback — list gone, list unreadable, neither hash valid means the row is skipped — non-hex frames dropped, withdrawal, erasure clearing the frame list with the pool id).
- Existing app tests changed on purpose (rule 6, also in the commit message): the WP17 pool tests on all three backends now assert inclusion and resolution instead of exclusion (`video-hash-submission.test.ts`, `video-hash-prisma-path.test.ts`, `video-hash-kysely-path.test.ts` — each was written to pin the pre-WP20 rule 3 and each names WP20 as its end date), `video-crawl-authorisation.test.ts`'s video-only case now expects the authorisation (its own name said "WP20"), `crawl-consent-tick.test.ts`'s annex mock follows the rename, and `crawler-client-contract.test.ts` seeds a video fingerprint on its own case and asserts the real pool serves the video entry through the real client's schema and a video finding is accepted and stored (the video sits on its own case because findings of one case merge per location — the pre-existing dedup — which the test notes in place).
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, build ok, 50 test files / 555 tests all passed (46 / 529 before this WP: +4 files, +26 tests). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (4 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 153 test files / 1309 tests all passed (152 / 1302 before: one new file of 7 tests, the rest rewritten or extended in place).

**Negative check** (run, then reverted): degrading the index to exact-chunk probes only (`probeMasks = [0]`) failed `video-index.test.ts`'s brute-force property test on its near-duplicate queries — the probe radius is what finds them — while the integration test kept passing, honestly: its 6-bit near match always leaves error-free chunks, so it pins the end-to-end flow and the contract while the property test pins the index itself. Restored, all 26 new tests pass again.

**Not done or not verified**

- The Tier-1 platform-API payload still carries a VPDQ row's best-frame PDQ hash under `algorithm: "PDQ"` (WP19's recorded decision said WP20 is the right place for a video form). WP20's own scope is the pool and the matcher; changing the versioned partner payload is a product decision that still has no partner to receive it (none enabled), so it stays as WP19 left it — recorded here so it is not lost.
- The root `CLAUDE.md` crawler row was **not** updated: that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval (the standing decision since WP1). Intended addition (apply on approval): after the crawler sentence — "; since video-fingerprints spec WP20 the pool serves approved, consented video fingerprints (vPDQ) as `{ id, kind: \"video\", frames }` and the crawler matches poster and preview images against their frame hashes through an in-memory multi-index (one preview within the threshold is a finding, reviewed like any other; no video bytes are fetched)". `crawler/README.md` and the runbook document all of it instead.
- For counsel: the crawl authorisation is now offered for a case whose only fingerprint is a video. The template text and the agreement record still say "copies of my images" (unchanged legal text; WP19 deliberately never edited the template), and the annex names the video as "Video fingerprint (vPDQ, N frames)" beside the image hashes — counsel should read the authorisation with a video-only case in mind before one is signed. `reviewedByLegal` was not set anywhere.
- Resync-withdrawal of a video id is covered by the unit tests and the shared purge path (the same `FindingsSink.purge` and `PoolMatcher.withdraw` the image lifecycle test exercises end to end), not by a dedicated integration case: on the small video fixture the queue-to-flush window is too short for a re-sync to land deterministically between the two, and the server-driven withdrawal in the second integration test proves the same callback and purge on a video id.
- The Docker image build, CI on Linux and the Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged; no build-input change — the new crawler modules bundle from `src/` as before and the Dockerfile needs nothing new).
- No real site was crawled and no real video fingerprint exists (rule 7); every hash in every test is generated in code or comes from a synthetic image. No video fixture was needed: WP20's paths carry frame hashes only, the same decision as WP17's and WP19's notes.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The consent-gate flip is not in the WP20 text, but without it the WP is unreachable: a pool entry requires a live authorisation listing the video, and until the gate flipped a video-only case could not authorise one. The gate's own comment ("until video search is built, video-fingerprints spec WP20") named this WP as the switch.
- Image pool entries keep their exact pre-WP20 shape (no `kind` field): the WP names the video shape only, and leaving the deployed image shape untouched keeps the pool readable by a crawler one version behind during a rolling deploy. The crawler's schema is a union and tolerates both.
- "Multi-index hashing or a BK-tree": multi-index hashing, 16 chunks of 16 bits, probe radius floor(threshold/16). A BK-tree at radius 31 of 256 bits visits most of the tree — worse than the brute force it would replace at exactly the sizes the spec calls out.
- The 3-frames clause of the video match rule is not implemented: Phase 2 never samples an online video (no video bytes are fetched), so only the thumbnail clause (one preview image within the threshold) can fire. Documented in `crawler/README.md`; Phase 3 owns the other clause.
- A video entry serves its stored frame list as-is: the survivor's client already deduplicated and capped the list at submission, the index is insensitive to order, and re-deriving anything here would fork the method between client and pool. The crawler's client schema still enforces 1..1000 strictly 64-hex frames, so a bad pool entry is refused at load.
- `videoMatches` counts video hits in the match loop while `matches` counts findings queued; they can differ only where the same (fingerprint, image address) pair repeats within a run, which the per-run image dedup makes impossible in practice. Both are counts only (rule 3).
- The pool entry carries hashes only — no frame qualities or timestamps: the WP's shape is `{ id, kind: "video", frames }`, and the survivor's client already dropped low-quality frames at submission, so per-frame quality would add data without changing any match.

### WP21. Match video previews (crawler) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- New `crawler/src/image/sprite.ts` (WP21's one new module; its bounds are constants in code, not config — the WP14 precedent, no operator has a spec'd use case for tuning them): `isPreviewSpriteUrl` decides from the image's **address** (pathname only, never the query) that it is a preview sprite sheet — a contact sheet of the video's thumbnails — by the two path tokens the players that ship these files use, "sprite" and "storyboard" (deliberately not "thumb"/"thumbnail": those names cover single thumbnails, which are one preview already hashed whole). `spriteGrid` picks the tile grid from the decoded size: tiles at least `MIN_IMAGE_SIDE` on both sides, at most `MAX_SPRITE_TILES` (100), tile aspect aimed at 16:9 (video thumbnails), exact ties resolved to the fewest tiles — so a 16:9 photo that merely carries the name keeps its whole-image hash, and a true sheet cuts on its true lines whenever its reading is unambiguous.
- The split runs in the decoder child, as the spec requires: `hashSpriteImage` (`src/image/hash-image.ts`) decodes once under every whole-image rule (sniff, format agreement, pixel cap, minSide — the decode path is now shared by `hashImage` and `hashSpriteImage` through one internal `prepareImage`, no behaviour change to `hashImage`), cuts the grid out of the decoded pixels and computes one PDQ hash per tile — in memory, nothing on disk. A 1x1 grid (a misnamed single image) answers with exactly the whole image's hash.
- The decoder protocol gained a second request and reply shape, as strict as the first: the parent sends `{ id, bytes, minSide, sprite: true }` (normal requests keep the exact three-field shape they always had, which the fake worker's shape probe still pins); a sprite is answered with one reply carrying every tile — `{ id, ok: true, sprite: true, tiles: [{ pdq, quality }], rss }`, 1..100 strictly shaped tiles — or the unchanged error codes. `parseReply` validates the tile list as fiercely as the single hash (exactly `{ pdq, quality }`, 64-hex, 0..100), a reply of the **wrong kind for its task** — a single hash to a sprite request, a tile list to a normal one — marks the child compromised and replaces it, and the once-only retry of a child that died mid-task preserves the sprite flag. The isolation set is unchanged (scrubbed env, keyless argv, permission-model flags, heap cap, parent-side recycling, stdout discarded), and the fake worker's env/argv/shape probe now runs for sprite requests too.
- Extraction (`crawl/extract.ts`): the WP4 `<video poster>` candidate is now tagged "video preview" (`Extracted.videoPreviews`, a set of urlKeys — the tag is bookkeeping that never leaves the process); the `og:image` family is tagged when the page declares an `og:video` (any of the three OG video property spellings; `twitter:image` keeps its WP4 treatment, and an `og:image` without an `og:video` stays an ordinary candidate); a JSON-LD `VideoObject.thumbnailUrl` (string or array, `@graph`-walked, `@type` case-insensitive) is collected and tagged — only from `VideoObject` nodes, so another type's `thumbnailUrl` is nothing. Every one of them remains an ordinary image candidate: same fetch path, caps, robots and budgets.
- Video sitemaps (`crawl/xml-docs.ts`): Google's `<video:thumbnail_loc>` is parsed inside `<url>` entries exactly like `<image:loc>` — resolved against the document, attributed to its `<loc>` page, and costing one `MAX_SITEMAP_URLS` slot like every taken address (an unusable value spends its slot the same way an unusable `image:loc` does, and a thumbnail's slot is spent before its page's loc is). `SitemapPage` gained `videoThumbnails`, and the crawler hashes them for their in-scope page with the preview tag, during the sitemap's own fetch like image-sitemap entries.
- `crawler.ts` (the lane-shared file, six small hunks): `processImage` takes the preview tag from the extractor and the sprite decision from both the candidate address and the one that served it after redirects; the browser path decides the same way from the intercepted response's address (the markup-level tag does not cross the render protocol — see deviations); the shared decode -> match -> report tail (`decodeMatchReport`) takes `{ preview, sprite }`, calls the child's tile mode for a sprite, counts `videoPreviews` once per preview hashed and `spriteTiles` per tile, and matches every tile hash through the unchanged composite `PoolMatcher` — image fingerprints and video frames alike, best distance first. Several tiles of one sprite hitting the same pool id collapse into one finding through the existing per-fingerprint-and-image-address dedup; the finding contract is untouched (the finding carries the sprite's address as the image address, the page as claimed, the tile's distance and quality) and every match still goes to a person. The single-image quality floor keeps its `low_quality` skip counter exactly as before; a sprite's low-quality tiles are dropped without one.
- `metrics.ts`: the run summary gains `videoPreviews` and `spriteTiles` beside `videoMatches` — counts only; both live inside the `summary` object, so the logger's field allowlist needed no change.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (the run flow, a new "Video previews are image candidates (WP21)" guarantees row, the Deep coverage row's sitemap sentence, the Matching section, the decoder-isolation protocol paragraph, the run-summary list, a Known limits bullet), the runbook's new WP21 paragraph, this entry. Root `CLAUDE.md` was not written (below).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New `tests/unit/sprite.test.ts` (16: the address rule incl. query-ignoring and the deliberate non-matching of "thumbnail"; every grid case — the true 3x2 sheet, the single-row strip, the real-world 70-tile 10x7 sheet, 16:9 staying whole, the ambiguous 640x180 documented at its coarser (2,1) reading, the too-small-to-halve image, the 1024x1024 case where the exact (9,16) reading is past the tile cap, and the documented false-positive cuts of 320x240 and 800x600; the in-process split — every tile hash equal to the standalone image's, strips too, the misnamed single image answering its whole-image hash, the whole-image size rules applying before any cut, the 70-tile sheet inside the cap).
- New `tests/unit/video-preview-extract.test.ts` (15: poster tagging and its data-URI skip, the tag deduplicated across spellings, every og:video spelling gating the og:image family, twitter:image untagged, og:image untagged without an og:video, the og:video itself never a candidate, JSON-LD thumbnailUrl in @graphs and arrays, a non-VideoObject thumbnailUrl nothing, a VideoObject's image keeping its WP4 treatment, malformed blocks skipped whole, cap-dropped candidates untagged, and the sprite address being none of the extractor's business).
- New `tests/integration/video-previews.test.ts` (2, over the new `buildVideoPreviewFixture` — host `prev.fixture.test`, robots.txt naming a video sitemap, synthetic images only): every preview kind end to end — six findings with exact page and image addresses (the poster matching an image entry and a video; the og:image; the JSON-LD thumbnail; a sprite tile; the video-sitemap thumbnail attributed to its sitemap page), `matches` 6, `videoMatches` 6 (the sprite's two tiles both hit), `videoPreviews` 5, `spriteTiles` 6, `imagesHashed` 7; the same grid geometry under a plain name hashed whole with no finding even though its first tile is a pool frame (the address, not the content, gates the split); the decoy no finding; `/clip.webm` and the og:video target never requested (Phase 2 fetches images only); the log scan.
- Extended: `tests/unit/xml-docs.test.ts` (+3: thumbnail collection incl. CDATA beside image:loc, unusable values and their slot costs, and the cap pinning that a thumbnail's slot is spent before its page's loc); `tests/unit/decoder-isolation.test.ts` (+5: the real child splits a sprite into the standalone hashes, sprite requests keep the whole-image error codes, the strict sprite reply shape in parseReply, wrong-kind replies killing the child in both directions, and the sprite retry preserving the flag — plus a sprite-request probe inside the existing key-leak test). The fake decoder worker (`fake-decoder-worker.mjs`) gained the sprite request modes behind new first bytes.
- Existing tests changed only where a type gained a field on purpose (rule 6, also in the commit message): `xml-docs.test.ts`'s `loc` helper now builds the `SitemapPage` shape, which includes `videoThumbnails` — every assertion it feeds is unchanged. No existing test was deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, build ok, 53 test files / 596 tests all passed (50 / 555 before this WP: +3 files, +41 tests). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (4 pre-existing warnings in untouched files), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 153 test files / 1309 tests all passed (unchanged by this WP; no `asmita/` source was touched, only the runbook gained a paragraph).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval, which has not come for any WP since WP1 (silence is not consent). Intended addition (apply on approval): after the WP20 sentence — "; since video-fingerprints spec WP21 the crawler also hashes a page's video previews — video posters, og:video preview images, JSON-LD VideoObject thumbnails, video-sitemap thumbnails and preview sprite sheets (split into tiles in the isolated decoder child, one hash per tile) — against both image fingerprints and video frames". `crawler/README.md` and the runbook document all of it instead.
- The browser path tags an intercepted image "video preview" only when its address names a sprite: the markup-level tag (poster, og:image, JSON-LD) does not cross the render protocol, which would need a per-image flag that exists only to feed a counter. Matching is unaffected — WP20 already matches every rendered image against video frames, and WP16's gather already loads `<video>` posters — so the loss is counter precision on browser sources, which stay off for real sites until the worker is sandboxed.
- An `og:image` on a BROWSER-rendered page is not hashed at all: the WP7 rule "image candidates are the intercepted responses only" already discards the static extraction for rendered pages, and WP21 does not reopen it. AUTO pages never render when they have static image candidates, so the case is `render = BROWSER` sources only.
- The sprite split is a heuristic with documented, test-pinned limits (`crawler/README.md`, "Known limits"): a sheet whose own aspect is an exact 16:9 multiple is cut at the coarser reading (its finer tiles are lost), and a non-16:9 photo that merely carries the name is cut on wrong lines and loses its whole-image hash. A mis-split never produces a wrong finding — PDQ does not survive crops, so wrong tiles match nothing; a person reviews any match that fires.
- No real site was crawled (rule 7); the Docker image build, CI on Linux and the Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged; no dependency or build-input change — `src/image/sprite.ts` bundles like its neighbours, and the decoder-worker bundle carries it through `hash-image.ts`).
- No subagents were run for this WP: its parts share the decoder protocol and the lane-shared `crawler.ts`, so the work was done serially with a test run after each part, which caught three wrong test-arithmetic assumptions (two grid expectations, one sitemap slot order) before they could reach the full check.
- WP21 adds no platform-facing or survivor-facing text, so no counsel review mark is required (the WP19 rule); the finding contract and the analyst review path are unchanged.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "Preview sprite sheets" is not defined by the spec, so both halves of the feature are recorded decisions: a sprite is recognised by its **address** (pathname tokens "sprite" / "storyboard"), and its tiles by geometry (a 16:9-targeted grid search over the decoded size). Both bounded (tiles at least `MIN_IMAGE_SIDE`, at most 100 per sprite) and documented with their failure modes. A wrongly tagged image costs bounded tile hashes that match nothing; a missed sprite is hashed whole like any image and can still match as a poster-scale preview.
- The tile grid cannot be proven from bytes alone; ties resolve to the fewest tiles, so an exact-16:9 sheet (a 4x4 grid of 160x90 tiles, say) is cut at the coarser reading and its finer tiles are lost. Chosen so the commonest misnamed file — a photo — keeps its whole-image hash whenever its own aspect already hits 16:9; the cost of a wrong grid is coverage on that one file, never a wrong finding.
- Tiles below the PDQ quality floor are dropped without an `imagesSkipped` counter: they are pieces of an image already counted as hashed, not image fetches, and counting them as skipped images would distort the fetch-facing skip reasons.
- `videoMatches` can now exceed the video findings queued, by design: two tiles of one sprite hitting the same video count two hits (WP20's per-hit rule) and one finding (the per-fingerprint-and-address dedup). WP20's note said the two counts could diverge only on same-pair repeats "impossible in practice" — sprite tiles are that case, made deliberate; counts only, rule 3 intact.
- `video:thumbnail_loc` is matched literally, exactly as `image:loc` always has been: a sitemap that binds the Google video namespace to a different prefix is not read.
- A JSON-LD `VideoObject.thumbnail` (schema.org's older image property) is not collected; the spec names `thumbnailUrl`, and nothing in the WP asks for the other property.
- The whole-image hash of a sprite is not computed beside its tiles: a contact sheet's own hash is never one of its tiles' hashes (PDQ is not crop-invariant), so it would be one more pool query per sprite with no plausible match.

## SSCD

### WP28. A second matching layer with SSCD embeddings, seeded from confirmed copies (app + crawler) — 2026-11-19

Owner decision, 19 November 2026 (Saquib). Defined outside the crawler spec; the spec's "Rules that do not change" and "How to work" were followed. Design, licence analysis, numbers, risks and retention are in **ADR 008** (`asmita/docs/adr/008-sscd-second-layer.md`, status Proposed).

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- Off by default, on both sides. Crawler: `ENABLE_SSCD` (only the exact string "true"), `SSCD_SCOPE` (`matched_sources` default, `all`), `SSCD_MAX_PER_RUN` (2000), `SSCD_THRESHOLD` (0.85 cosine), `SSCD_MODEL_ID`/`SSCD_MODEL_VERSION` (must match the app; `sscd_disc_mixup`/`onnx-1`), `SSCD_MODEL_FILE` (absolute path, required when on), `SSCD_EMBED_DIM` (512), `SSCD_TIMEOUT_MS` (20 s) — all Zod, fail closed, in `.env.example`. App: `ENABLE_SSCD`, `SSCD_MODEL_ID`/`SSCD_MODEL_VERSION` read only in `crawler-flags.ts`; deliberately absent from `wrangler.jsonc`. Off, the wire, the behaviour and the logs are byte-for-byte pre-WP28 (the findings POST carries no `method` field, the summary carries no sscd counters, `pool_loaded` carries no embedding count) — pinned by an integration test.
- Model: `sscd_disc_mixup` (Meta AI, the SSCD copy-detection reference model). **Licence checked before any use: both the code and the weights are MIT** — the repo's LICENSE (relicensed from CC-NC to MIT, 2022-07-14) and the lead author's explicit confirmation that "The entire project, including model weights, is licensed under the MIT license" (GitHub issue #12); quotes and URLs in ADR 008. The artifact run is a third-party ONNX export whose provenance was verified against Meta's official torchscript (recorded source sha256 matches the official download, hashed independently); pinned URL + sha256 in the Dockerfile, an MIT notice shipped beside the weights, weights never in git.
- The fourth isolated child, `dist/sscd-worker.js` (`src/image/sscd-pool.ts` + `sscd-worker.ts`): image bytes in, one fixed-shape 512-dim vector out, the decoder child's whole discipline (scrubbed env, keyless argv with the model path, Node permission model with reads confined to its bundle, sharp, onnxruntime-node and the model file, heap cap, parent-side recycling — 500 tasks / 1.5 GiB RSS, because the ~98 MB model is resident — per-task timeout with kill-and-replace, once-only crash retry, stdout/stderr discarded, strict reply validation where a wrong dimension or a non-finite value kills the child). Decode reuses the decoder's rules (`prepareImage`, exported for it): sniff allowlist, sharp format agreement, pixel cap, minimum side. onnxruntime-node CPU, single-threaded, memory only. A separate child rather than a third request kind on the decoder, because onnxruntime's native memory profile is very different from sharp's; the decoder's protocol, budgets and tests are untouched.
- Seeding (the simpler correct timing, chosen per the WP's instruction and recorded): **at the moment of a PDQ match, while the bytes are held** — the crawler embeds the matched image once per image and offers the vector to the new `POST /api/crawler/embeddings`; the app resolves the pool id, recomputes the finding's location digest exactly as `recordCrawlFindings` does, and stores it (`FindingEmbedding`, `vectorEncrypted`, RLS policy `asmita_app_all` like every crawler table) **only when that finding exists, is APPROVED, and the authorisation is live** — the analyst's acceptance is the gate (`finding_not_confirmed`, `embedding_exists`, `model_mismatch`, `fingerprint_not_accepted`; the last withdraws the pool id like a finding rejection). Memory, Kysely (Worker) and Prisma paths aligned; no audit entry ever written for a vector. Known cost of the simple design, recorded in the ADR: one inference per match sighting even when the finding is not (yet) confirmed — bounded by the per-run cap.
- The pool serves, while the app's `ENABLE_SSCD` is on, each in-pool image fingerprint's embeddings as `sscd: { model, version, vectors }` (at most 16, newest first, only the configured model + version); entries without embeddings keep the pre-WP28 shape; the pool `version` covers the vectors. `GET /api/crawler/sources` gains `matched: true|false` (a finding on the source was already accepted) — the second layer's default scope gate.
- The second layer in the crawler: one scope gate covers every SSCD inference on a source (the second layer, the borderline score and the seeding — a confirmed finding's own source always carries the flag, so the seeding loses nothing); at most one embedding per image, evaluated once (a multi-fingerprint match embeds once, and a scope/budget refusal is decided once per image); images PDQ did not match are compared by brute-force cosine (`src/match/sscd-index.ts`; the scale bound — 2 KiB/vector, ~20 MB at 10k embeddings — is documented in ADR 008 and the README); a hit at or above `SSCD_THRESHOLD` becomes a finding with `method: "sscd"` + `sscdSimilarity`, no distance, the same analyst review, and always the second reviewer (a new layer starts strict). Borderline PDQ matches (distance 25-31) carry an `sscdSimilarity` score against that fingerprint's own embeddings — information for the analyst, never an automatic accept or reject. Sprite tiles and video hits are never embedded or scored; an image PDQ already matched is never scanned against other fingerprints' embeddings (the WP's words).
- App data model: `CrawlFinding` gains `method` (`PDQ`/`VPDQ`/`SSCD`, default `PDQ`; a data backfill relabels pre-existing video findings `VPDQ`; a finding reported without a method is labelled from the submission's algorithm), `sscdSimilarity`, and `distance` becomes nullable (an SSCD finding has no PDQ distance — the migration documents this one widening; everything else is additive). The closer-sighting merge handles null distance on all three backends (a distance-bearing sighting beats a distance-less one; a carried score never lowers the stored one while CANDIDATE). `planReview`/`needsSecondReview` treat a null distance as always-two-step. Analyst UI: an SSCD row shows "Matched by SSCD · similarity 0.87" (always needing the second reviewer), a borderline PDQ row shows the SSCD score labelled as information for the reviewer, and PDQ/VPDQ rows keep today's display.
- Deletion, all three backends: withdrawing the crawl authorisation, rejecting the fingerprint and erasing the case each delete the embeddings at once (wired into `withdrawCrawlConsent`, the Kysely and Prisma erase paths, `eraseCrawlMemoryForCases` and `reviewHashSubmission`'s reject); embeddings are not subject to the 30-day URL blanking (the address of a confirmed copy expires; the descriptor must keep working — ADR 008 records the retention reasoning).
- Consent: one sentence added to item 2 of `CRAWL_AUTHORISATION` (`prisma/template-seeds.ts`), seeded unreviewed (the seed logic resets `reviewedByLegal` because the legal text changed; it is never set by code): "Where a person at Asmita has confirmed that a copy matches, Asmita may also keep a numerical description of that confirmed copy — never the image itself — so that altered versions of it can be found too." **For the owner's approval; listed in the commit message.**
- Logs carry counts only: `sscdInferences`, `sscdMatches`, `sscdSkippedBudget`, `sscdSkippedScope`, `sscdEmbeddingsPosted/Stored/Rejected/Unsent` (present only while the layer is on), plus the child's own counters in the summary's `sscd` block and `poolEmbeddings` in the pool logs (gated the same way). No vector, dimension or per-image similarity is ever logged.
- Evaluation (`crawler/scripts/eval-sscd.ts`, not part of `npm test`, generated images only): 12 synthetic bases × 10 alteration families, run **through the real isolated child against the real pinned weights in the Node 22 container**. PDQ recall 30.0% (it caught only the re-encode, blur and colour filters); **PDQ+SSCD recall 58.3% at the chosen threshold 0.85** — the 10% crops 10/12, borders 9/12, every mirror flip 12/12; unrelated-image false matches at 0.85: 31 of 1,452 pairs (2.1%, highest cosine 0.867) on a deliberately hard synthetic set; **198 ms per image** through the child. All numbers, the threshold reasoning (and what still escapes: heavy crops, screenshot-with-chrome) are in ADR 008.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (intro, run flow, a new guarantees row, the decoder-isolation section's SSCD child, the pool/sources/findings contract, the embeddings route, the config table, the run-summary list, deployment notes, known limits), the runbook (a WP28 paragraph with the operator checklist for switching it on, the env tables both sides), ADR 008, this entry. Root `CLAUDE.md` was not written (below).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)
