- Migrations (the new table, the three finding columns, the backfill) have run only against the memory store, the recording Kysely fake and the mocked Prisma client, never a real Postgres (the runbook's standing go-live item; the RLS statements follow the 20261117050000 template exactly).
- The Kysely/Prisma pool reads and the embeddings route were not exercised against a live Worker (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1` remains unrun, pre-existing open item).
- No real site was crawled and no real fingerprint exists (rule 7); every hash and vector in every test is generated in code, and the evaluation set is synthetic images only — no photo of a person, no real survivor data.
- The root `CLAUDE.md` crawler row was not updated, following the WP1-WP90 precedent: that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval and the session was instructed never to wait for input. Intended addition (apply on approval): after the WP21 sentence — "; since WP28 the crawler can also match SSCD embeddings of analyst-confirmed copies as a second layer (off by default: `ENABLE_SSCD` both sides) — the crawler embeds a matched image in a second isolated child and offers the vector to the app, which stores it encrypted only for findings an analyst has confirmed, serves it back through the pool under the same consent gating, and every hit goes to the same human review (ADR 008)". The README, the runbook and ADR 008 document all of it instead.
- A subagent licence/model/ONNX research pass and a subagent app-side implementation pass ran in parallel with the crawler work (the research pass produced the licence quotes, the pinned artifact and its sha256, all independently re-verified by the evaluation run; the app pass's every file was audited against this entry's design before the checks).

**Deviations from the WP text, with reasons**

- **Seeding timing**: the WP offers "the next time it sees the image in memory (or at the moment of the match if it is still held; choose the simpler correct design)". Chosen: at the moment of the match, on every PDQ-match sighting, with the acceptance gate in the app — no pending-confirmed list needs to cross the pool (which would carry image addresses or digests back to the crawler), and a confirmed copy seeds on its next sighting anyway. The cost (one inference per sighting of an unconfirmed or already-embedded match) is bounded by `SSCD_MAX_PER_RUN` and recorded in ADR 008.
- **Runtime model-hash check dropped**: the WP pins the sha256 "in the Dockerfile" (done), and a startup re-check would need `node:fs` in `src/`, which rule 1's memory-only lint and static test forbid; the build-time check is the pin, and a container that lost its model reports `sscd_model_missing` per image, counted, never fatal.
- **The scope gate covers seeding and borderline scoring too**, not only the second layer: one uniform gate per source is simpler, and nothing is lost — a confirmed finding's own source always carries the `matched` flag, so every seeding opportunity sits inside the default scope.
- **An SSCD finding always needs the second reviewer**: the WP says borderline scores are "not an automatic accept or reject"; for pure SSCD findings (no PDQ distance at all) the two-step approval path is the cautious default for a new layer (recorded in ADR 008).
- **`distance` became nullable** (a widening, documented in the migration) instead of inventing a distance for SSCD findings: the app's merge/review/UI logic reads null as "least certain", and old rows and old code always wrote a distance.
- **The crawler sends `method` on every finding while SSCD is on** (including `pdq`/`vpdq`), not only on sscd findings: the borderline score needs a labelled pdq finding, and honest labelling of video findings costs nothing; while off, nothing is sent (byte-identical wire, pinned by test).
- **Embeddings accepted up to 50 per request on the app** (the crawler sends at most 10): the app's envelope mirrors the findings route's 50; the crawler's own cap keeps the body far under the server's 256 KiB.
- **`embedding_exists` answers as `status: "stored"` with a reason**: the route's result vocabulary is stored/rejected, and a repeat is a success (idempotence), not a rejection the crawler would count as refused.
- **`SSCD_MAX_PER_RUN` covers seeding and borderline scores as well as the second layer**: the WP names the cap for "SSCD inferences"; every inference is one child task of the same cost, so one budget is the honest accounting.

### WP17. Video fingerprints on the server (app) — 2026-11-19
## Crawler v3

### WP9. Find sitemaps robots.txt does not list (crawler) — 2026-11-18

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- Prisma: `VPDQ` added to `HashAlgorithm`; `HashSubmission` gained three nullable columns — `videoFramesEncrypted` (the frame list as JSON, encrypted with `encryptField`), `videoFrameCount`, `videoDurationSec`. Additive migration `asmita/prisma/migrations/20261119000000_video_fingerprints/` (enum value plus three ADD COLUMNs, nothing else). For a VPDQ row `hashEncrypted` holds the highest-quality frame's hash (ties: the earliest frame), `quality` that frame's quality, and `hashDigest` the sha256 of the sorted unique frame hashes joined with newlines, so the existing `@@unique([caseId, hashDigest])` dedups re-uploads whatever the frame order.
- `POST /api/cases/[caseId]/hashes` now takes a union: the existing image items (unchanged, `kind` defaults to image, `kind: "image"` also accepted) and strict video items `{ kind: "video", frames: [{ hash, quality, t }] (1..1000), durationSec: 1..3600, clientVersion? }` — the video item and each frame object are `.strict()`, so any other field is a 400 `invalid_payload`. Frame content is re-validated in `validateVideoItem` (`src/lib/hash-submission.ts`) with the same per-frame media-smuggling defence as an image hash (`data:`, comma, slash, plus → `media_payload_rejected`; hex → `invalid_hash_format`), producing per-item results and the same `media_payload_rejected` security event as images. Same declaration, CSRF and rate limits; a video counts as one item. The body cap rose from 16 KiB to 1 MiB, sized to the largest legal request (10 items x 1000 frames x ~96 bytes) and still rejecting anything a valid submission cannot be.
- Storage on every backend: the memory dev store keeps the frame list on the row (never returned to callers — every result and listing goes through a display projection that drops it), and Prisma stores it encrypted. Kysely touches `HashSubmission` in the pool and the erasure paths, both updated.
- Account erasure / case purge remove the frame list with the row on all three backends: the Kysely and Prisma erase functions and the memory store now clear `videoFramesEncrypted` in the same statement that clears `crawlPoolId`; the row itself is kept as for images (dispatch records still resolve, platforms keep blocking), and `HARD_DELETE_RETAINED` says so.
- Admin: the Fingerprints queue and the case page show a video as "Video · N frames · M:SS" (frame count pluralised; 60 minutes renders 60:00) above the digest prefix, with the same approve/reject actions. Survivor case page: "Video N ••••••••" (new key `case.fingerprints.videoItem`, en + hi, listed as machine translated in `hi-review-status.json`).
- Crawler pool: `buildCrawlPool` and `resolvePoolFingerprint` exclude `algorithm = VPDQ` on the memory, Kysely and Prisma paths (spec rule 3), so `/api/crawler/pool` never returns a video fingerprint until WP20; `crawler/README.md`'s pool contract documents it and the runbook's migration checklist gained the new migration.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/video-hash-submission.test.ts` (17: validator, digest/best-frame rules, memory store, frame-list erasure, pool exclusion, labels, i18n), `tests/unit/video-hash-route.test.ts` (8), `tests/unit/video-hash-prisma-path.test.ts` (3, mocked Prisma), `tests/unit/video-hash-kysely-path.test.ts` (3, recording Kysely fake), `tests/unit/video-hash-migration.test.ts` (7, schema and additive-migration assertions). 38 new tests.
- Existing tests changed only where the contract changed on purpose: `tests/integration/account-delete.test.ts` (its fixture row gained the two new display fields; the retained-fingerprints audit text it pins gained the frame-list sentence). Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests all passed, build ok (no `crawler/` source was touched by this WP). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 148 test files / 1211 tests all passed (38 of them new in this WP).

**Not done or not verified**

- No video fixtures were committed: WP17's server never sees video bytes (only frame hashes), so every test uses synthetic hex strings; the committed synthetic WebM belongs to WP18, which fingerprints a real file in her browser.
- Until WP19, a video fingerprint in the annex/dispatch path renders like one image hash (its best frame) through the existing annex builder — the spec assigns the video annex entry and its wording to WP19, and counsel must read that wording then.
- Migrations still exercised only against mocks and the memory store, not a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook open item); the new migration is additive only.
- The root `CLAUDE.md` row was not updated (protected agent-instruction file, needs the owner's approval — same decision as WP1). Intended addition on approval: note that `ENABLE_HASH_UPLOAD` covers video fingerprints too (vPDQ, WP17), which the crawler pool excludes until WP20.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "Account deletion and case purge remove the frames with the row" was read as: those flows clear `videoFramesEncrypted` on the kept row, in the same update that removes `crawlPoolId`. Deleting the whole row was rejected because hard delete keeps fingerprint rows on purpose (platforms keep blocking; `HashDispatch` rows are kept and reference them), and the video spec's own rule 2 demands no parallel deletion path for a video fingerprint. The frame list is the video-specific data, so that is what the erasure removes; the row keeps its best frame's hash, frame count, duration and dispatch history.
- Image items were left non-strict (unknown keys stripped, exactly as today) while video items are strict: the spec says both "strict Zod" and "existing image requests are unchanged", and making image items strict would newly reject requests the current client contract accepts.
- The admin label pluralises ("1 frame") rather than the literal "N frames"; the spec's "Video · N frames · M:SS" is a template, not a grammar rule.
- The route's body cap (16 KiB → 1 MiB) is not named in the spec; without it the largest legal video item (1000 frames, ~96 KiB) could never be submitted. The cap is still far below anything resembling media, and every field inside remains size- and shape-checked.
- Environment flake, not a code issue: this host runs three other lanes' agents against the same n22 lock and CPU, and two full-check attempts each failed on one load-sensitive pre-existing suite that this WP does not touch — attempt 1 on `asmita/tests/unit/pdq-reference-vectors.test.ts` (two 5 s PDQ vector timeouts; crawler fully green), attempt 2 on `crawler/tests/integration/browser-escape.test.ts` (two Chromium render tests; the same suite was green in attempt 1). A third run with no code change printed ALL CHECKS PASSED, both suites green.

**Re-verification (2026-11-19).** The lane re-ran WP17 after the previous session's check log ended cancelled mid-run (a container error after the crawler suite; no complete log), with the WP already committed and a clean tree. This attempt audited the committed work file by file against the WP17 spec (schema and additive migration, the route's strict video union and unchanged image items, the memory/Prisma/Kysely storage and erasure paths, the admin and survivor labels, the i18n keys, the pool exclusion on every backend, and the five new test files' coverage of the done-when list), changed no code, and re-ran the full checks from scratch: **ALL CHECKS PASSED** (`check.sh`, Node 22) — crawler lint, type-check and build with 29 test files / 350 tests; asmita lint (0 errors, the same 3 pre-existing warnings), type-check, `prisma validate` and 148 test files / 1211 tests. This note is the attempt's only change.

### WP19. Videos in notices, consent and review (app) — 2026-11-19
- `crawler/src/crawler.ts`: when a source's robots.txt (loaded after its first claim, the WP4 point) names no `Sitemap:` line, the crawler probes the conventional sitemap paths directly, once per source per run: `/sitemap.xml` on the source host first, `/sitemap_index.xml` only when the first answered nothing usable, and — a source approved on a subdomain — its registrable parent domain's `/sitemap.xml` as well (at most these three fetches). Every probe goes through the same safe-fetch path and per-origin robots.txt check as a page fetch (a robots-refused probe never reaches the network and costs no budget), waits the source's per-host delay (the off-site delay for the parent host), and counts against the source's `pageBudget` and the global `MAX_PAGES`. A 404/410, a robots refusal, a network failure, an off-host redirect or a non-XML body is quietly ignored — no error code. A probe that parses reuses the WP4 document path (`xmlLinks` → the bounded `xml-docs.ts` parsers, `MAX_SITEMAP_URLS` and the event cap, the non-content and query-variant rules, `<image:loc>` entries hashed for their in-scope pages), so for the parent probe only entries inside the source's own scope survive; the links attach to the source's first reported page entry through the same one-shot mechanism as the robots sitemap stash (`attachSeedLinks`). The parent probe's redirect hops are pinned to the parent hostname exactly.
- `crawler/src/crawl/url.ts`: `registrableParent` — the registrable parent domain (tldts, private suffixes honoured) when the source host sits on a subdomain of it, null when the host is the registrable domain itself (`example.com`, `someone.blogspot.com`).
- `crawler/src/metrics.ts` + the run summary: `sitemapProbes`, the probes actually made.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run flow, the Scope row's one deliberate parent-domain exception, the Deep coverage row, the bounded-state note, the run summary), the runbook's new "Sitemaps robots.txt does not name (crawler v3 WP9)" paragraph, this entry. No app-side change, no contract change, no new config knob (the paths and caps are fixed by the spec), `.env.example` unchanged.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/integration/sitemap-probe.test.ts` (6) — the spec's done-against, over a new `buildSitemapProbeFixture()` in `tests/support/fixture-site.ts` (hosts `plain`/`index`/`sub.probe.test` plus the parent `probe.test` and `sib.probe.test`, synthetic images only, link-less home pages so a probe is the only way the content pages can enter the frontier): (a) robots.txt without Sitemap lines but `/sitemap.xml` present — a urlset with an `<image:loc>` entry, and `/sitemap_index.xml` never probed after the hit; (b) only `/sitemap_index.xml` — the `/sitemap.xml` probe 404s quietly, the index's child sitemap is followed as a claimed row; (c) a subdomain source whose parent sitemap lists in-scope and out-of-scope pages (a sibling's and the apex's own) — the in-scope page reported and fetched, the out-of-scope entries dropped, never fetched and never reported as discoveries. Assertions: at most two probes per source plus one parent probe each (`probe.test/sitemap.xml` exactly 3, its robots.txt exactly 1, `sitemapProbes` 8), every planted image found exactly once (including the probe-time `<image:loc>`, attributed to its page), `errors === {}` (404 and non-XML quiet), `sitemapDocs`/`pagesXml`/`sitemapUrlsTaken`/`sitemapImages` exact, and a tight-budget run (pageBudget 5) proving probes spend the budget without ever taking the slots their own claimed batch needs.
- New unit cases in `tests/unit/url-robots-extract.test.ts` (+1): `registrableParent`'s subdomain, registrable-itself, private-suffix, trailing-dot and IP cases.
- Existing tests changed only where the new probes change counts on purpose (nothing deleted, skipped or loosened; every change annotated in place): `frontier-app.test.ts` (the etag source's robots names no sitemap, so its three probe paths join the expected request list, the 304-run's `pagesAttempted` is 2 pages + 3 probes, the two-process test counts the per-process probes apart from frontier pages and subtracts them from the attempted sum, the killed-run filter skips the probe paths), `frontier.test.ts` (three of the fifteen flood budget slots are probes: the `linksDropped` bound, the flood-host page count and the app-side claim carry them — the app cannot see probes, so its own day count is three short), `deep-coverage.test.ts` (the trap source's probes: `pagesAttempted` 57 → 60, robots `{ok:2}` → `{ok:2, absent:1}` for the parent probe's robots.txt), `scope.test.ts` (the parent-domain check becomes an exact allowlist: the only contact with the parent host is its robots.txt plus the one sitemap probe).
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 30 test files / 357 tests all passed, build ok (29 files / 350 tests before this WP). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 143 test files / 1173 tests all passed (unchanged by this WP).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval, which did not come for WP1–WP8 either (silence is not consent). Intended row addition (apply on approval): extend the architecture list's crawler sentence with "crawler v3 WP9: a source whose robots.txt names no `Sitemap:` is probed at the conventional paths (`/sitemap.xml`, then `/sitemap_index.xml`), and for a subdomain source also its registrable parent domain's `/sitemap.xml` — robots-checked, counted against the page budget, at most two probes plus one parent probe per source per run, with only in-scope entries kept". `crawler/README.md` and the runbook document all of it instead.
- No real site was crawled (rule 7): probing was exercised only against the local fixture server. A parent sitemap reached through redirects (an apex redirect to `www.`, say) is deliberately refused by the pin-to-the-parent-host rule and is therefore not covered by a fixture.
- Loop mode re-probes once per pass ("per source per run": each `run --loop` pass is a run); a pass that claims nothing probes nothing. No dedicated loop test asserts probe counts across passes — the per-pass cap is the same code path the one-shot tests cover.
- The Docker image build, CI on Linux and the Worker smoke were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged; no dependency or build change — tldts was already a dependency).

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "probe `/sitemap.xml` and then `/sitemap_index.xml`" is implemented as a sequential fallback: the second path is probed only when the first answered nothing usable (404, non-XML, or parsed but yielding no links). Probing both unconditionally would spend one extra request per source per run on every site with a working `/sitemap.xml` for no additional coverage; "at most these two probes" holds either way.
- The parent probe is unconditional for subdomain sources ("also probe"), even when the source's own `/sitemap.xml` hit.
- Probes count against the source's `pageBudget` and the global `MAX_PAGES`, but the claimed batch is reserved first: a probe may never take a budget slot its own carrier batch needs, or a source whose budget barely covers its first claim would probe every run and never crawl (the tight-budget test pins this). Consequence: a source whose first claim fills its entire remaining budget is not probed that run. The app cannot see probe fetches (they are not reported), so its daily budget counts only reported pages — the crawler's side is the stricter one (visible in `frontier.test.ts`).
- A robots-refused probe (the parent's robots.txt unreachable or disallowing) never reaches the network and costs no budget: the robots verdict is consulted before the budget slot is taken.
- Probe failures record no error code, a little beyond the two the spec names ("A 404 or non-XML answer is quietly ignored"): robots refusals, 410s, network errors and off-host redirects are quiet too. A probe is speculative — the source's page fetches already record real failures — and the expected misses must not pollute the error counts (a site without `/sitemap_index.xml` would otherwise log an error every run).
- `pagesXml` keeps its meaning, "claimed pages that answered with an XML document": a probe-parsed sitemap counts under the WP4 counters (`sitemapDocs`, `sitemapUrlsTaken`, `sitemapImages`) and `sitemapProbes`, not `pagesXml`, because it was not a claimed frontier row.
- The parent probe runs the whole parent document through the same `xmlLinks` path (so image-sitemap entries of in-scope pages would be hashed like any sitemap's), not literally only `<loc>` elements; the fixture's parent sitemap carries loc entries only. The scope filter the spec asks for is exactly the code that drops every out-of-scope entry of any sitemap.
- Existing test updates (rule 6, also in the commit message): the probes add real requests, a robots fetch and budget consumption to every source whose robots.txt names no sitemap, which changes exact request lists, robots outcome maps and page/budget counts in four test files. Each change is annotated in place; none weakens what its test proves — the scope test's parent-host check is now an exact allowlist, tighter than the old boolean.

### WP10. Count the same page once (crawler + app) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- The single annex builder now renders a video fingerprint (algorithm VPDQ) as one entry: `src/lib/notice-generator.ts`'s `buildHashAnnex` gained an optional `videos` list (`HashAnnexVideo` = the row's kept frame count plus its frame hashes) — one entry per video, "Video fingerprint (vPDQ, N frames)", numbered after the image hashes, followed by at most `MAX_ANNEX_VIDEO_FRAMES` (200) evenly spaced frame hashes (first and last always kept, the count noted when truncated) and one plain sentence saying how vPDQ is matched (the PDQ hash of one frame per second of playback, a match being frames within a small Hamming distance). Every frame hash gets the same 64-hex validation as an image hash (`hash_annex_invalid_hash`), and the image-only annex is byte-identical to before, including its "Algorithm:" line; with videos present the algorithm line names both (PDQ for images, vPDQ for videos; vPDQ alone when the case has no image hash, and the image "Hash values" section is then omitted).
- `src/lib/case-hash-annex.ts` (the one builder every consumer uses — signing, URL-notice and advisory dispatch, representative notices, and the crawl authorisation) splits each row by algorithm: images render exactly as before, a VPDQ row decrypts its stored frame list (the JSON of `{ hash, quality, t }` the WP17 route validated) and hands the hashes to the renderer. If the frame list is gone or unreadable — account erasure clears `videoFramesEncrypted` while keeping the row (WP17) — the entry falls back to the stored best frame's hash (`hashEncrypted`) under the same header, so an entry never silently vanishes; in practice the case gate (erasure also clears the case's signed advisory) makes that path unreachable, it is defence only.
- The crawl authorisation needed no code change: its fingerprint list is the annex appended to the unedited `CRAWL_AUTHORISATION` template (`src/lib/crawl-consent.ts` only gained a comment), so videos are named there as one entry with their frame count, and the tick's `templateRef` already pins video ids like image ids (a video added between preview and tick is refused `notice_changed_since_preview`).
- Admin approval of a video behaves like an image: the existing `/api/admin/hashes/[hashId]/approve` route is algorithm-agnostic and already auto-dispatches the signed advisory only to the platforms on `hashAdvisoryPlatformIds`; nothing was changed there, it is now proven for videos by test.
- **For counsel (the required mark):** the annex wording — the entry name, the frame-hash list, and the vPDQ matching sentence — is new platform-facing text that every hash advisory, URL notice and representative notice carries. It is written plainly, `reviewedByLegal` was never set anywhere, and counsel must read it before any video fingerprint is dispatched to a platform. It is recorded for them in `asmita/docs/product/crawler-notice-flow.md` ("For counsel") as well as here.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/video-hash-annex.test.ts` (8: renderer — mixed and video-only annexes, image-only byte-identical, the 200 cap with even spacing first/last kept, at-cap and over-cap notes, one entry per video, pluralisation and lowercasing, empty/malformed frame hashes), `tests/unit/video-annex-sign-dispatch.test.ts` (9: the done-when — signing records the video id and the PDF lists both entries; the dispatch email body is byte-identical to the signed PDF's body and both ids are recorded as sent; a video added after signing is not sent and no frame hash leaks; signing refuses a video added since preview; approval auto-dispatches only to the signed platform list; approval while a signed fingerprint is still pending sends nothing with `fingerprints_pending_review`; no dispatch without a signed advisory; the cleared-frame-list fallback; the representative annex for a matched video), `tests/unit/video-crawl-authorisation.test.ts` (3: the authorisation's list names the video as one entry beside the image hash and pins both ids, a video-only case authorises, no fingerprints still refuses). 20 new tests; no existing test was changed, deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests all passed, build ok (no `crawler/` source was touched by this WP). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 151 test files / 1231 tests all passed (20 of them new in this WP).

**Not done or not verified**

- No new i18n keys: WP19 adds no survivor-facing wording (the annex and the authorisation's appended list are English legal text shown under the existing `crawl.auth.englishNote`; the admin and survivor labels were WP17's), so the video spec's en/hi rule does not trigger here.
- No video fixtures and no ffmpeg run: WP19's paths carry frame hashes only (the video itself never reaches the server, WP17 stored the hashes), so every test uses synthetic hex strings; the committed synthetic WebM belongs to WP18.
- The Tier-1 platform-API payload (schemaVersion 1, no partner enabled) still carries a VPDQ row's stored best-frame PDQ hash under `algorithm: "PDQ"` — that shape has no video form, changing a versioned partner contract without a partner is riskier than leaving it, and the operative documents (email body and attached PDF) describe the video fully through the annex. WP20, which rebuilds video matching, is the right place to give the payload a video form.
- `crawler/README.md`, `asmita/docs/ops/crawler-runbook.md` and the root `CLAUDE.md` row are unchanged: no crawler behaviour, config or migration changed in this WP, and root `CLAUDE.md` stays a protected agent-instruction file (same decision as WP17). The one doc this WP required for counsel is in `asmita/docs/product/crawler-notice-flow.md`.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The spec names the video entry twice with slightly different words: "Video fingerprint (vPDQ, N frames)" for the annex (bullet 1) and "Video fingerprint, N frames" for the crawl authorisation's fingerprint list (bullet 2). The authorisation's list is the annex appended to the template, and bullet 1 makes `case-hash-annex.ts` the single annex builder — one text feeds both documents — so both cannot differ. The fuller wording from bullet 1 was kept everywhere: it names the entry as a video with its frame count exactly as bullet 2 asks, and adds the algorithm tag a platform needs. The template text itself is untouched, as bullet 2 requires. If counsel or the owner prefer the shorter name inside the authorisation only, it is a one-line follow-up.
- Frame hashes are listed plain (no per-frame quality or timestamp): the spec asks for "its frame hashes", the entry header already gives the kept frame count, and keeping the platform-facing text minimal is the safer default for legal review. Frame count pluralises ("1 frame"), as WP17's admin label already decided.
- The "one plain sentence" is placed once above the entries (not per entry), so a case with several videos states the matching rule once; the spec asks for one sentence, not one per video.
Progress notes for the video-fingerprint work packages (spec: `asmita/docs/product/video-fingerprints-spec.md`, draft ADR 007). Lane V2 (`feat/video-v2`) is WP18, coded against the WP17 contract written out in the video spec; lane V1 builds WP17 in parallel.

### WP18. Fingerprint a video in her browser (client) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22, final run on the committed tree). The WP18 Playwright journey (chromium) was also run to green inside the Node 22 container against a throwaway local Postgres 17 — it is not part of `check.sh`.

**What was built**

- `asmita/src/lib/pdq/vpdq.ts` (new) — the vPDQ method on our PDQ, one module with a pure half and a browser half.
  - Pure, unit-tested without a browser: `sampleTimes` (every whole second `t < duration`, so 6 samples for a 6.000 s file; at most 3600), `videoTooLong` (over 3600 s, refused before any frame is read), `submittedDurationSec` (whole seconds 1..3600; equals the number of sampled seconds by construction, so it always covers every `t`), `finalizeFrames` (quality below 50 dropped, a frame within Hamming distance 10 of the previous KEPT frame dropped, then at most 1000 evenly spaced frames), `bestFrameQuality`, `toVideoHashItem` (the WP17 wire item: kind/frames/durationSec/clientVersion only), and `VpdqError` with fixed codes (`video_too_long`, `video_cannot_read`, `video_no_frames`, `video_cancelled`).
  - Browser sampler `sampleVideoFrames`: her chosen file loads through an object URL into a hidden `<video>` (aria-hidden, off-screen, 2×2 px), the sampler seeks to each whole second, draws the frame to a canvas, computes PDQ with the same `computePdqHash` the photo flow uses, and yields `{ hash, quality, t }` per second. Cancelable through an `AbortSignal`, reports 0..100 progress, every wait is bounded (15 s metadata, 10 s per seek — a hang becomes `video_cannot_read`, never an infinite spinner), and the `finally` frees everything on success, failure or cancel: the decoder (`src` removed, `load()`), the element, the canvas and the object URL. `fingerprintVideo` drives the generator through the keep pipeline and returns the WP17 item.
- `SubmitForm.tsx`: the fingerprint picker accepts `video/*` beside `image/*` (and `.webm/.mp4/m4v/mov/mkv/ogv` when the browser reports an empty MIME type). A video is read with live "Reading your video on this device: N%" progress, the standing sentence that the video is not uploaded, and a Cancel button (aborts the read; finished files stay, the rest are dropped; nothing was sent); ready shows "Video fingerprint ready · N frames" and then "Video fingerprint created on this device." Each failure reason has its own plain sentence, every one ending with "Nothing was sent": longer than 60 minutes (read from the metadata, before any frame), cannot read / unsupported codec (suggests converting to WebM or choosing another file), and no frame with enough detail. The draft in sessionStorage carries the WP17 video item beside unchanged image items; a superseded selection (files picked again while a read is in flight) can no longer write the older run's state (active-controller guard).
- `ReviewSignForm.tsx` + `review-summary.ts`: the draft type admits both item kinds; the review lists a video as "Video N · fingerprint ready · N frames" with the quality badge of its best frame (`numbered` gained the `review.fingerprints.videoItem` key; the card title broadened from "Image fingerprints" to "Fingerprints" because it now lists both).
- CSP (`src/lib/csp.ts`): `media-src 'self' blob:` on every page — the hidden `<video>` reads her file from a same-origin blob: object URL, and soft navigations keep the source document's policy, so the exception cannot be scoped to `/submit`. `script-src` stays closed to `blob:` everywhere except `/talk`'s worklet. A blob: media URL is page-created data the page already holds; it grants no cross-origin media.
- i18n: new `submit.hash.video.*`, `review.fingerprints.video` and `review.fingerprints.videoItem` keys in `en.json` and `hi.json`; `submit.hash.fileLabel`, `submit.hash.fileHint` and `review.fingerprints.title` reworded. All new and reworded Hindi values are listed as machine translated in `hi-review-status.json` (video spec rule 4); no native review yet. No notice, annex or other platform-facing legal text was touched (that is WP19), and `reviewedByLegal` was not set anywhere.
- Fixture `asmita/tests/fixtures/video/synthetic-6s.webm` — 268 KB (under the 300 KB cap), VP9/yuv420p, 320×240, exactly 6.000 s, six one-second segments from six different ffmpeg lavfi synthetic sources, built with Docker ffmpeg (the host has none):

  `docker run --rm --user 1000:1000 -v <repo>:/w -w /w jrottenberg/ffmpeg:6.1-ubuntu -y -f lavfi -i "testsrc=duration=1:size=320x240:rate=25" -f lavfi -i "smptebars=duration=1:size=320x240:rate=25" -f lavfi -t 1 -i "mandelbrot=size=160x120:rate=25" -f lavfi -t 1 -i "cellauto=size=320x240:rate=25:rule=110" -f lavfi -t 1 -i "life=size=320x240:rate=25:mold=10:ratio=0.1" -f lavfi -i "testsrc2=duration=1:size=320x240:rate=25" -filter_complex "[2:v]scale=320:240[m2];[0:v][1:v][m2][3:v][4:v][5:v]concat=n=6:v=1:a=0[v]" -map "[v]" -c:v libvpx-vp9 -crf 40 -b:v 0 -pix_fmt yuv420p -an asmita/tests/fixtures/video/synthetic-6s.webm`

  Verified on the committed file: the six seconds are pairwise far apart (mean absolute pixel difference 100–160/255 between any two sampled seconds, measured from extracted frames), so one frame per second keeps all six after the quality floor and dedup — the "expected frame count" the Playwright test pins is 6 with t = 0..5.

**Decisions, recorded per the standing rule**

- "Her dashboard's add flow" is the same `SubmitForm`: the dashboard's add path is my-cases → "Submit a new report" → `/submit`, fingerprints close on a case's first signature (`hash-submission.ts` keeps intake to case creation) and the case page has no add-fingerprint flow to extend. One implementation covers both entries.
- The E2E intercepts `POST /api/cases/*/hashes` and answers the WP17 success shape: this branch carries the client only and its route would reject a video item (WP17 is lane V1, not merged here). The test asserts the exact WP17 contract on the request body (kind `video`, frames with 64-hex hash/quality/t, durationSec, clientVersion, none of the image item's fields); after the merge the interception can be dropped so the real route enforces the same schema server-side, and the assertion is identical either way.
- The E2E journey ends on the case-saved page, not the sign step: signing waits on the notice template's legal review, and no test may set `reviewedByLegal` (rule 8), so "nothing ready to sign yet" is the honest end state; the sign step itself is `notice-integrity.spec.ts`'s, which marks the template reviewed as the human reviewer.
- The frame cap picks indices with exact integer arithmetic (`floor(i * (n-1) / (cap-1))`): a float stride can round two picks onto the same index at the tail.
- A non-finite duration gets one bounded end-seek attempt (some WebM files report Infinity until a seek resolves it) and is then refused as unreadable; only a known duration over 3600 s gets the "longer than 60 minutes" message.
- "Unsupported codec or a read failure" share one kind message, as the spec words them together (suggest converting or choosing another file); decode errors and the bounded timeouts both map to it.
- `durationSec` sent to the server is the count of sampled seconds (ceil of the duration); its equality with `sampleTimes(d).length` is unit-tested over a sweep of durations.
- The quality floor is asserted against the exported constant (`VPDQ_MIN_FRAME_QUALITY` is 50; a frame exactly at the floor is kept, one below it is dropped), so the spec number is pinned in code, not repeated as literals.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New `tests/unit/vpdq.test.ts` — 12 tests, no browser: sample times start at 0 and cover every whole second (3600 at the cap), > 60 minutes refused and only those, the duration invariant, the quality floor (constant pinned; at-floor kept, below-floor dropped), dedup at real Hamming distances (distance 10 from the previous kept frame drops, 11 keeps), both cap cases (1500 → 1000 with every gap 1 or 2 original seconds; 1001 → 1000 strictly increasing, no float-stride collision), the empty output, best-frame quality, the strict WP17 wire item (exact key set; hex-only hashes; no `data:`, base64, comma, slash or plus anywhere), and the error shape.
- `tests/unit/csp.test.ts`: one existing assertion changed with the behaviour — the old blanket `expect(...).not.toContain("blob:")` is now scoped to `script-src`, because `media-src` carries `blob:` on every page — and one test added pinning `media-src 'self' blob:` on `/`, `/submit`, `/case/...`, `/review-sign`, `/talk` and the nonce-less policy, with `script-src` never containing `blob:` outside `/talk`.
- New `tests/e2e/video-hash-flow.spec.ts`, chromium project only (the spec pins it; the mobile project skips these two — a new test's scoping, not a loosened one):
  1. The full journey (7.9 s): register, pick the committed synthetic WebM beside a URL; the page shows the per-second reading progress (recorded from the DOM by a MutationObserver, because the six-second read is fast) and the "Your video is not uploaded." sentence; ready shows "Video fingerprint ready · 6 frames"; the review lists "Video 1" with "6 frames"; the submission carries exactly the WP17 video item — 6 frames of 64-hex, t = 0..5, quality 50..100, durationSec 6, clientVersion `vpdq-ts-*`, none of the image fields; the case is created and she lands on the case-saved page; and no request body of the whole run contains the fixture's bytes or any `data:image/` or `data:video/` URL — the video never leaves her device and no request body ever contains image or video data.
  2. Garbage bytes as `.webm` (3.1 s): the kind message ("We could not read this video… Nothing was sent."), the submit button stays disabled, and no `/api/cases/` request was made at all.
- Package totals from the final `check.sh` run on the committed tree (Node 22): `crawler` — lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok (untouched by this WP, re-confirmed green). `asmita` — lint 0 errors (the 3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script, no new ones), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 144 test files / 1187 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- Acceptance of the video item by the real route is WP17's own test duty (lane V1); on this branch it is asserted client-side against the WP17 contract with the route stubbed at the network layer (see decisions). The same journey runs against the real route after the lanes merge.
- The browser sampler is verified on the committed fixture and on undecodable bytes; it has not run on a real survivor video, a real device, or exotic codecs (HEVC on some devices, hour-long files) — ADR 007 already records that browsers cannot read every format; the kind message is what that case gets.
- The mobile project does not run the video spec (the spec pins the journey to chromium).
- The E2E run needed a throwaway Postgres 17 reachable at the Node 22 container's loopback 5433 (`tests/e2e/support/database.ts` refuses any other address). The local, uncommitted TCP forwarder used for that (`asmita/e2e-pg-proxy.mjs`, environment tooling, not product code) and the container's corrected password (the throwaway was first created with a password that did not match the URL committed in the repo; the container was aligned to the repo, not the reverse) are host setup, outside the repository.
- No `crawler/` source or doc changed (no crawler behaviour change), so `crawler/README.md` and the runbook are untouched. Root `CLAUDE.md` was not updated (protected agent-instruction file; as for WP1–WP3 and WP8 the write waits for approval). Intended addition (apply on approval): in the feature-state table's hash-upload row, note that video fingerprints (vPDQ) are fingerprinted client-side in her browser (WP18, `src/lib/pdq/vpdq.ts`; one frame per second, only frame hashes, qualities and timestamps are ever sent) with server storage (WP17) and notices (WP19) on the other lanes.
- The Hindi video strings are machine translated (listed in `hi-review-status.json`) and have not been read by a native speaker; the survivor-facing UI wording has not been reviewed by a human, only exercised by the Playwright run.

### WP37. Review labels on crawler findings (analyst console) — 2026-11-19
**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

This WP overrides the spec (assigned as a lane brief, not present in `full-crawler-spec.md`): a merge-conflict resolution, not feature work. master had just merged PR #75 (`feat/platform-partners`) and the video-fingerprint lane (WP17–19); this branch carries the partner-sharing lane (WP31–33). No feature code was added, no test was deleted, skipped or loosened.

**Conflicts resolved, both sides' intent kept**

- `asmita/src/i18n/hi-review-status.json` — both sides appended keys to the same list; both kept: the branch's `partnerShare.*` and master's `submit.hash.video.*`, `submit.hash.fileLabel`, `submit.hash.fileHint`, `review.fingerprints.videoItem`, `review.fingerprints.video`. Valid JSON, no duplicate keys. Verified programmatically: every string either side added is present in the merged file; the same check over `en.json` and `hi.json` (auto-merged by git) found nothing lost either.
- `asmita/src/lib/case-ops.ts` — three conflicts, all in the hard-delete path. `HARD_DELETE_RETAINED.fingerprints` now states both: fingerprints are kept encrypted, removed from the crawler pool **and withdrawn from the partner feed**, and a video fingerprint's frame list is removed with the erasure (its row keeps the best frame's hash). The Kysely and Prisma erase twins keep the branch's WP32 steps (feed WITHDRAW events written in the transaction before the agreements go, `PartnerOutcome` rows deleted, `PartnerShareConsent` rows deleted) and master's change to the same closing `HashSubmission` update, which now clears `crawlPoolId` and `videoFramesEncrypted` together.
- `asmita/src/lib/store.ts` — the memory erase keeps the branch's `erasePartnerShareConsentsForCases(erasedCaseIds)` and master's loop that drops a kept VPDQ row's `videoFrames` list.

**Real breakage the merge caused, fixed (fakes extended, assertions untouched)**

- `tests/unit/video-annex-sign-dispatch.test.ts` (master's): the branch's WP32 wraps `reviewHashSubmission` in `db.$transaction` and syncs the partner feed inside it. The test's `@/lib/db` fake had no `$transaction`, no `case.findMany`, and no partner delegates, so approval threw and the route answered 404. The fake gained a `$transaction` passthrough (the same pattern as the branch's own `hash-one-signature.test.ts`), a `case.findMany`, and partner-feed reads that answer empty — the suite signs no partner-sharing agreement, so no feed event is ever appended. All 10 tests pass with their original assertions.
- `tests/unit/video-hash-prisma-path.test.ts` (master's): the merged `eraseUserWithPrisma` calls `tx.partnerOutcome.deleteMany` and `tx.partnerShareConsent.deleteMany` (branch, WP32), which the fixed tx mock lacked; the erasure threw, the job was marked FAILED and `hardDeleteDueUsers` returned `[]`. The tx mock gained the two delegates plus `partnerFeedEvent.findMany/createMany` (the shared-fingerprint read returns nothing here, so no event is written). The test's assertions, including `data: { crawlPoolId: null, videoFramesEncrypted: null }`, are unchanged and now pass against the merged code.

No production code was changed to make tests pass.

**Tests** — final `check.sh` run on the committed tree (Node 22): `crawler` — lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok (untouched by this WP, re-confirmed green). `asmita` — lint 0 errors, type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 169 test files / 1456 tests all passed.

## WP39. Site chrome, navigation, target sizes and CSS accessibility (app) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

Web-audit work package (accessibility/forms/performance/security audit, owner-approved 2026-11-20), not one of this spec's WPs; the spec's "Rules that do not change" and "How to work" applied alongside the audit rules. Branch `fix/a11y-chrome-css`, cut from `feat/platform-partners` and built on its Header/MobileMenu/Footer/globals.css changes. No `crawler/` source was touched; nothing here changes crawler behaviour, so `crawler/README.md`, the runbook and the CLAUDE.md crawler row needed no update.

**What was built** (paths relative to `asmita/`)

- A1: "Skip to main content" link as the first focusable element of the page chrome (`src/components/layout/Header.tsx`, first child of the shell in `AppShell.tsx`), visually hidden until focused; `<main>` gained `id="main"` and `tabIndex={-1}`. `nav.skipToMain` in en and hi.
- A14: the mobile menu button's accessible name now matches its visible state — no `aria-label` overriding "Menu" while showing "×"; open state = aria-hidden glyph + visually hidden "Close menu" (`nav.menuClose`).
- A15: Escape and outside-tap close the mobile menu and return focus to the menu button; nested `details/summary` submenus replaced by `button aria-expanded aria-controls` toggles (current-page group still starts open).
- A16: footer language toggle buttons are ≥44px, wrapped in `role="group"` with an `aria-label` (`nav.language`), `lang="hi"` on "हिंदी"; segmented look kept.
- A18: pages that render the fixed Quick Exit pill get bottom clearance so nothing focusable sits under it (`html:has(.quick-exit) body { padding-bottom }` plus `scroll-padding-bottom` on html).
- A19: CopyDraft copy button ≥44px; the live region moved outside the button.
- A20: eligibility Back button ≥44px; the question heading is focused on every index change, including back to question 1 (not on first render).
- A22: FAQ chevron uses `content: "↓" / ""` so it is not announced.
- A24: hard-coded English accessible names moved to i18n keys in FlowProgress (`flow.progress`), MobileMenu (`nav.mobile`), Header (`nav.primary`, `nav.home`) and Footer (`nav.home`); all seven new keys added to `en.json` and `hi.json` and listed as machine translated in `hi-review-status.json`.
- A26: `.field:focus-visible`/`.input:focus-visible` outline restored (2px `var(--brand)`).
- P6: `.field`/`.input` and `.filing-input` borders drawn in smoke (`var(--color-smoke)`, ~5.7:1 on parchment) instead of ash (~1.5:1) or transparent — full borders, no side stripes.
- P7: `html { font-size: 16px }` removed; every px font size in globals.css (tokens, rules, clamp bounds) converted to rem; `<meta name="text-scale" content="scale">` added via Next `metadata.other` in `src/app/layout.tsx`.
- P9: `text-wrap: pretty` scoped to `.reading-body p` and `.faq-answer`; `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on `.reading-body` and the chat bubbles (new `chat-bubble` class in `HomeChat.tsx`).
- P10: hover lifts on `.link-card` and `.home-help-tile` moved inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`.
- P12: `color-scheme: light` on `:root`.
- Home axe flake: `tests/e2e/accessibility.spec.ts` hardened — `page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: "reduce" })`, a global reduced-motion CSS rule that turns off all transitions/animations, `document.fonts.ready` awaited before analyze, routes /eligibility, /track, /check-image, /start and /get-help added, plus new tests: skip link as first Tab stop moving focus to main; language toggle ≥44px (mobile project); wizard heading focus on every step change.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/site-chrome-a11y.test.ts` (24 tests: chrome markup in both locales, menu button semantics, focus-return source guards, language group, CopyDraft live region, wizard heading, globals.css regression guards incl. no px font sizes left, i18n parity and review-status listing). New e2e tests inside the hardened accessibility spec as above.
- No existing test needed to change: nothing else asserted the old markup.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build, all passed (re-run to capture counts; no crawler source touched). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 145 test files / 1206 tests all passed.
- Playwright: the full suite runs in the separate verify step; the accessibility spec itself was additionally run here against a throwaway local Postgres 17, both projects: 29 passed, 1 skipped (the 44px toggle test is mobile-project-only), 24 axe scans over 12 routes, no flake.

**Not done or not verified**

- The optional second commit converting Tailwind `text-[Npx]` classes in tsx to rem was not done (the brief marks it optional; globals.css is fully rem).
- No screenshot diff was run; "identical output at default settings" holds by construction (rem = px/16 at the default 16px root) and by the passing e2e, not by pixel comparison.
- Observed, left alone (pre-existing on master, outside this WP): `/my-cases` renders the Quick Exit button twice — once from `(victim)/layout.tsx` and once from the page itself. Both render at the same fixed spot, so it looks like one pill; two DOM buttons. Worth folding into a later WP.
- The Worker smoke was not run (not part of this WP's checks; the CSS/markup changes are Worker-neutral).

**Deviations from the brief, with reasons**

- A18 clearance is scoped in CSS with `html:has(.quick-exit)` instead of adding a class to every page that renders the pill: the pill renders inside `main` on the auth pages but outside the page shell on victim pages, so one structural hook per page would not cover both positions.
- The skip link is rendered by `Header.tsx` (the brief named AppShell.tsx and Header.tsx): Header is the first child of the page shell, synchronous, and unit-testable; AppShell is an async server component that cannot be statically rendered in vitest.
- Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` all transitions and animations are disabled globally (`*, *::before, *::after`), which subsumes the previous per-selector list and is what the flake hardening asked for.
- The seven new Hindi strings are machine translated by the implementing agent (no Sarvam run for this batch) and are listed in `hi-review-status.json` with the batch and date recorded in `machineTranslated.by`; they await the same native-speaker review as the other machine-translated keys.

## WP55. Merge master into fix/a11y-chrome-css; resolve conflicts — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22). A merge-lane work package, not in `full-crawler-spec.md`: master had moved on (PR #73 video fingerprints WP17–WP19, PR #75 feat/platform-partners, PR #76 constraints scoping) and this branch's PR could not merge. A merge of `origin/master` (e7eebb4) into `fix/a11y-chrome-css` was started and left with conflicts; this WP resolved them, keeping **both** sides' intent, behaviour and tests, and committed the merge (c23ba69, a true merge commit, no rebase).

**What was done**

- Only four files were touched by both sides, so only they could conflict: `asmita/src/i18n/en.json`, `asmita/src/i18n/hi.json`, `asmita/src/i18n/hi-review-status.json` and `crawler/docs/v2-progress.md`. The three JSON conflicts were resolved and staged as unions; this WP verified each one programmatically against both parents (every key path from HEAD and from master present, valid JSON, no duplicate keys, en/hi parity 1005 = 1005, both sides' `machineTranslated` key lists and both sentences in `machineTranslated.by`), then resolved the remaining conflict in `crawler/docs/v2-progress.md`: master's "## Video" section (WP17, WP19, WP18, in master's order) followed by this branch's WP39 entry, byte-for-byte as each side wrote them, nothing dropped from either.
- No feature work, no test changes: the merge resolution adds no code. The 48 staged files are master's video lane, its docs and migration, plus this branch's already-committed chrome work; each non-i18n file was touched by one side only, so git merged them without conflict.
- Full checks after the resolution: **ALL CHECKS PASSED** (`check.sh`, Node 22) on the first run — `crawler`: lint, type-check, build, 29 test files / 350 tests; `asmita`: lint (0 errors), type-check, `prisma validate`, 154 test files / 1283 tests. Both sides' suites run together here for the first time (WP39's `site-chrome-a11y` and the hardened accessibility spec's unit siblings beside the video lane's 10 new test files), which is the real proof the merge dropped neither side's behaviour.

**Decisions, recorded per the standing rule**

- The lane arrived with the three i18n JSON conflicts already resolved and staged in the working tree, leaving only `crawler/docs/v2-progress.md` unmerged. The staged resolutions were audited against both parents (key-set union, duplicate detection, value comparison) instead of being redone, and the audit passed on every count; only the progress-doc conflict was resolved by hand here.
- In `en.json`/`hi.json` the video lane's rewordings of `submit.hash.fileLabel`, `submit.hash.fileHint` and `review.fingerprints.title` win: WP39 added keys only and never touched those three strings, so both sides' intent is the union of keys plus master's wording — the WP39 reading of those strings ("photos") would have dropped the video lane's deliberate broadening.
- In `v2-progress.md`, master's Video section comes first and the WP39 entry last: the file is an append log in completion order, the video entries reached master (PR #73) before this branch's WP39 was cut, and keeping our branch's entry as the trailing append is the shape the spec's reporting rule expects when the branch later merges back.
- No rebase, no force push, no push at all: the lane's instructions forbid them; the merge commit carries both parents (0695abf, e7eebb4).

**Not done or not verified**

- The branch was not pushed (forbidden by this WP's brief); the PR's mergeability after this commit is therefore not observed here.
- The Playwright e2e suite is not part of `check.sh` and was not run in this WP; both sides' e2e specs (`accessibility.spec.ts`, `quick-exit.spec.ts`, `video-hash-flow.spec.ts`) coexist in the tree untouched, compiled by lint/type-check, but were executed only by their lanes' own runs.
- Nothing else was verified beyond the full checks: no Worker smoke, no real Postgres, no deployment — all pre-existing open items, unchanged by a merge resolution that adds no code.
## WP40. Web audit: forms, errors and focus in the survivor flow — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22). This is an owner-approved best-practice audit work package (A3, A4, A7–A13, A23, A27, S12), not a crawler-spec WP; no `crawler/` source changed, and the crawler re-ran green only to confirm nothing broke.

**What was built**

- A3: the registration code entry is one single box (`RegisterForm.tsx`): `type=text`, `inputmode=numeric`, `autocomplete=one-time-code`, `maxlength=6`, `pattern=[0-9]{6}`, letter-spacing, visible label. The six per-digit boxes and their hard-coded "Verification code digits" group name are gone; typed, pasted and autofilled codes all land in it. The admin sign-in already used a single box and does not share the component.
- A4: one shared `asmita/src/components/FormError.tsx` (`role="alert"`) now carries the failure messages of `SubmitForm`, `ReviewSignForm` (sign step), `SignNoticeForm`, `DeleteAccountForm` and `CaseDashboardActions` (success lines stay polite `role="status"`). RegisterForm's local copy is the component that was promoted. ImageChecker is WP43's, untouched.
- A7: the per-line link checks left the `aria-live` list; they recompute after a 600 ms pause or on blur (the draft is always parsed fresh at submit, so a quick submit sends what was typed), and one summary sentence ("Links ready to file: N. Lines that will be left out: M.") is announced in a `role="status"`.
- A8: video hashing shows a native `<progress>` element; React progress updates are throttled to about four a second (first and final ticks always land). The file list is no longer a live region; the start ("Your video is not uploaded.") and finish ("Video fingerprint created on this device.") sentences are polite statuses.
- A9: `SubmitForm`, `ReviewSignForm` (sign step), `SignNoticeForm` and `FeedbackForm` wrap their fields in a real `<form>` with a `type="submit"` button, so Enter submits. The buttons are disabled only while submitting; validation runs on submit and focuses the first problem. Request bodies, signed text and the fingerprint annex logic are untouched.
- A10: the sign forms' contact email is checked on blur, sets `aria-invalid`, links its error and existing hint via `aria-describedby`, clears on input, and gets `autocapitalize=none autocorrect=off spellcheck=false`.
- A11: focus moves to each new step's heading (`tabIndex=-1`) — registration age → email → code (and the track-mode "no case" screen), review → sign, and the helpline call start; after a wrong code, focus returns to the code box.
- A12: the registration age radios are a `fieldset` with a visually hidden legend (the existing "How old are you now?" string) and share the `ageNow` name; the second question's radios share `ageThen`.
- A13: the submit form's platform checkboxes are a `fieldset` with the existing question as `legend`; the fieldset takes the focus when a hash-only submit has no platform chosen.
- A23: every `FeedbackForm` field is described by its hint (`aria-describedby`).
- A27: after the account deletion request, the done screen's message is a `role="status"` and focus moves to its heading.
- S12: the submit form's links textarea gets `autocomplete=off autocorrect=off autocapitalize=off`.
- New strings in `en.json` + `hi.json`, listed as machine translated in `hi-review-status.json`: the link summary, six sign-form field errors (name / contact / confirmation, in both the review-sign and case-sign wordings), and the hidden helpline-call heading.
- Docs: `asmita/docs/product/web-audit-progress.md` created with the WP40 entry (what changed, what was not verified, sentences needing the owner's approval, decisions made alone).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New `tests/unit/forms-focus.test.ts` — 15 tests: the shared FormError markup, the age fieldset/legend/shared name, the single code box (source-pinned: `one-time-code`, `maxlength=6`, `pattern=[0-9]{6}`, no per-digit ids), the sign form's form/hint linkage/autocorrect-off, the feedback form's described hints, the submit form's never-disabled button, no `aria-live` anywhere in it, the `<progress>` element and the 250 ms throttle, and the eight new keys present in both languages and listed as machine translated.
- New `tests/e2e/forms-focus.spec.ts` — 4 tests: a failed code request shows `getByRole("alert")`; Enter submits the feedback form; the helpline call start focuses the new screen's heading; and the database journey (chromium only, the reviewer marks the template reviewed as in notice-integrity.spec.ts) — Enter on the sign screen announces and focuses each missing field, then signs from the Enter key and lands on the confirmation page.
- `tests/e2e/reporting-journey-polish.spec.ts`: the six-digit-boxes test rewritten for the single box (typed, paste-equivalent insertion, autofill-style fill, registration completes) plus a new wrong-code test (alert + focus back on the box) — the old markup it asserted is gone by design.
- `tests/e2e/filing.spec.ts`, `hash-flow.spec.ts`, `video-hash-flow.spec.ts`: the three `toBeDisabled()` assertions on "Continue to review" became click-then-assert (status hint + focus on the first problem), because A9 removes validation-blocking disabled states on purpose; `video-hash-flow.spec.ts` also gains the visible `progress` element assertion.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests all passed, build ok (no `crawler/` source touched). `asmita`: lint 0 errors (4 pre-existing warnings in untouched files), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 153 test files / 1265 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- The full Playwright run is the separate verify step and was not run here (this host has no throwaway Postgres 17 on the E2E port); the new and changed specs are written and type-checked with the rest of the app.
- The Hindi sentences were drafted by machine, not read by a native speaker (flagged in `hi-review-status.json`).
- Screen-reader behaviour was reasoned from ARIA semantics, not listened to with a running screen reader.

**Deviations and decisions, with reasons**

- The line checks lag 600 ms behind typing (the audit asked for "blur or a short pause"); the submit path always parses the current text, so nothing typed is lost by submitting quickly.
- The video progress keeps its visible "Reading your video on this device: N%" text beside the new `<progress>` bar: the audit's complaint was the per-tick announcement, which the removed live region and the throttle fix; the committed e2e's recorded progress (two distinct values, ending at 100) still holds because the first and final ticks always render.
- The registration and code-entry buttons keep their existing gating (loading, 6 digits): A9 names only the submit, review-sign, sign and feedback forms, so their rule was not extended beyond the list.
- The age radios' legend reuses the existing "How old are you now?" string, visually hidden — no new sentence was added for it.
- The wrong-code e2e uses "000000" against a random code; the 1-in-a-million collision is accepted (all codes in every test are synthetic).
- "Forced submit failure shows an alert" is covered by intercepting `POST /api/auth/request-otp` with a 500 on the email step (no database needed), which exercises the shared FormError on a real failure path.


## WP42. Web audit: image and video fingerprinting off the main thread — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22). This is an owner-approved best-practice audit work package (P1, P2, P13 and the fallback, CSP and build checks), not a crawler-spec WP; no `crawler/` source changed (the crawler re-ran green only to confirm nothing broke — it imports `pdq.ts`, whose exports and `computePdqHash` are untouched, and its `pdq-contract` test still pins exactly that).

**What was built**

- P1: a module Web Worker (`asmita/src/lib/pdq/hash.worker.ts`) now does the PDQ hashing. The form hands it her chosen file; the worker decodes it (`createImageBitmap`), draws it to an `OffscreenCanvas` at the image's own size and reads the pixels — the same full-resolution path the main thread used, so hashes stay bit-identical. Only `{ hash, quality }` comes back.
- P2: the vPDQ sampler keeps its frame selection exactly (same seeks, seconds and keep pipeline) and hands each drawn frame to the same worker as a transferable `ImageBitmap`. The page canvas, object URL and bitmaps are freed as before.
- P13: `SubmitForm` has no static import from the pdq modules any more (only erased type imports); it loads the hashing entry (`hash-file.ts`, which carries the worker client, PDQ and vPDQ) only when a file is chosen. Verified in the builds: the submit chunk contains none of the hashing code; the worker is a separate file in `next dev`, `next build` (Turbopack) and the vinext/Cloudflare build (`dist/client/_next/static/workers/…`).
- Fallback: Worker/OffscreenCanvas/createImageBitmap missing, or a worker that fails to load (then not retried for the page) → the exact previous main-thread path runs, for images in `hash-file.ts` and per frame in the sampler; a worker that answered once and crashed is rebuilt. Strict reply validation (id, 64-hex hash, integer quality, fixed codes); anything malformed terminates the worker and the request fails with a fixed code the callers treat as "hash on the main thread".
- CSP: `worker-src 'self'` on every policy; no `blob:` — both bundlers emit the worker as a real same-origin file (verified by loading it with a 200 in the e2e specs).
- One-line fix found while verifying: the video entry waits one macrotask before its "ready" line replaces the reading line — the worker's async tail let React batch the final 100% progress tick (which WP40 A8 always keeps) with the completion update, so the reading line never visibly reached 100%.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New `asmita/tests/unit/hash-worker.test.ts` — 20 tests: the worker's message handler returns bit-identical hashes to the direct function on all 8 Meta reference vectors (and through the file and bitmap paths with stubbed decode steps); fixed error codes (`decode_failed`, `hash_failed`, `bad_request`), unanswerable messages return null, every touched bitmap is closed (also on failure), and replies serialise to exactly `{id, ok, hash, quality}` / `{id, ok, code}` with no media; plus source pins for the lazy load (P13), the module-worker construction, the frame transfer, both main-thread fallbacks and the CSP.
- `tests/unit/pdq-reference-vectors.test.ts` and every existing pdq/vpdq test: unchanged and passing (bit-identical hashes; the pure functions and exports are untouched).
- `tests/unit/csp.test.ts` +1: `worker-src 'self'` on every policy variant, never `blob:`.
- E2E (chromium, against a throwaway local Postgres 17): `hash-flow.spec.ts` (5) and `video-hash-flow.spec.ts` (3) pass — both journeys now also assert the worker script loads with 200, the video journey still records its progress line ending at 100%, and a new CPU-throttled test proves Cancel stops the video read mid-hash, drops the file and leaves the form usable (no hang on the in-flight worker request). `filing.spec.ts` (3) also passes.
- Package totals from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22): `crawler` — lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok (no `crawler/` source touched). `asmita` — lint 0 errors (the 4 pre-existing warnings in untouched files, no new ones), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 154 test files / 1286 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- The full Playwright run is the separate verify step; the WP's own specs and the neighbouring ones that exercise the same page were run here (see Tests).
- No real phone and no real 12-MP photo were available: the offload is structural, but the milliseconds saved were not measured on real hardware.
- Safari and Firefox were not driven; their fallback paths are the previous main-thread code, covered by unit tests and source pins.
- The `/check-image` page is WP43's and was not touched.

**Deviations and decisions, with reasons**

- The worker decodes her file itself (the WP allowed "createImageBitmap on the main thread (or in the worker from the File)"): decoding is the heaviest step for a 12-MP photo, so it moved too.
- The protocol has a third request kind, `pixels`, next to `file` and `bitmap`: it is the pure inner step both reduce to, and it is what lets the unit tests drive the real handler with the reference vectors in Node, where no DOM APIs exist. The page never posts it.
- No request can hang: the worker answers every well-formed message — including its own handler throwing — with a fixed refusal code, and replies are validated strictly.
- Pre-existing e2e race found and worked around in the specs, not the app: after `fill()` the links textarea keeps focus; a click within the 600 ms debounce window (WP40 A7) blurs it, flushing the checks, which renders the checks list and removes the platform question — a reflow that moves the page under the pointer so the click misses the declaration checkbox (a missed click can land on whatever moved there). It reproduces at HEAD (verified with WP42 stashed), and the worker makes it likelier by finishing inside the same window. The specs now wait for the app's own `#url-checks` list and pin `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (like the accessibility spec, which also makes the site's smooth scrolling instant). No assertion was weakened; the product edge is recorded for the owner in `asmita/docs/product/web-audit-progress.md`.
- Also pre-existing on this host and left as found (verified at HEAD, pages untouched by this WP): `forms-focus.spec.ts`'s alert assertion sees Next's own route announcer as a second `role=alert`, and its sign-form checkbox click has the same blur-reflow race on the review-sign page.
- Local footnote: running `npm run build` leaves `.next/types` behind, which makes the repo's plain `tsc --noEmit` fail on generated validator types until `.next` is removed — a repo-wide footgun unrelated to this WP (check.sh never builds); worth a look in a housekeeping pass.
