## Owner changes

### WP26. 24-hour platform deadline (owner decision, 19 November 2026) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22, final run on the committed tree).

**What was built**

- Three analyst labels on a crawler finding — "Looks AI-made", "Sexual content", "Possible minor" — our own simple fields on `CrawlFinding` (`labelAiMade`, `labelSexual`, `labelPossibleMinor`), each with who set it and when (`label*ById`, `label*At`); the possible-minor label also records who cleared it, when and why (`labelPossibleMinorClearedById/At`, `labelPossibleMinorClearReason`) and the review state it came out of (`minorProtocolBefore`). Additive migration `asmita/prisma/migrations/20261119010000_finding_review_labels/` (three enum values — `MINOR_PROTOCOL`, `CRAWL_FINDING_LABEL_SET`, `CRAWL_FINDING_LABEL_CLEARED` — plus the columns); no new table, no policy statement, RLS untouched. Kysely types regenerate from the schema on every install (`prisma-kysely` generator), never hand-written.
- `POST /api/admin/findings/[id]/labels` (`findings:review`, CSRF, strict Zod body `{action, label, reason?}`, rate-limited 200/hour/admin like the review route): one planner (`applyFindingLabel` in `src/lib/crawl-findings.ts`) drives the memory, Kysely and Prisma paths, every write conditional on the state it read — a racing review or label action is `label_conflict`, never a half-applied label. Every change is audited `CRAWL_FINDING_LABEL_SET` / `CRAWL_FINDING_LABEL_CLEARED` like every other finding action; a no-op (already in the wanted state) changes and audits nothing. While `ENABLE_CRAWLER` is off the route refuses 503 `crawler_disabled`, like every finding change.
- **"Possible minor" is a safety switch.** Setting it: the finding leaves the adult queue at once (`reviewStatus` → `MINOR_PROTOCOL`, its earlier state kept in `minorProtocolBefore`), cannot be approved or dismissed (`planReview` refuses `minor_protocol_active`, mapped 409 in the review route), and no representative notice can be prepared or sent (409 `possible_minor_label_set` from `prepareRepresentativeNotice`, whatever the review status; preview and dispatch share the gate, and the page's notice gate shows the block). Clearing it: an admin other than the setter (`second_admin_required`) with a written reason (`reason_required`), stored on the finding and in the audit entry; only then does the finding return to the state it came from. A DISMISSED finding keeps its dismissal (it is out of the adult flow already): the label is recorded on it, and clearing restores nothing.
- UI: `LabelControls` on the finding page (each label's state and when; the possible-minor set and clear both confirm, the clear asks for the required reason; the setter sees the clear disabled with "another admin must clear it"; a cleared label shows when and the reason). The finding page shows the labels on the row; the findings list gains a Labels column, a label filter (`?label=ai-made|sexual|possible-minor` on the page and `GET /api/admin/findings`), and the Minor protocol status chip. `ReviewControls` offers no adult action while the label is set. The interim SafetyNote now says to mark the finding "Possible minor". The list never carries which admin set a label — only whether the viewer is that admin (`currentAdminSetMinorLabel`); the list rows carry no new identity.
- Crawler overview: counts findings carrying each label and how many sit in the minors protocol (memory, Kysely, Prisma), the same address-free aggregate as before.
- Docs: runbook section "Review labels" (what each label is, the safety switch, the second-admin clear); `possible_minor_label_set` in `asmita/docs/product/crawler-notice-flow.md`; a dated note in ADR 006 "Decisions recorded since acceptance".

**Decisions, recorded per the standing rule**

- No minors handling for crawler findings exists yet (the written POCSO procedure is ADR 006 go-live gate 3, still open; root CLAUDE.md's "Minors never reach the case flow" stands). So the `MINOR_PROTOCOL` review state **is** the clearly separate state that marks such a finding for the minors protocol: out of the adult queue, not approvable (so no `SubmittedUrl` reaches her case and nothing downstream of approval can happen), no notice, and the interim rule — tell the SUPER_ADMIN straight away — still applies, on the page and in the runbook. Nothing but a second admin's clear with a reason puts it back into the adult flow. When the written procedure exists it gets its own work package.
- Labels are our own three fields in our own words; no INHOPE or Thorn taxonomy is copied.
- The setter stays recorded on the row (`labelPossibleMinorById`) as the clear's counterpart; the console tells only the setter that they cannot clear it, never another admin's identity.
- WP28 (SSCD lane) is adding a match-method field to findings: the two changes share no column, helper or query key (`?label=` here), so the branches merge cleanly; both touch `findings/page.tsx` additively (a Labels column and filter here, a match-method display there).
- The label filter uses its own query values (`ai-made|sexual|possible-minor`), not the label enum names, so the URL stays stable even if the internal names change.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/finding-labels.test.ts` (19: the memory path and the route — set and clear each label with who and when, no-op audits nothing, the list label filter, and every possible-minor block: the queue move, approval and dismissal refused, an approved finding moved too, a dismissed one labelled without moving, the setter's clear refused, a reason required, a second admin's clear restoring the earlier state and review resuming, a re-set starting the clear record over, and the viewer-only identity flag; the route: 503 crawler-off, CSRF, permission, payload, error codes, 404), `finding-labels-kysely.test.ts` (8: one read and one conditional update per action, the status guard in the write, one hash-chained audit insert, conflict audits nothing, no address column in anything written), `finding-labels-prisma.test.ts` (6, including the review and representative-notice blocks on this path), `finding-labels-migration.test.ts` (8: the schema and migration shape, additive-only statements, no RLS/policy/table).
- Changed only where the contract changed on purpose: `crawl-stats*.test.ts` (label counts on all three backends, the dismissed finding that keeps its dismissal), `admin-crawler-ui.test.ts` (LabelControls rendering and error sentences, the label list and notice gate, the overview counts), `crawler-notice.test.ts` (the `possible_minor_label_set` gate), `crawl-discoveries.test.ts` (its memory fixture gained the new columns). Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened.
- Package totals from the final `check.sh` run on the committed tree (Node 22): `crawler` — untouched by this WP, re-confirmed: lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok. `asmita` — lint 0 errors (4 pre-existing warnings: 3 in an untouched script, 1 in QuickExit), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 156 test files / 1300 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- Migrations are exercised against the schema text, mocks and the memory store only, not applied to a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook open item, unchanged). `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` inside one migration file is fine on PostgreSQL 12+ (Supabase runs 15) and the new enum value is never used within that same migration.
- No e2e journey exercises the label UI (check.sh does not run e2e; the crawler journey's review path is unchanged for unlabelled findings).
- Root `CLAUDE.md` was not updated: a protected agent-instruction file, the write waits for the owner's approval (same decision as WP1–WP3, WP8 and WP19). The runbook, ADR 006 and `crawler-notice-flow.md` carry the change instead.
- `crawler/` itself is untouched — no crawler behaviour changed, so `crawler/README.md` is unchanged; the crawler package was only re-confirmed green.

**Parallel-session note (2026-11-19).** Two sessions shared this lane, as happened on WP8. The WP37 code, tests and docs above are one body of work; the other session made the commit (`d64ee52`) while this session's final `check.sh` was running. This session audited the committed tree file by file against the WP37 definition (it is this session's work, unchanged), verified the working tree is byte-identical to the commit (`git status` clean, empty diff to HEAD) — so the whole final check run tested exactly the committed content, with the asmita test phase starting after the commit landed — and confirmed the branch is one local commit ahead of `origin` and **not pushed**. The full checks this session ran from scratch printed ALL CHECKS PASSED (`crawler`: lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok; `asmita`: lint 0 errors, type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 156 test files / 1300 tests). This note is the only change since.
- `PLATFORM_DEADLINE_HOURS` (`asmita/src/lib/escalation-ladder.ts`) is 24: the survivor's ladder, the admin case work and every test that derives from it now give the platform 24 hours. Its doc comment states both facts: Rule 3(2)(b) sets 2 hours (G.S.R. 120(E), in force 20.02.2026; PRD Section 4); 24 hours is the period our notices ask for.
- `asmita/prisma/template-seeds.ts`: every sentence in which a notice asked the platform to act within 2 hours now asks for 24 hours, phrased as Asmita's request and never attributed to the Rule. Changed sentences: the IT_RULES_2021 subject line (twice: `subjectTemplate` and the body's `Subject:` line), demand (i) of IT_RULES_2021, demand (i) of IT_RULES_AND_DMCA, and item 4 of CRAWL_REPRESENTATIVE_NOTICE. Every sentence that states what the Rule requires (the legal-basis paragraphs, the SOP note, the hash advisory's 5.2, the L1 cover's item 2, the FIR package's item 4) keeps the two-hour period, and every `legalCitations` entry is unchanged — no law is misstated and no citation added. The changed sentences are listed before -> after in the commit message for the owner's approval; the seed logic that resets `reviewedByLegal` when text changes is untouched, and nothing sets `reviewedByLegal`.
- Survivor-facing copy that tells her when the platform must act already said 24 hours everywhere (`home.blockA.*`, `hiw.tl1.body`, `faq.s2.a4` in en.json and their hi.json counterparts, the public home page, resources, chat knowledge, the L2 email), and the ladder's deadline step now computes 24 hours from the constant. No en.json or hi.json value needed a change, so `hi-review-status.json` is untouched and nothing new is machine translated.
- `asmita/src/app/(public)/for-platforms/page.tsx` does not exist on this branch; there was nothing to leave alone.
- `asmita/src/lib/escalation-engine.ts`: comment-only update — the L1 cover states the Rule's two-hour deadline, which the representative notice does not demand (it asks for twenty-four hours), so the exclusion rationale still holds and is now worded for the new text.
- Docs: `asmita/docs/product/escalation-ladder.md` (step 2 is 24h; the Deadlines section states the ask and the Rule's period separately; a WP26 sentence in the Notice redraft section), `asmita/docs/product/crawler-notice-flow.md` (the two stale two-hour sentences), `asmita/docs/legal/notice-redraft-2026-11.md` (a dated **Update 2026-11-19** note: the changed sentences reset `reviewedByLegal` on the next seed and are listed before -> after in the commit for re-review), and the legal pack `asmita/docs/legal/Asmita-Legal-Notices-2026-11.docx` regenerated from the changed templates with `node scripts/generate-notice-pack.mjs` (Node 22 helper), as that memo requires after any template change.

**Decisions, recorded per the standing rule**

- The survivor's copyable appeal wording `ladder.draft.appeal` (en.json and hi.json) keeps "a platform must take all reasonable and practicable measures to remove it within two hours": it is a statement of what Rule 3(2)(b) requires, the legal reviewer confirmed that exact wording on 2026-11-17, and restating the Rule at 24 hours would misstate the law to her and to the Grievance Appellate Committee. The survivor-facing bullet is met by the deadline copy, which says 24 hours; the appeal states the law, like the for-platforms page the WP shields.
- The IT_RULES_2021 subject line changed to "within twenty-four hours": the subject states the relief the complaint seeks, so it must match the demand; the Rule's own two-hour period is stated in the legal-basis section the subject sits over.
- In the two demand items, "as Rule 3(2)(b) of the IT Rules requires" became "as Asmita requests (Rule 3(2)(b) of the IT Rules requires it within two hours)": keeping the attribution to the Rule would misstate it (it sets two hours, not twenty-four), and dropping the citation would weaken a legal demand; the parenthetical keeps the citation and both periods correctly attributed. The joint notice's forty-eight-hour parenthetical is unchanged (still true against both periods).
- The L1 follow-up cover, the FIR package and the hash advisory are unchanged: all their two-hour sentences state what the Rule requires, and none of them asks the platform to act within two hours. The L1 still fires at 24 hours — now exactly when the asked period ends; the engine's own schedule (L1 24h, L2 48h, L3 7 days) was never computed from `PLATFORM_DEADLINE_HOURS` and is unchanged.
- No crawler behaviour changed, so `crawler/README.md` and the runbook are untouched; root `CLAUDE.md` contains no deadline statement and was not edited.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- `tests/unit/notice-template-seeds.test.ts`: the "Indian templates demand removal within two hours" test now asserts the split — statements of the Rule keep "TWO (2) HOURS" in IT_RULES_2021, IT_RULES_AND_DMCA, HASH_ADVISORY and ESCALATION_L1; the two demands ask "within TWENTY-FOUR (24) HOURS of receipt of this complaint, as Asmita requests"; a negative regex proves the 24 hours are never attributed to the Rule; the CRAWL_REPRESENTATIVE_NOTICE assertions pin the new ask sentence and the Rule's two-hour statement. Each changed assertion carries a WP26 note saying why (owner decision; the law keeps its true period).
- `tests/unit/escalation-ladder.test.ts` and `tests/unit/escalation-ladder-view.test.ts`: notes only — both assert through the constant, so they move with it; the view test's "two-hour deadline" comment now says 24-hour. `tests/unit/admin-cases.test.ts` needed no change (it derives from the constant).
- Package totals from the final `check.sh` run on the committed tree (Node 22): `crawler` — lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok (untouched by this WP, re-confirmed green). `asmita` — lint 0 errors (the 3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script, no new ones), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 152 test files / 1250 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- The PDF copy of the legal pack was not regenerated: no docx-to-pdf converter exists on this host, and the 2026-11-17 regeneration updated the docx only, so the PDF already lagged one review cycle. The docx is the artifact counsel reads, per the memo that requires regeneration.
- `reviewedByLegal` is not set anywhere, so nothing can be sent from the changed templates until the legal reviewer records the review on each row in production. That is the owner's and counsel's step, deliberately outside this WP.
- The regenerated docx was verified by extracting its text (all five changed sentences present) but not opened in a Word processor.

### WP29. Bring the legal pages up to date with every new feature — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22, final run on the committed tree). Owner decision, 19 November 2026 (Saquib). Counsel must review every paragraph listed below before the pages are published; both pages stay marked "Draft pending legal review" and no `reviewedByLegal` was set anywhere.

Every other public page was searched for statements about what Asmita collects, keeps or shares (home, about, what-we-do, how-it-works, faq, check-image, research, contact, talk, resources): none makes a claim the eight WP29 features make false, so none was changed. No tick box, consent template, notice template or crawler code was touched; `prisma/template-seeds.ts` is untouched.

**What was written — every new or changed paragraph, for owner and counsel approval**

Privacy policy (all values are English in `en.json`; see the English-only decision below for `hi.json`):

- Edited: `privacy.hero.version` — "Last updated · 19 November 2026 · Version 1.1" (was 17 May 2026, Version 1.0).
- Edited: `privacy.tldr.p1.detail` (the "We never see your content" promise) — now says the app never opens a link you submit, fingerprints are computed on your device, and when you ask for the web search a separate Asmita service visits public websites and compares what it sees with your codes, keeping nothing but the matches, which a person reviews.
- Edited: `privacy.s01.p1` (scope) — now also covers the crawler and the information platform staff give when they join the partner programme.
- Edited: `privacy.s03.intro` — "five categories" → "six categories".
- Edited: `privacy.s03.i2.detail` (case data) — fingerprints are image codes (PDQ) or video codes (vPDQ), generated on your device; the images and the video are never sent.
- New: `privacy.s03.i5` — "Web-search and watch data." — what the search stores (encrypted found addresses, match records, the one-tick authorisation; purged 30 days after first seen; site names of confirmed copies on the case page).
- New: `privacy.s03.i6` — "Partner programme contacts." — work contact details of platform staff, encrypted at rest.
- Edited: `privacy.s04.i1.detail` ("The intimate content itself") — never receive or store your image or video; the deeper AI check exception; the crawler holds public pages and images in memory only for the moment it takes to fingerprint each one and then throws them away; nothing is written to its disk.
- Edited: `privacy.s05.i2b` — retitled "Fingerprint codes -"; purposes extended: notices, the web search when you ask for it, and partner platforms only under your separate tick.
- New: `privacy.s05.i5` — "Web-search and watch data -" (find copies, check removed content stays down, alert you).
- New: `privacy.s05.i6` — "Partner programme contacts -" (verify and run the programme).
- Edited: `privacy.s07.i1` — appended: found addresses are purged on the same 30-day schedule as reported links.
- New: `privacy.s07.i6` — "Fingerprints (images and videos):" — kept with your case; on account deletion kept detached from your identity so platforms can keep blocking, a video's per-second list deleted; stopping a watch deletes the fingerprints it covers.
- New: `privacy.s07.i7` — "Found copies and web-search records:" — 30-day purge; the signed authorisation kept as a record; site names never linked to you.
- New: `privacy.s07.i8` — "Partner programme contacts:" — kept while the programme may need to reach that platform; Grievance Officer deletes on request. No period invented (none exists in code or spec).
- Edited: `privacy.s08.i1` (platforms) — appended: every notice asks the platform to act within 24 hours, alongside the timelines the law itself sets.
- New: `privacy.s08.i6` — "Partner platforms, only with your separate tick." — only the fingerprint code and a case reference, plus the links from your case on that partner's own platform; never your image, video or name; partners must delete on withdrawal.
- New: `privacy.s09.i7` — "Crawler isolation." — separate service, nothing written to disk, images fingerprinted in an isolated child process and thrown away, logs carry counts only.
- Edited: `privacy.s10.i3` (right to erasure) — appended the exception: fingerprint codes are kept, detached from you, so platforms can keep blocking.
- Edited: `privacy.s13.p1` — "All personal data is stored on servers physically located in India" narrowed to the truth: account, case, notice and fingerprint records are stored encrypted on servers in India.
- Edited: `privacy.s13.p2` (transfers) — the two cross-border exceptions: a notice you ask us to send to a platform outside India; and, when you have asked for the web search, your fingerprint codes held in the crawler's memory for each pass (up to 50 minutes, hourly) because the crawler runs on Cloudflare's container platform, which cannot place a container in India — nothing is written there, every stored record stays in Mumbai.
- New section `#fingerprints` ("Your image fingerprints") — `privacy.fp.p1` + 5 cards (what we collect, why, where processed, how long, who shared with) + a callout that a rough, blurry likeness can sometimes be reconstructed from a PDQ hash (already stated to platforms in the notice templates and in the FAQ), which is why fingerprints are encrypted.
- New section `#video` ("Video fingerprints") — `privacy.video.p1` + 5 cards + `privacy.video.p2`: vPDQ, one code per sampled second made on her device, at most 1,000 codes, the video never leaves her device ("not one frame of it is ever sent"); on account deletion the entry is kept detached and its per-second list is deleted; browsers cannot read every format and nothing is sent if hers cannot be read.
- New section `#crawler` ("How Asmita searches the web") — `privacy.crawl.p1`/`p2` + 5 cards + callout: what it fetches (public pages and images, held in memory only, thrown away after fingerprinting, nothing written to disk, logs carry counts only, non-matches never reported); what it stores (encrypted match records purged 30 days after first seen, encrypted page addresses still to visit, site names never linked to you; confirmed copies appear by website name only and in the export); the one-tick authorisation (covers comparison and representative notices); representative notices sent by Asmita in its own name, never her signature, same verified-contact and legal-review gates, 24-hour ask; withdrawal (fingerprints leave the pool at once, unreviewed matches deleted, unsent copies stopped for good, search stops within about an hour, sent notices cannot be recalled, account deletion withdraws every case); where it runs (Cloudflare Containers, hourly, ≤ 50-minute pass, pool in memory outside India, records and keys in Mumbai).
- New section `#watch` ("Watch for copies, and checks that content stays down") — `privacy.watch.p1` + 3 cards + `privacy.watch.p2`: watch without filing a complaint (no links collected, nothing sent to any platform unless you choose, alert email carries only the case reference, page shows the site name, "take it down" converts and asks for the full authorisation, stopping deletes your fingerprints); removal checks (revisited about one, three and seven days after a link is marked removed, check record holds no address, with fingerprints and a live authorisation it can tell whether your image is still on the page, otherwise only whether the page is up, three clean checks mark it verified removed, if back: notice-sent state, follow-ups restart, email reference-only); the 12-month re-upload watch after a case is resolved (under the authorisation already signed, no new permission, stop watching withdraws it).
- New section `#partner-sharing` ("Sharing fingerprints with partner platforms") — `privacy.pshare.p1` + 5 cards: separate tick; what leaves Asmita (only the fingerprint code, a case reference, and the links from your case on that partner's own platform); what partners must agree to (find/remove/block only, never identify or profile, secure, human review of every match, delete on withdrawal); how to withdraw; what partners do not get (nothing beyond a signed notice; notices to a partner also go to its agreed channel; only with the platform's written permission are its pages watched by the search).
- New section `#sscd` ("A second matching layer (SSCD)") — `privacy.sscd.p1` + 5 cards: an encrypted numerical description (an embedding) computed from a confirmed copy the search found, never from her original; finds crops, filters, re-encodes; deleted with her fingerprint, authorisation or case; never leaves Asmita, never sent to platforms, not even under the partner tick; and the layer is off until switched on — nothing changes while it is off.
- New section `#platform-partners` ("Platform partner sign-ups") — `privacy.ppartners.p1` + 4 cards: what is collected (organisation, platform, domain, kind, audience size, interests, contact name, role, work email, optional phone and message; name, email, phone and message encrypted at rest); why (verify the sign-up, agree the programme, run it); kept while the programme may need to reach them, Grievance Officer deletes on request; shared with Asmita staff running the programme only, and a sign-up never causes survivor data to be shared.

Terms of use (inline English text on the page):

- Edited: hero stamp — "Last updated · 19 November 2026 · Version 0.5 · Draft pending legal review" (was 17 May 2026, Version 0.4).
- Edited: how-a-case-works, first paragraph — fingerprints of your images or videos, generated on your device, only the code reaches Asmita.

**Decisions, recorded per the standing rule**

- English only. The owner's instruction keeps these pages English only. The privacy copy lives in i18n, and the June 2026 pass (commit 03eb3df) had already translated the sixteen original sections to Hindi — so "English only" was applied to this WP's own text: every new and every edited key carries the English text in `hi.json` too, and the Hindi page shows the new sections in English. The date stamp `privacy.hero.version` keeps its Hindi label with the new date. `hi-review-status.json` records this under `excludedFromTranslation`. A later Hindi pass can translate the WP29 keys; `tests/unit/legal-pages.test.ts` derives the English-only key set (en value equals hi value) and says so in its name, so a translation pass updates that test on purpose.
- Version bumps: privacy 1.0 → 1.1, terms 0.4 → 0.5. The WP fixes only the "Last updated" date; the versions were bumped because both policies changed materially, and both stamps keep their existing wording otherwise (the terms stamp keeps "Draft pending legal review").
- "Fingerprints of found copies" in the WP's crawler list is the SSCD embedding (WP28, not yet built): the crawler's default storage is match records and encrypted addresses, and the embedding is described in the `#sscd` section as off until switched on. No fingerprint of a found copy is stored while SSCD is off.
- The 24-hour ask is written as the owner worded it — notices ask platforms to act within 24 hours, alongside the law's own timelines. The templates themselves demand removal within the statutory window (two hours under Rule 3(2)(b) as amended in 2026, per `prisma/template-seeds.ts`) and acknowledgement within 24 hours; the pages do not restate those numbers beyond "the timelines the law itself sets", so no citation question is opened.
- No retention period was invented for partner-programme contacts (none exists in code or spec): the pages say they are kept while the programme may need to reach the platform and that the Grievance Officer deletes a sign-up on request.
- Watch, removal checks, partner sharing and platform partner sign-ups are specified on `origin/feat/watch-verify`, `origin/feat/watch-w1`, `origin/feat/watch-w2`, `origin/feat/partner-sharing` and `origin/feat/platform-partners`, and are not yet merged into master; SSCD is designed but not built (WP28 waits on video Phase 2). The pages describe these features as specified, as the owner asked; the SSCD section says the layer is off, and counsel should read those sections with particular care because they describe not-yet-live behaviour.
- The FAQ answer `faq.s1.a2` ("The link itself never reaches our server") predates WP29 and is inconsistent with the policy's own statement that submitted URLs are stored as text for 30 days. None of the eight WP29 features makes it false, so it was left untouched (out of this WP's scope), and it is flagged here for the owner.
- `asmita/docs/ops/crawler-runbook.md`, `crawler/README.md` and root `CLAUDE.md` are unchanged: no crawler behaviour, config or migration changed in this WP, and root `CLAUDE.md` stays a protected agent-instruction file (same decision as WP17-WP19).

## WP52 (Lab lane) — E1 benchmark harness, tools/lab-bench — 2026-11-20

WP52 is not part of the crawler v2 spec: it is a Meri Asmita AI Lab work
package (owner-approved 20 November 2026; charter committed as
`asmita/docs/lab/charter.md`). This entry is the pointer the work package
asked for; the full record — every decision, every verification — lives in
`asmita/docs/lab/progress.md`.

**What was built.** A standalone Node 22 + TypeScript package at
`tools/lab-bench/` (own `package.json` + lockfile, vitest, `npm run check` =
type-check plus tests), implementing experiment E1 of the Lab charter:

- `build-set <portraits-dir> <out-dir>` makes the charter's variants per
  portrait — untouched original, crop, resize, mirror, sticker overlay,
  screenshot with borders, WhatsApp-style re-encode (long edge 1600, JPEG
  quality about 70), an eye-aligned face-swap composite (colour-matched
  patch through a feathered elliptical mask, CPU only) and that composite
  through the WhatsApp re-encode (the stop-rule combination); a labelled
  `real/` + `fake/` deepfake-dataset folder is imported as-is. Manifest:
  ids, transforms, labels — nothing about a person.
- `match <set-dir>` scores whole-image PDQ (`asmita/src/lib/pdq/pdq.ts`
  imported, never copied; decode via a faithful copy of the crawler's
  `prepare.ts` recipe; production quality floor 50), the pinned
  `sscd_disc_mixup` ONNX (loading approach copied from
  `origin/feat/crawler-sscd`'s `sscd-pool.ts`; that branch is NOT merged),
  face-crop PDQ and background-masked PDQ — recall at a 1% false-match rate
  per transform.
- `detect <set-dir>` runs the charter's detector adapters — OpenAI vision,
  Hive, Sightengine, and the Community Forensics ViT-S/16 384 (MIT, arXiv
  2411.04125) as the local onnxruntime-node model — skipping any without a
  key or weights; accuracy and AUC per transform.
- `report <run-dir>` writes local HTML/Markdown. `data/`, `runs/` and
  `models/` are gitignored; `npm run models:fetch` downloads the two pinned
  ONNX weights with sha256 checks; tests use tiny synthetic images and
  invented `example.test` fixtures only. `asmita/docs/lab/e1.md` carries
  the method and an empty results table for the real run.

**Checks (all run on Node 22, final run on the committed tree).**
`tools/lab-bench`: tsc clean, 11 test files / 82 tests. Crawler: lint,
type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build. Asmita: lint 0 errors,
type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 152 test files / 1250 tests.
`check.sh` printed ALL CHECKS PASSED. No crawler or asmita source changed.

**Not done or not verified.** No real E1 run happened: there is no
portraits folder of Indian faces on this machine, so `e1.md`'s tables stay
empty for the owner's real run. The hosted detector adapters are written
from the providers' public API docs and tested only against invented
fixtures with an injected fetch (no keys; the first keyed run must
spot-check the recorded raw responses). `crawler/README.md` and the runbook
are untouched (no crawler behaviour change).

**Deviations.** None from the crawler spec (this WP sits outside it, and
nothing in `asmita/` or `crawler/` changed). The complaint workflow's
no-fetch lint rule and `no-url-fetch` test were not edited: asmita's ESLint
runs from `asmita/` and does not cover `tools/`, so no narrowing was
needed for this work package — recorded as a decision in
`asmita/docs/lab/progress.md`, where the charter's narrowing becomes live
with the first lab WP that adds `src/lib/lab/**` or `src/app/**/lab/**`.
## Thorn-inspired

Owner decisions, 19 November 2026 (Saquib), from research into Thorn's published work. Copy no Thorn text or code except where a licence is stated; everything below is written in our own words. Thorn's research is cited by name and link only.

### WP35. Wording on the "Is this image AI-made?" page (app) — 2026-11-19
- New shared module `asmita/src/lib/crawl-page-key.ts` (dependency-free; the crawler imports it read-only like `asmita/src/lib/pdq/pdq.ts` and esbuild inlines it into `dist/main.js`): the page-URL identity both sides now share. `http`/`https`, one leading `www.` label, a trailing slash on a non-root path and a final `/index.html`, `/index.htm` or `/index.php` are one page; tracking parameters are dropped and the rest sorted; a non-default port stays distinct (another server); the root keeps its `/`. The crawler's `urlKey`/`queryKey` (`crawler/src/crawl/url.ts`) and the app's `crawlPageKey` (`asmita/src/lib/crawl-pages.ts`, the digest under every `CrawlPage` row) both delegate to it, so a page the two sides spell differently is stored and fetched once. Addresses keep their real form everywhere — the key is identity only, never an address that is fetched, reported or logged — and existing frontier rows keep the digest they were stored with (the digest is computed once, at insert; a same-page duplicate against an old-rule row is accepted until that row ages out).
- "Fetch the canonical form": within one extraction (a page's link buckets, a sitemap or feed document) the first spelling seen is kept and an `https` spelling upgrades an `http` one; across pages the app's digest keeps whichever spelling was reported first. `extract`'s own dedup and the sitemap/feed `xmlLinks` path share the rule.
- `<link rel="canonical">` on fetched HTML (static, the AUTO fallback and the rendered DOM): collected by `extract` as metadata — never links, collected on nofollow pages too, deduplicated by key, capped at `MAX_CANONICAL_PER_PAGE` (20, the report contract's limit) — and `crawler.ts` reports the targets that are in scope and not obvious non-content as the new optional `aliases` field of a reported page (max 20, deduplicated by key). A canonical address is never fetched just because it is canonical; a self-referencing canonical is no alias; an off-scope canonical is neither fetched nor reported as a discovery.
- App side, `reportCrawlPages` on the memory, Kysely (Worker) and Prisma paths, aligned: the aliases are applied before the links — every live row carrying an alias's digest is marked `DEAD` (never claimed again), and an alias with no row gets a digest-only `DEAD` "tombstone" row whose unique `(sourceId, urlDigest)` swallows any later link to the same page (a link arriving in the same report included). A link and its alias never coexist as live rows. Alias addresses are validated exactly like links (in scope, no credentials, no non-default port), stored encrypted, never fetched, never audited, never logged; tombstones count against the same per-source frontier cap, after real links; a retried report is idempotent (kills and tombstones cannot resurrect or duplicate). `pageRows` now also reads the claimed rows' digests for the self-alias check.
- Contract: `POST /api/crawler/frontier/report` (route + `frontierReportPageSchema`) and the crawler client's `ReportedPageSchema` gained the optional strict `aliases` field (string array, max 20); a report entry carrying more than 20 aliases is skipped whole, like a >500-links entry. The test mock gained the same rules plus a `reportedAliases` record.
- Build: the Docker build stage and `crawler/Dockerfile.dockerignore` now copy/allow `asmita/src/lib/crawl-page-key.ts` next to `pdq.ts`; `crawler/tsconfig.json` includes it. Verified: `docker build --target build` succeeds and `dist/main.js` carries the shared module; the runtime stages are unchanged.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run flow, the Real-addresses row, the bounded-frontier row, the frontier/report contract), the runbook's new "Counting the same page once (crawler v3 WP10)" paragraph, this entry. No new config knob (the caps are fixed by the spec), no schema change, no migration.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New crawler tests: `tests/unit/page-key-contract.test.ts` (6: `urlKey` equals the shared module's key over every equivalence pair and every near-miss; all forms of one page collapse to one key; distinct pages stay distinct; the root's index document; query strings kept sorted and tracking-free; image candidates share the rule), `tests/integration/canonical.test.ts` (5, over a new `buildCanonicalFixture()` in `tests/support/fixture-site.ts` — a home page linking one target in four forms, a canonical page whose target has a live row, a late twin, a self-canonical and an off-scope canonical): the target is fetched exactly once per run in the first form seen and the www host never contacted; the https twin linked after the row exists changes nothing; the killed live row and the tombstoned late twin are never fetched again; the self-canonical page stays alive; the off-scope canonical host is never contacted and never a discovery; the report contract holds (no bad request, body within 256 KiB) and the log scan stays clean. Three new unit cases in `tests/unit/url-robots-extract.test.ts` (four forms one link, https preferred, canonical collection on nofollow pages, dedup and the cap); its existing dedup-key expectations now expect the normalised `https` key (the behaviour the WP changes on purpose — rule 6, also in the commit message).
- New app tests: 11 in `tests/unit/crawl-frontier.test.ts` (every equivalence pair digests to one frontier row through the real claim/report path; distinct near-misses; agreement with the shared module; the root index document; a legacy-digest row keeps its digest while the new-rule link of the same page is a second row; the alias kill; the tombstone swallowing its own report's link; a later link to a tombstone never live; per-source digest isolation; self-, off-scope, port, credential and malformed aliases refused; an alias of a dead row ignored; a >20-alias entry skipped whole by the strict schema), 2 in `tests/unit/crawl-frontier-kysely.test.ts` (the kill update by digest `IN` and live-only, the tombstone insert `DEAD`, never a live alias row, the digest read for the self-alias check) and 2 in `tests/unit/crawl-frontier-prisma.test.ts` (the `updateMany` kill, the `createMany` tombstone with `skipDuplicates`, the self-alias no-op).
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 32 test files / 371 tests all passed (30 / 357 before this WP), build ok. `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 143 test files / 1188 tests all passed (1173 before this WP, same file count: the new tests live in existing files).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval, which did not come for WP1–WP9 either (silence is not consent). Intended row addition (apply on approval): in the architecture list's crawler sentence, after the frontier routes — "crawler v3 WP10: the same page spelled `http`/`https`, `www.`/bare, with or without a trailing slash or a final `/index.*` is one frontier row (one shared page-key module both sides run, existing rows keep their stored digest), and a fetched page's in-scope `<link rel="canonical">` target is reported as an alias the app kills or tombstones, so the same content is never fetched twice and a canonical address is never fetched just for being canonical". `crawler/README.md` and the runbook document all of it instead.
- The Docker image's full multi-stage build (deps + runtime with Chromium) was not rerun; the stage the WP touches was: `docker build --target build` succeeds and the produced `dist/main.js` contains the shared module (verified in the image). The runtime stages are unchanged in their inputs from WP9's image.
- The Kysely (Worker) and Prisma paths are proven against the recording fake and the mocked client, not a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook open item, unchanged). The alias `UPDATE ... WHERE urlDigest IN (...)` has therefore compiled and been recorded, not executed on Postgres.
- No real site was crawled (rule 7); the Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) and CI on Linux were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged).
- Environment, not code: five lane sessions share one Node-22 container lock on this host, so the full check spent most of its wall-clock queued behind other lanes; every phase that ran, passed.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "Fetch the canonical form (prefer https, keep the host form seen first)" is implemented as a dedup identity plus a first-form-kept/https-upgrade rule, not by rewriting the address the crawler fetches: forcing `https` on a site that only serves `http`, or re-hosting to the bare domain, would synthesise addresses the site never published and could break signed URLs, while the count-once goal is delivered entirely by the shared key (one row, one fetch — whichever spelling arrived first, upgraded to https only when the same page or document offers both). The stored and reported address stays exactly what the site wrote, which the "Real addresses" guarantee promises.
- The `www.`/bare merge is unconditional in the key, which is the spec's "only when both are in scope" read through the existing safety order: both sides refuse an off-scope address before any key is computed (the app's `acceptableLink`/`pageOnSourceHost`, the crawler's `inScope`), so two spellings can share a key only when both are in scope. Side effect, on purpose: an image candidate's key also merges a CDN's `www.`/bare spellings of one image, so it is hashed once instead of twice.
- "The app does not fetch it again" for a canonical alias is implemented as kill-plus-tombstone: the live row is marked `DEAD` and never claimed, and when no row exists a digest-only `DEAD` row is stored so a later link (same report or later) can never create a live one. A tombstone is a normal dead row — it leaves with the 30-day retention, and after it ages out a genuinely new link to the same page may create a live row again, exactly like any other dead page; unbounded suppression would need a second table for no coverage gain.
- A report entry with more than 20 aliases is skipped whole (`accepted` excludes it, the route answers 200), not refused with a 400: the frontier report validates per entry by design (WP3) so one bad page entry can never lose the batch, and a >500-links entry is already handled the same way. Strict Zod on both sides is what the spec asks for; the crawler's own cap (`MAX_REPORT_ALIASES_PER_PAGE`) never sends more.
- The canonical collection is not fed through the query-variant guard or the non-content skip at extraction time (those govern links); the alias filter (`inScope`, `isNonContentUrl`, dedup, cap 20) runs in `crawler.ts` at report time, so a hostile `<link rel="canonical">` flood costs at most 20 stored strings and no fetches.
- The `url-robots-extract.test.ts` dedup-key expectation changed from `http://example.com/p?a=1&b=2` to `https://example.com/p?a=1&b=2`: the key's scheme normalisation is this WP's own change (rule 6 requires the reason, which is also in the commit message); the assertion still proves the same property (tracking dropped, rest sorted, address untouched).

(2025)") and a "Read the survey" link carries the URL — the site's established external-link pattern (`target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, as on /get-help). ## Web best-practice audit

Work packages WP39–WP43 come from a web best-practice audit of the site (accessibility, forms, performance, security headers), owner-approved on 20 November 2026 (Saquib). They are defined by the audit brief, not by this spec; this spec's "Rules that do not change" and "How to work" still apply, together with the audit's own rules. The per-WP audit log (what changed, what was not verified, what needs the owner's approval) lives in `asmita/docs/product/web-audit-progress.md`.

### WP43. Accessibility of the "Is this image AI-made?" page (app) — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22, run on the finished code tree; the only edits after the run were these progress-log lines, which no check reads).

**What was built**

- A5 — the states of the on-device check on `/check-image` (`src/app/(public)/check-image/ImageChecker.tsx`) are announced. The "Reading credentials on your device..." line is a `role="status"` paragraph (polite), the result is announced by moving focus to its heading, and the error card is a `role="alert"`. For the result the WP offered a `role="status"` wrapper or a focused heading; the focused heading was chosen — the pattern `EligibilityWizard`, `HomeChat` and `CasePeek` already use — because it reads one line instead of politely replaying the whole result card. "What we found" therefore became a real `<h2>` (it was a `<span>` carrying the same classes): same classes, same look, but a focusable (`tabIndex={-1}`, `outline-none`) heading a screen reader can also jump to by heading navigation. Focus moves there on every completion, including a second image picked after a first result.
- A4 — every error message on the page is a `role="alert"`: the "We could not read this file" card and the deeper check's "could not be completed right now" line.
- A6 — the hidden file input is never a tab stop any more (`tabIndex={-1}` at all times; it was `{ageConfirmed ? 0 : -1}`, so after the age gate it sat in the tab order beside the visible button) and now carries an accessible name: `aria-label` from the existing `check.upload.label` ("Choose an image to check"), the same pattern as `SubmitForm`'s hidden picker. The visible "Choose image" button is the only tab stop. `aria-hidden={!ageConfirmed}` stays: the input is disabled while the gate is up.
- The deeper check's states (WP38's addition) got the same treatment: its "Checking with OpenAI..." line is `role="status"`, its error line is `role="alert"`, and its result title receives focus when it arrives (its button unmounts on completion, so focus would otherwise fall back to the page).
- WP35 and WP38 wording is untouched: no `en.json`/`hi.json` value changed, no new key was added, so `hi-review-status.json` is unchanged too.

**Decisions, recorded per the standing rule**

- The result is announced by focus, not a live-region wrapper: a `role="status"` region around the result would politely read out the whole card (C2PA details, metadata list, technical-details entries) on every check; the focused heading reads one line and leaves the rest to browse. The WP allowed either mechanism.
- The input's accessible name reuses `check.upload.label`: it is exactly the right name for the picker, it already exists in both languages, and the audit's "every new user-facing string goes in en.json AND hi.json" rule then adds nothing.
- `/check-image` was not added to the axe route list in `tests/e2e/accessibility.spec.ts`: that list change belongs to WP39's lane (its brief names those routes); duplicating it here would collide when the audit lanes merge.
- The deeper check got the same announcements although the brief names the page's main states: it is part of "whatever WP38 added", and its result otherwise arrives unannounced.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New `tests/unit/check-image-a11y.test.ts` — 8 tests: the hidden input carries its accessible name, `tabindex="-1"` and `sr-only` and stays disabled behind the age gate while the visible button renders (and the old conditional `tabIndex` is gone from the source); the label exists in both languages; the two checking lines are `role="status"`, both error messages are `role="alert"`, the result heading is the focused `<h2>`, and both focus effects are wired. The conditional states are pinned on the component source because `renderToStaticMarkup` reaches only the idle state — the same pinning style `admin-crawler-ui.test.ts` uses.
- New `tests/e2e/check-image-a11y.spec.ts` (run on the chromium and mobile projects, both passing): the hidden input has `tabindex="-1"` and the `aria-label`; after a synthetic image is picked, focus lands on the "What we found" heading; after focus is deliberately moved off it, a second pick announces again. Run before the commit through the Node 22 container against a throwaway Postgres 17 (2 tests, 20.8 s); the full Playwright suite remains the separate verify step.
- The existing `/check-image` assertions in `hash-flow.spec.ts` (age-gated picker) are untouched and still hold: one file input, disabled until the age tick, enabled after.
- Package totals from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22): `crawler` — lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok (no `crawler/` source touched; re-confirmed green). `asmita` — lint 0 errors (the 4 pre-existing warnings, all in untouched files, no new ones), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 157 test files / 1309 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- No real screen reader was run. The announcements use the standard mechanisms (`role="status"`, `role="alert"`, programmatic focus to a heading) and are asserted structurally (unit) and by focus (e2e).
- The deeper check's announcements cannot be reached in e2e: the harness sets no OpenAI key, so the page shows "temporarily unavailable" and the deeper check's states never run there. They are pinned by the unit tests only.
- The "We could not read this file" card is defensive: both on-device readers resolve instead of throwing (`readImageProvenance` catches internally, `readC2paCredentials` resolves to "unavailable"), so no real pick reaches it; its `role="alert"` is pinned by the unit test, not exercised by a live run.
- The new e2e spec was run alone (both projects), not with the whole Playwright suite; that full run is the audit's separate verify step.
- No crawler behaviour, config or migration changed, so `crawler/README.md` and the runbook are untouched. Root `CLAUDE.md` was not updated (protected agent-instruction file; as for WP1–WP3 and WP8 the write waits for approval).

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- None beyond the decisions above: every change sits inside the WP's named page (check-image and what WP38 added to it), and nothing outside that scope was touched.
- App: `CrawlPage.priority` (additive migration `20261119060000_crawl_page_priority`: `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 100`, plus the index `(sourceId, priority, nextDueAt)` the claim's new order reads; existing rows keep 100). Every claim now hands out the due rows of a source `ORDER BY priority, nextDueAt` — the memory path's sort, the Kysely (Worker) path's `orderBy` chain and the Prisma path's raw claim all changed in one shape, so two crawler instances still never share a URL and the 30-minute claim window is untouched. `linkPriority` (exported, tested) is the report-to-row rule: sitemap 10, feed 20, pagination 30, other pages 50 plus 10 per depth level, capped at the default 100; the seed row keeps 100 (the app's own, not from a report). The report's link entries gained two optional strict fields on both sides: `pagination` (a flag the crawler sets on `rel="next"` and its siblings) and `lastmod` (a sitemap `<lastmod>` as a strict ISO date) — a link's kind/flag/depth sets the priority of the row it stores, and a live row whose reported lastmod is newer than its last fetch is bumped to priority 5 and made due immediately (`FRONTIER_PRIORITY_LASTMOD`, one chunked digest read + one chunked update per report, status-guarded so a concurrent kill stays final). A row never fetched, a dead row and a row the very same report fetched are never jumped; a claimed row keeps its claim and becomes due when the window expires. Prisma, Kysely and memory paths aligned; Kysely table types regenerate from the schema on install as before.
- Crawler: `parseSitemap` collects `<lastmod>` for urlset `<url>` entries and sitemap-index children, normalising to strict ISO UTC (`isoLastmod`: a full ISO date-time with offset or Z, or an ISO date; partial formats and junk are quietly dropped, never guessed — so what the parser produces always passes the report contract, tested). `xmlLinks` carries the lastmod on the links it reports (and keeps a previously reported one through the WP10 https upgrade); `collectLinks` flags pagination links with `pagination: true`; robots.txt `Sitemap:` stash links and feed entries carry no lastmod (the spec names sitemap links; a feed's pubDate/updated are publication dates, not modification times). The crawler stays priority-blind: it reports facts, the app owns the policy — the schemas accept no `priority` from the wire.
- Test support: the mock API mirrors the app's WP11 rules on purpose (priorities on stored rows, claim order, the lastmod jump and its refusals) and its strict server schema accepts the new fields; new `buildPriorityFixture()` (synthetic pages only, no images, hosts disjoint from every other fixture) serves the robots-named sitemap with a newer-than-any-fetch and an old date-only lastmod, a pagination link, a feed and plain pages.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run flow, the frontier paragraph, the claim/report contract bullets), the runbook's new "Crawl the useful pages first (crawler v3 WP11)" paragraph, this entry. No new config knob (the priorities are fixed by the spec), no `.env.example` change, no admin-UI or stats change (the dashboard's queue buckets count statuses, which WP11 does not touch).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New crawler tests: `tests/unit/report-contract.test.ts` (3: the client schema accepts the pagination flag and the strict-ISO lastmod, keeps them optional, and refuses what the app's strict Zod refuses — a non-boolean flag, a non-ISO or offset-less date, `lastmod: null`, a client-sent `priority`, unknown fields), `tests/integration/priority.test.ts` (5, over the new fixture: run 1 fetches in priority order — seed, sitemap 10, feed 20, pagination 30, plain page 60, then the depth-2 rows; the report carries the pagination flag and both normalised lastmods; the rows carry the priorities; a day later, with a two-page claim limit, the newer-than-last-fetch entry is claimed a whole batch before the older equal-priority pages — nothing but priority 5 explains that order; the old lastmod bumps nothing; the report contract and the log scan hold), one new case in `tests/unit/xml-docs.test.ts` (lastmod forms: offset, Z, date-only, bogus, partial, absent; an index child's own lastmod; every produced lastmod passes the report contract).
- New app tests: 6 in `tests/unit/crawl-frontier.test.ts` (the `linkPriority` table incl. the 100 cap; a report stores each kind's priority and the depth formula; claim order is priority then longest-due, with a pre-WP11 100 row running last; the lastmod jump through the real claim/report path — priority 5, due now, claimed 24 h early — and its three refusals: a page the same report fetched, a never-fetched row, a gone row; the strict contract acceptance of the crawler's WP11 fields with per-entry skips for a bad flag, a non-ISO date and an unknown field), 4 in `tests/unit/crawl-frontier-kysely.test.ts` (the claim's two-key order; the insert values' priorities; the chunked digest read and the priority-5 update with its live-rows guard; no bump in the three refusal cases) and 4 in `tests/unit/crawl-frontier-prisma.test.ts` (the `ORDER BY "priority" ASC, "nextDueAt" ASC` raw claim; the createMany priorities; the bump `updateMany`; the three refusals).
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 34 test files / 380 tests all passed (32 / 371 before this WP), build ok. `asmita`: lint 0 errors, type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok (the new migration is the only schema change and validates), 143 test files / 1202 tests all passed (1188 before this WP, same file count: the new tests live in existing files).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated, following the WP9/WP10 precedent: that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval, which has not come for any v3 WP (silence is not consent). `crawler/README.md` and the runbook document the whole behaviour instead. No admin page, env var or stats shape changed, so nothing else in that row moved.
- The Kysely (Worker) and Prisma paths are proven against the recording fake and the mocked client, not a real Postgres (pre-existing runbook open item, unchanged): the priority column, the two-key claim, the digest read and the bump update have compiled and been recorded/executed on mocks, not run against a live database. The migration is additive (one column with a default, one index) and validates; `prisma migrate dev` was not run (no database in this environment, per rule 7).
- No real site was crawled (rule 7); the Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) and CI on Linux were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged). The crawler Docker build is unchanged by this WP (no new cross-tree import: `xml-docs.ts` now imports zod, an existing dependency of the same package, bundled as before).
- Environment, not code: five lane sessions share one Node-22 container lock on this host, so phases of the full check queued behind other lanes; every phase that ran, passed.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "An entry whose lastmod is newer than the row's last fetch gets priority 5 and is due now": a row never fetched has no last fetch, so no comparison holds — it keeps the priority its link stored and is due immediately anyway (it would be odd for a fresh row to outrank a genuinely changed one). Dead rows are final, and a row the very same report fetched keeps its fresh 24 h schedule (the report's own fetch is the strongest freshness signal; bumping it would schedule a second fetch of a page just fetched). All three refusals are tested on every backend.
- The spec's "pagination links 30" needed a way to tell a pagination link apart in the report: implemented as the optional `pagination` flag on the link entry, not a new link kind. The DB's `CrawlPageKind` enum (PAGE/SITEMAP/FEED — read by the stats buckets and the admin views) stays untouched, a pagination link is still a page document, and the flag is data while the priority stays the app's policy — so no report can write a priority directly.
- "Optional `lastmod` on reported sitemap links": parsed from both urlset `<url>` entries and sitemap-index `<sitemap>` children — both are links reported out of a sitemap document, and a changed child sitemap is exactly as jump-worthy as a changed page. The app accepts the field on any link kind but the crawler only ever sets it on sitemap-derived links; the value is normalised to ISO UTC so both schemas enforce one strict shape.
- A lastmod that is not a strict ISO date or date-time is dropped, including the partial formats the sitemap spec allows (e.g. `2026-11`): the alternative — guessing a timezone or a day — would put words in a site's mouth, and a dropped lastmod only costs a missing queue jump, never coverage.
- An existing row's priority is not recomputed when the same link is reported again (even from a different kind): only the lastmod bump changes a stored row's priority. The stored kind is the document's kind, and re-ranking rows on every re-report would churn the queue for no coverage gain; a link whose row does not exist yet stores the current rule's priority when it is new.
- "Other pages 50 plus 10 per depth level (cap 100)" uses the new row's own depth (the reporting page's depth plus one), so a home-linked page is 60 and the cap is the pre-WP11 default; the seed row is the app's own and keeps 100, which also documents "lower runs first" for every row stored before this WP.

### WP12. Follow redirects that live in the page (crawler) — 2026-11-18

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- New `crawler/src/crawl/page-redirects.ts` (no new dependency, no DOM): `parseMetaRefresh` finds the first qualifying `<meta http-equiv="refresh">` with a bounded whole-document scan — a quote-aware tag end (a target may legally contain a raw `>`), attribute order tolerated, `content` values over 2 KiB ignored, the delay an integer up to `MAX_META_REFRESH_DELAY_S` = 10 (negative reads as 0), the target `url=` after a `;`/`,`/whitespace separator, entity-decoded and quote-stripped. A refresh without a target (a timed reload), one slower than 10 s, or a malformed one is page content, not a redirect. `htmlMetaRefreshTarget` gates on the answer being HTML (`text/html`, `application/xhtml+xml`). `visibleTextBytes` (script, style and comment bodies removed, tags stripped, entities decoded, whitespace collapsed) and `isScriptOnlyStub` implement the stub rule: at least one `<script>`, nothing the static extractor took (no image candidates, no page links, no feeds) and under 2 KiB (`STUB_VISIBLE_TEXT_LIMIT` = 2048) of visible text.
- `src/net/safe-fetch.ts`: a new opt-in `FetchOptions.followMetaRefresh` (unset never follows — fail closed). When the caller opts in and the answer is HTML, the fetch loop follows a qualifying meta refresh as one more hop of the same redirect loop: the target resolves against the page's own URL, is refused by `checkUrl` (scheme, port, credentials, address) exactly like a Location header, is offered to `beforeHop` — so the crawler's per-hop scope check, the robots check and the per-host delay all apply before any request — and is counted against the same `maxRedirects` (5) budget as HTTP redirects. A target that does not parse keeps the page (browsers ignore a broken meta refresh; a malformed Location is a protocol error, a broken content attribute is not). The target's answer maps like any hop's: 304 → `not_modified`, 404/410 → `gone`, robots → `blocked`, else `error`.
- `src/crawler.ts` (kept small — a lane-shared file): page fetches opt in with `followMetaRefresh: render !== "browser"` — a BROWSER page's pre-fetch leaves the refresh to Chromium, which follows it natively during the render; an AUTO or HTTP page resolves it on the static path, so the extracted content, links and validators are the target's and findings keep the page address as claimed. The render decision gains the WP12 stub clause for AUTO sources (`isScriptOnlyStub`).
- No contract, config, schema or app change: no new environment variables (the 10 s and 2 KiB bounds are the spec's own, constants in code), the frontier-report and findings shapes are untouched, and no `asmita/` code changed.
- Fixtures (`crawler/tests/support/fixture-site.ts`, new `buildPageRedirectsFixture`; hosts `redirects.fixture.test`, `stub.fixture.test`, `away.test` overlap no other fixture): the meta-refresh redirector whose target carries the planted image while the redirector's own decoy image must never be fetched, an off-scope refresh to `away.test`, an 11-second refresh that is page content instead, a self-refresh loop, and a robots-disallowed refresh target; and on the AUTO host a `location.replace` stub, its target with the planted image and a child link, and a small script-less page that must never be rendered.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run flow, the Redirects guarantee row, the WP7 rendering section, the sources-contract `render` sentence) and `asmita/docs/ops/crawler-runbook.md` (section 2). The root `CLAUDE.md` crawler row could not be written (below).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/page-redirects.test.ts` (36: the content-value grammar and every not-a-redirect case, the stub rule with its 2 KiB boundary, and the fetcher end — opt-in default, HTML-only gate, hop numbers offered to beforeHop, a scope or robots refusal needing no request, blocked scheme and blocked address targets, an unparseable target keeping the page, a self-refresh chain stopping at exactly the six requests the shared budget allows, HTTP redirects and meta hops in one budget, a 404 target, `<base href>` ignored), `tests/integration/page-redirects.test.ts` (10 — the spec's done-when: the meta-refresh page's target image hashed under the claimed page address with the target's links reported and the redirector's decoy never fetched; the off-scope refresh never contacted, robots.txt included, the page reported error and no discovery recorded; the 11-second refresh processed as content; the loop bounded by the shared budget; the robots-disallowed target never requested and the page reported blocked; the `location.replace` stub rendered in auto mode with its target's images hashed and the rendered DOM's links reported; the script-less page never rendered; exactly the three planted findings; the log scan).
- No existing test file was changed, deleted, skipped or loosened; the shared `buildFixture` is untouched (the new pages live in their own builder and hosts).
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 31 test files / 396 tests all passed (29 / 350 before this WP, +46), build ok. `asmita`: lint, type-check, `prisma validate` ok, 143 test files / 1173 tests all passed (unchanged by this WP; no `asmita/` source was touched).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file was refused at its approval prompt with no response (as for WP1–WP8; silence is not consent), and this session was instructed never to wait for input. Intended addition (apply on approval): after "crawler/ (outside asmita/) is the service itself" — "; crawler v3 WP12: on its static fetch path it follows in-page `<meta http-equiv=refresh>` redirects (delay ≤ 10 s, scope- and robots-checked per hop, inside the same 5-hop redirect budget) and, for auto render sources, sends tiny script-only stub pages (under 2 KiB of visible text, no links) to Chromium". The runbook and `crawler/README.md` document all of it instead.
- ADR 006 received no dated note: WP12 adds no new fetch path, data flow or stored data — an in-page redirect rides the same safe-fetch hop rules the ADR already covers.
- The new behaviour was exercised against the local fixture sites only; no real site was crawled (rule 7). The container/CI items stay as in WP7 (Chromium in the test container comes from the mounted Playwright cache — one-time environment setup outside the repository).
- No new run-summary counters were added (WP12 asks for none): an operator sees in-page redirects only through their outcomes — a followed refresh is an ordinary fetched page, a refused one an ordinary error/blocked row.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The meta refresh is followed inside `safe-fetch`'s existing redirect loop rather than by a second fetch from the crawler's page processing: "as one more redirect hop" read literally — one loop, one hop budget, the same `beforeHop` scope/robots/delay checks, and no second HTTP path (rule 2). This also keeps this lane's changes to the lane-shared `crawler.ts` to three small hunks.
- An off-scope meta refresh makes the page report `error` (like an HTTP redirect hop that leaves the source's domain), not "page kept as content": "never off-scope" is read as "the hop fails as an off-scope redirect would", which also stops the frontier re-fetching a redirector that can never yield content (error backoff, dead after five). No discovery is recorded either: only anchors are discovery candidates (WP5's rule), and a meta refresh is not an anchor.
- The declared delay is not waited out: a refresh hop is a redirect and already pays the per-host delay and the robots check; a site's N seconds are display time, not a politeness signal. A refresh that is *not* followed (over 10 s) keeps its content, so nothing is lost there.
- The redirector's own image candidates and links are discarded when a qualifying refresh is followed, as a browser discards the redirecting page: the target's document is what is extracted. Pinned by the redirector-decoy fixture.
- `<base href>` is not consulted for the target: it resolves against the page's own address (absolute targets unaffected), so a page's base cannot retarget a relative refresh at another host — the conservative direction for scope.
- The stub clause of the AUTO rule is a strict subset of the WP7 clause already in the code (a script and no image candidates): a stub always has a script and no candidates, so WP12's "also send" changes no behaviour on its own. It is implemented exactly as specified — its own function, constant, clause and tests — so the stub contract is explicit and pinned; the WP7 clause is untouched (rule 6).
- The first *qualifying* refresh is followed and non-qualifying ones are skipped, matching browsers, where every refresh schedules a navigation and the earliest to fire wins.
- The whole document is scanned for the refresh meta, not only `<head>`: browsers process body metas too, and the scan is bounded by the HTML byte cap the fetcher already enforced. Attribute matching is a bounded tag scan, not a DOM parse (safe-fetch keeps its no-DOM hot path).

### WP13. Do not retry what cannot work (crawler + app) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22 — two independent full runs: the session's own and the lane driver's, identical counts).

**What was built**

- `crawler/src/errors.ts`: fetch failures are classified by the new `fetchFailureClass` — **permanent**: `dns_not_found` (a new code; `ENOTFOUND` maps to it instead of `dns_failed`) and `tls_failed` (an existing code, newly classed); **temporary**: `dns_failed` (every other resolver failure), `connect_failed`, `connect_timeout`, `headers_timeout`, `body_timeout`, `network_error`, and `http_status` with a 5xx or 429 answer. `dns_not_found` joins `tls_failed` outside the in-run retry set, so neither is retried inside a fetch either. 404/410 and 401/403 keep their own page mappings and get no class; every non-fetch error (scope, robots, caps, decode, API, browser) has none.
- `crawler/src/net/policy.ts`: `resolveAllowed` splits resolver failures — a resolver `ENOTFOUND` is the permanent `dns_not_found`; any other resolver failure and an empty answer stay the retryable `dns_failed` (an empty answer is not proof a name does not exist).
- `crawler/src/crawl/robots.ts`: a robots.txt fetch that fails permanently is rethrown instead of becoming the RFC 9309 "unreachable" disallow-all verdict — the host is dead, so the page fetch that asked reports the permanent failure and no request is ever made; without this the robots layer would mask the permanent failure and every page of the host would become a robots-blocked row rechecked every 24 h forever. The rejected verdict is cached like any robots result, so a dead host costs one robots probe per run. Temporary robots failures keep the unreachable treatment exactly.
- `crawler/src/crawler.ts`: a page fetch that fails permanently is reported `gone` straight away, so the app kills the row without the five error retries; temporary failures keep the `error` report and the 1 h / 6 h / 24 h backoff; 429/503 keep today's treatment exactly (in-run `Retry-After` retries, `error` at the app). A source whose home page (the depth-0 seed) fails permanently is counted once per source (`homePermanentFailures`), never per page and never with a hostname (rule 3). The first-report robots sitemap read catches the new rethrow (empty sitemap list on a dead host).
- `crawler/src/metrics.ts`: the run summary gains `failures.permanent` / `failures.temporary` — counted wherever an error with a class is recorded, page fetches and image fetches alike — and `homePermanentFailures`.
- App side: no app code change — `gone` was always final, so the row already dies on the first `gone` report; `asmita/tests/unit/crawl-frontier.test.ts` (memory path) pins exactly that: failCount stays 0, `lastFetchedAt` counts it as visited, a repeated report never resurrects the row, and it is never claimed again.
- Fixtures (`crawler/tests/support/fixture-site.ts`, new `buildPermanentFailuresFixture`; hosts `temp500.test`, `temp502.test`, `reset.test`, `rate429.test`, `dead.test`, no overlap with any other fixture): a plain-500 home page (fetched once — 500 was never in the in-run retry set), a 502 home page and a mid-answer connection reset (both retried in-run like today), a 429 with `Retry-After: 0`, and a host absent from the test policy's DNS so resolving it fails with `ENOTFOUND`. `run-fixture.ts` gains a test-only `wrapFetcher` hook so the integration test can count fetch attempts.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (the run flow, the failing-source paragraph, a new guarantees row, the robots failure-policy table, the report contract, the run-summary list) and `asmita/docs/ops/crawler-runbook.md` (the report route, the exit-code table paragraph).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/failure-classes.test.ts` (31: the mapping and class of every syscall code in both classes, the no-class cases, the retry decision through `withRetry` — a dead name and a certificate failure are never retried, temporary ones and 429/503 keep their retries — the resolver split, the robots pass-through vs the unreachable verdict, the summary counters and the summary shape) and `tests/integration/permanent-failures.test.ts` (2 — the spec's done-when: a DNS-dead source reports its home page `gone` on the first attempt, one robots probe and one page fetch with no in-run retry, the row dead with failCount 0, a second run claims nothing; and 500/502/reset/429 keep today's in-run retries, report `error`, the app backs off 1 h and hands the rows out again an hour later; both with the no-hostname log scan).
- Existing tests changed on purpose (rule 6): `tests/unit/log-retry-limits.test.ts` and `tests/unit/ssrf.test.ts` expected `ENOTFOUND` → `dns_failed`, which is now the distinct `dns_not_found`; `tests/integration/lifecycle.test.ts` proved the dead source through the robots-unreachable path WP13 removes — that case is now a robots.txt connection reset (still unreachable-robots, still blocked pages) and the DNS-dead source is asserted through its new `dns_not_found` and `homePermanentFailures` counters instead.
- Package totals, from the full checks (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 33 test files / 429 tests all passed (31 / 396 before this WP: +2 files, +33 tests), build ok. `asmita`: lint (0 errors; 3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched file), type-check, `prisma validate` ok, 143 test files / 1174 tests all passed (+1 test; no `asmita/` source was touched).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file was refused at its approval prompt with no response (as for WP1–WP8 and WP12; silence is not consent), and the session was instructed never to wait for input. Intended addition (apply on approval): after "`crawler/` (outside `asmita/`) is the service itself" — "; it classifies fetch failures (crawler v3 WP13: a DNS name that does not exist or a TLS certificate failure is permanent — the page is reported `gone` on the first attempt instead of retried, while timeouts, resets and 5xx keep the error backoff)". The runbook and `crawler/README.md` document all of it instead.
- ADR 006 received no dated note: WP13 adds no fetch path, data flow or stored data — it stops retrying fetches that can never succeed, inside the rules the ADR already covers.
- The TLS-certificate permanent class is exercised at unit level (the `tls_failed` mapping, class and retry decision) and through the robots pass-through; the end-to-end fixture proves the DNS case, because a TLS-dead fixture host would need its own test-CA setup that the permanent-failure fixture does not add (the fetch path itself is covered by `tests/unit/tls.test.ts`).
- The new behaviour was exercised against the local fixture sites only; no real site was crawled (rule 7).
