This progress entry is the WP's own record and is committed separately (not pushed by the session).

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The robots.txt rethrow is an addition the spec's wording implies but does not spell out: "permanent failures are reported `gone` straight away" can only hold if the robots layer does not swallow the failure first — the robots.txt fetch shares the page's origin, so whatever killed it kills the page fetch too. The README's robots failure-policy table documents the new row.
- "A DNS name not found" is identified with the resolver's `ENOTFOUND`, both in the syscall mapper and in `resolveAllowed`; an empty resolver answer deliberately stays the temporary `dns_failed`, because an empty answer is not proof the name does not exist.
- 429/503 are classed temporary for the summary counters only; their behaviour is untouched (temporary has no page-status effect — only permanent changes a report, to `gone`). The in-run retry set is unchanged: a plain 500 is fetched once, 502/504 retried, exactly as before.
- `homePermanentFailures` reads "a source's home page" as the depth-0 seed row (`https://<domain>/`), counted once per source per run; deeper pages of the same dead host report `gone` without a second count.

### WP14. Skip fake "not found" pages (crawler) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- New `crawler/src/crawl/soft404.ts`: `soft404Digest` digests a fetched HTML body normalised exactly as the spec says — whitespace collapsed, digits stripped — into 16 hex chars of sha256, so a "not found" template that echoes only the requested id or page number is recognised across URLs while genuinely different pages (different words, different image addresses) stay distinct. `Soft404Tracker` is the per-source, per-run memory: one entry per digest with the 16-byte hashes of the distinct claimed-URL keys that carried it (never a URL, never a body), and once one digest has appeared on `SOFT404_THRESHOLD` (the spec's 5) distinct URLs, `observe` flags every further page with it. Bounded: at most `SOFT404_DIGEST_CAP` (256) digests per source, least recently used dropped first, at most 5 URL-key hashes per digest (once flagged, counting more distinct URLs would only spend memory proving the known); a killed run loses the tracker whole, which is safe — the app's rows keep their schedules and a later run recounts.
- `crawler.ts` (three small hunks in the lane-shared file): `SourceCtx` gains the tracker, created per source per run inside `crawlSource`; `processPage` computes the digest of every fetched HTML body (after the 304, XML-document, non-HTML and scope checks) and, when the tracker flags it, extracts nothing — no images, no links, no browser render, no discovery candidates — and reports the page `gone`, so the app kills the row like any dead page. Recognition is retrospective: the page is still fetched (its body must be read), robots and every fetch rule apply unchanged, and the digest never leaves memory and is never logged (rule 3; the new integration test's log scan covers it).
- `metrics.ts`: the run summary gains `pagesSoft404`. Soft-404s are still fetched pages, so they count in `pagesFetched` too; the categories stay disjoint (`pagesAttempted = fetched + not_modified + non_html + xml + errors + …`).
- No contract, config, schema or app change: `gone` was already final in the app, so a soft-404 needs no app-side rule; the threshold and cap are constants in code (like `ROBOTS_SITEMAP_CAP`), no new env knobs; the frontier-report shape is untouched. The nested summary keys pass the logger's sanitiser without a `log.ts` change (only outer field names are allowlisted).
- Fixtures (`crawler/tests/support/fixture-site.ts`): new `buildSoft404Fixture` (hosts `soft404.fixture.test`, `alike.fixture.test`, no overlap) — a hub linking twelve unknown paths, every unknown path answered with the same 200 "not found" page that plants an in-pool image and one link (so the cut is observable: exactly one finding, one row under the template's link, every gone entry linkless), and nine genuinely similar gallery pages (same layout, the same digits everywhere, a different word and image each). Existing fixture data changed in three places, each for the same reason (below): the deep fixture's 30 listing pages, the trap fixture's admitted `/t?u=N` variants and the flood fixture's pages gained a non-digit word per page.
- Docs: `crawler/README.md` (the run flow, the bounded-frontier row's state inventory, a new "Fake not found pages are cut (WP14)" guarantees row, the report-contract `gone` sentence, the run-summary list, known limits) and `asmita/docs/ops/crawler-runbook.md` (the report route, the summary-reading paragraph). The root `CLAUDE.md` crawler row could not be written (below).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/soft404.test.ts` (14: the normalisation — whitespace collapse, digit stripping, genuinely-different pages staying distinct, the digest's fixed shape; the 5-distinct-URL boundary (1..5 false, 6th true), distinct-URL counting vs repeat observations, digest independence, the cap keeping at most N digests, LRU eviction with re-counting from zero after eviction, the per-digest URL-set cap, cap 0), `tests/integration/soft404.test.ts` (7 — the spec's done-when: the not-found site's twelve linked unknown paths are each fetched exactly once (recognition is retrospective) with the first five reported `fetched` and the last seven `gone` with empty links, the template's one link row fetched once and reported `gone`, exactly one finding from the template's in-pool image, `pagesSoft404` 8; the nine similar gallery pages all fetched, extracted and reported `fetched` with all nine images hashed once and nine findings attributed to their pages; the log scan).
- Existing tests changed only in fixture data, on purpose (rule 6): `buildDeepFixture`'s listing pages gained `<h1>Listing <letters></h1>`, the trap fixture's `/t?u=N` bodies gained a per-variant word, and the flood fixture's pages gained a per-page tag — because WP14's digit-stripping normalisation deliberately makes pages that differ only in numbers one digest, and those three fixtures' pages were digit-only variants of one template, so WP14 would have cut them (the 6th planted listing image, the trap pages' `pagesFetched` count, the flood pages' `linksDropped`) for reasons their tests were never about. Every assertion in the affected tests is unchanged, none deleted, skipped or loosened; the fixtures now model what their tests claim (genuinely different listing pages, eight fetched trap variants, a flood where every page offers its links).
- Package totals, from the full `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 35 test files / 450 tests all passed (33 / 429 before this WP: +2 files, +21 tests), build ok. `asmita`: lint, type-check, `prisma validate` ok, 143 test files / 1174 tests all passed (no `asmita/` source was touched; the runbook is documentation).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated: the write to that protected agent-instruction file has been refused at its approval prompt in every WP since WP1 (silence is not consent), and this session was instructed never to wait for input. Intended addition (apply on approval): after the WP13 sentence — "; and it skips fake 'not found' pages (crawler v3 WP14): a fetched body whose normalised digest — whitespace collapsed, digits stripped — has already appeared on five or more distinct URLs of the source this run is reported gone with nothing extracted, in memory only, never logged". The runbook and `crawler/README.md` document all of it instead.
- ADR 006 received no dated note: WP14 adds no fetch path, data flow or stored data — the digests live in the crawler's memory for one run and never reach the app, a log line or disk.
- "Digests never leave memory and are never logged" is enforced by code structure (nothing serialises the tracker), the counts-only logger and the new tests' log scan; there is no dedicated static rule for it like the app's no-fetch lint. An operator-verifiable proof would need a runtime taint check, which no WP specifies.
- The behaviour was exercised against the local fixture sites only; no real site was crawled (rule 7). The Docker image build, CI on Linux and the Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged).
- A second run re-counting from zero was verified through the per-run tracker design and the unit tests, not by a dedicated two-pass integration case (a soft-404 row is dead in the app, so a second run has nothing to claim — the same proof `permanent-failures.test.ts` uses for `gone`).

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- The spec's "each fetched HTML body" is applied to every fetched HTML page whatever the render mode, browser sources included: a BROWSER/AUTO page whose static shell repeats on five or more distinct URLs is cut before its render. Literal reading of the spec, and the honest consequence is documented in the README's known limits (an SPA whose every URL serves the byte-identical shell loses later rows); a render-only exception would make the tracker's memory unbounded in the other direction and no fixture or existing test exercises such a site.
- Sitemap and feed documents are not digested: the spec says HTML bodies, and XML documents are templates by nature (every `<urlset>` of one site resembles every other); a soft-404 answer is an HTML page.
- The three existing-fixture data changes above are the one place an existing test's inputs had to move: the alternative — exempting pages with image candidates or ignoring the cut in those tests — would have changed assertions (loosening, rule 6) or warped the spec's normalisation. The fixtures' pages were degenerate under WP14's own definition (digit-only variants), and the words make them what their tests' descriptions already claimed they were.
- Soft-404 pages count in both `pagesFetched` and `pagesSoft404`: the fetch happened and the body was read (recognition is retrospective), and the summary keeps its disjoint categories — `pagesSoft404` is the breakdown, like `imagesOffHost` inside `imagesSeen`.
- The tracker is per run and per source and is not kept across `run --loop` passes (unlike the query-variant guard): the spec says "within one run", and a pass boundary is a run boundary; a trap therefore gets a fresh count per pass, bounded by the app's own gone/dead bookkeeping.
- Beyond the 256-digest cap the least recently used digest is evicted rather than the new one refused: refusing would let a hostile site's template churn disable the cut permanently, while eviction only ever delays it — and re-observing an evicted digest starts it counting fresh, which is safe for the same reason a fresh run is.
- The spec's threshold 5 and the cap 256 are constants in code, not config: the spec fixes 5, every knob needs fail-closed Zod + `.env.example` + docs, and an operator tuning this per deployment has no spec'd use case (the query-variant cap needed a knob because real paginations legitimately vary; a soft-404 template does not).
- A soft-404 report carries no validators (like the 404/410 path): the row is dead, so ETag/Last-Modified would never be used.

### WP15. A lighter browser (crawler) — 2026-11-18

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- New `crawler/src/browser/blocked-requests.ts`: the requests the browser worker aborts before any routing work. By kind: fonts and audio/video media (Chromium's `font` and `media` resource types; image requests are never blocked — they are their own type). By host: a small built-in list of 14 advertising/analytics hosts (Google Analytics, Tag Manager, DoubleClick, ScorecardResearch, Hotjar, Clarity, Mixpanel, Segment — both `segment.io` and `segment.com` — Yandex Metrika, Xandr, Criteo, PubMatic, Rubicon), written in code, not configurable; a host matches if it equals an entry or is a subdomain of one (the never-crawl rule).
- `browser-worker.ts` `handleRoute`: the block check runs before the request cap, before the scope check, before the parent's robots verdict and before any fetch — blocked requests are never fetched, never asked about and never counted against `BROWSER_MAX_REQUESTS_PER_PAGE`, so the cap keeps its room for the page's images. The host check is repeated inside the redirect loop, so nothing that is followed can end on a blocked host.
- Network quiet (the WP's second half): the fixed `BROWSER_SCROLL_WAIT_MS` wait after the scroll loop, and the one-shot `Promise.all` over the requests in flight at that moment, are replaced by `waitNetworkQuiet`: wait until no request has been in flight for a 500 ms window, bounded by the page deadline. The window is capped by `BROWSER_SCROLL_WAIT_MS` (an idle page waits no longer than the old fixed wait did), and any in-flight request restarts it, so a slow or chained lazy-loader is waited for — including requests its own completions spawn after the old one-shot snapshot was taken. The per-scroll waits inside the scroll loop keep the old behaviour; only the wait after scrolling is replaced, as the spec words it.
- No protocol, contract, config or app-code change; the `RenderedMessage.stats.requests` comment now says blocked kinds are not counted (its meaning, not its shape). Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run diagram, a new "A lighter browser (WP15)" guarantees row, two WP15 paragraphs in "Browser rendering", the config table), `crawler/.env.example`, the runbook (section 3 and the env table). Root `CLAUDE.md` not touched (below).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/browser-blocked.test.ts` (8: blocked kinds vs every kind a gallery needs; host and subdomain matching across scheme, port, path, case and trailing dot; name-contains non-matches; the no-image-or-video-CDN guarantee against 16 well-known media CDN hosts plus a structural check of the list; unparseable URLs). `tests/integration/browser-light.test.ts` (7, real Chromium end to end against a new `buildLightBrowserFixture` in `tests/support/fixture-site.ts`: a home page with 12 web fonts, a video, an audio file and two tracker scripts from the built-in list — the fixture DNS maps the ad hosts to the fixture server, so an unblocked request or its robots.txt fetch would land in the request log — and three images added by script at load, crawled under `BROWSER_MAX_REQUESTS_PER_PAGE=8`; plus a `/slow` page whose chained lazy-loader answers only after 800 ms hops). Asserts: blocked kinds are never requested, image requests still are (exactly once each, all three matches posted), the cap leaves room for the images (the 16 blocked requests would consume it otherwise), the slow chained lazy-loader is waited for and its third, planted image is found, and the log scan stays clean. 15 new tests.
- Negative checks, run and then reverted (nothing of them committed): with the block checks disabled, the fixture server sees font and tracker traffic (including the trackers' robots.txt) and the cap test fails on the missing findings; with the old fixed wait restored in place of network quiet, the slow chain's third image is lost. Both features' tests bite.
- Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened: `browser.test.ts` (9), `browser-escape.test.ts` (7), `browser-renderer.test.ts` (13), `browser-proxy.test.ts` (11) and `memory-only.test.ts` (2, whose browser-rendered crawl covers the new wait) all pass unchanged.

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was again not updated: it is a protected agent-instruction file and the owner's approval for writing it did not come in this session (as for WP1–WP8; silence is not consent). Intended addition (apply on approval): in the crawler bullet's browser-rendering sentence, add that since crawler v3 WP15 the browser worker aborts fonts, audio/video media and a small built-in advertising/analytics host list before routing (never counted against the per-page request cap) and waits for network quiet instead of a fixed wait after scrolling.
- The blocked kinds were exercised only against the local fixture site (rule 7); no real site was rendered. Chromium's media stack retrying aborted autoplay loads was observed only as repeated per-request aborts — each blocked again, none reaching the network — and their CPU cost was not measured.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "A small built-in list of advertising/analytics hosts": read as well-known hosts whose sole business is advertising or analytics. Hosts that mix tracking with media serving (Taboola and Outbrain serve recommendation thumbnails; `facebook.net` is a CDN) are deliberately absent — the spec's "must not include any image or video CDN" wins over list length.
- "with the existing scroll wait as the upper limit": read as capping the 500 ms quiet window by `BROWSER_SCROLL_WAIT_MS`, not as capping the whole quiet wait. A cap on the whole wait would cut it at 500 ms — exactly when a slow loader is still in flight — making the spec's own done-when ("a slow lazy-loader is waited for") unreachable. The window cap keeps the efficiency the sentence asks for (an idle page waits no longer than the old fixed wait), and the page deadline stays the hard bound, as before.
- A redirect hop that lands on a blocked host is aborted before its verdict and fetch but still counted against the cap (the request was already counted when its redirect was followed). Only the page's own requests — the fonts, media and trackers the spec names — are cap-free, which is what the done-when exercises.

### WP16. Galleries in frames and components (crawler) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

**What was built**

- Static path (`crawler/src/crawl/extract.ts`): `<iframe src>` and `<frame src>` are collected as page links (kind `page`) under the anchor rules — nofollow (the page's meta robots / `X-Robots-Tag` or the element's own `rel`), the obvious-non-content skip, and a src straight to an image file is an image candidate, not a page — plus `<base href>` resolution, fragment dropping, dedup against an anchor to the same address, and the per-page link cap. `about:`, `data:`, `javascript:` and empty or missing srcs are nothing (never a self-link). Scope stays the crawler's check: an off-scope frame src is collected like any link, and link admission keeps the in-scope ones and turns the rest into discovery hosts, exactly as for anchors — so an off-scope frame is never fetched.
- Browser path (`crawler/src/browser/browser-worker.ts`, `GATHER_SCRIPT` + `gatherFrameImages`): after the network-quiet wait (WP15) the render walks every frame of the page (`page.frames()`, `about:srcdoc` frames included) and every open shadow root (closed roots are invisible by design), collects the same image candidates the static extractor reads — an `<img>` that has not loaded, raw srcsets, the lazy `data-*` attributes, `<video>` posters, inline background styles, anchor hrefs straight to an image file — and makes the browser load the ones the page has not asked for, in bounded chunks. Those loads are ordinary routed image requests — same proxy, same robots verdict, same request and image caps; bytes still come only from intercepted responses — and when the gather started loads the quiet wait runs once more, so their bytes arrive before the render answers. Per-render dedup against the hop-0 URLs the page's requests were routed under (`routedUrls`, served or refused alike), so nothing already asked for is asked again; loads are capped by the remaining per-page image slots; the walk is bounded (64 frames per render, 20 000 elements visited per frame, 200 raw candidates per list, 4096 remembered routed URLs) and every step is bounded by the page deadline. An out-of-scope frame contributes nothing: its navigation is refused before any request leaves (scope and robots checks as before), so it keeps only the empty initial document.
- Shared candidate rules: `IMAGE_EXT`, `LAZY_SRC_ATTRS`, `LAZY_SRCSET_ATTRS` and the new `styleBackgroundImages` are exported from `extract.ts` and reused by the worker, so the static and rendered paths collect the same candidates (the worker bundle carries cheerio as an external dependency, as it already does for the main bundle; the isolated worker still starts, renders and stays under its permission-model flags — verified by the real-Chromium tests).
- No protocol, contract, config or app-code change, and no new setting (the per-page caps bound the gather). Docs: `crawler/README.md` (run diagram, a new "Galleries in frames and components (WP16)" guarantees row, a WP16 paragraph in "Browser rendering"), the runbook (one paragraph). Root `CLAUDE.md` not touched (below).

**How this WP was finished** (the lane's check 1 had failed)

The WP arrived implemented but with the browser gather disabled by a temporary negative-check line (`if (task.msg.scrolls >= -1) return 0`, left in `gatherFrameImages` from the RED proof) plus an untracked debug-scratch test. Check 1 therefore failed exactly the four browser-side tests (the shadow-root gallery, the iframe gallery, the srcdoc gallery, and the planted-findings set) with zero gathered images — which is the negative check proving those tests bite: nothing else failed. This session removed the flag and the scratch test, corrected four documentation sentences that overstated the off-scope-frame behaviour ("the gather never walks one", "it holds no document": an aborted frame keeps the empty initial `about:blank` document and the walk of it finds nothing — the guarantee the tests pin, never contacting the off-scope host, was always true), and changed nothing else in the implementation.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/frames-extract.test.ts` (12: in-document iframe src and frameset frame src as page links; off-scope src still collected — scope is the crawler's check; `<base href>` resolution; fragment dropped, dedup against an anchor; about:/data:/javascript:/malformed and empty/whitespace/missing srcs collect nothing; meta-nofollow and element `rel=nofollow`; the non-content skip counted; a src straight to an image file as an image candidate; the per-page link cap with the overflow counted).
- New: `tests/integration/frames.test.ts` (11, real Chromium end to end against the new `buildFramesFixture` in `tests/support/fixture-site.ts`, two sources, synthetic images only): the static source follows an in-scope iframe gallery and a `<frameset>` frame page and hashes both planted images; the off-scope iframe's host (mapped into the fixture DNS, so a wrongly followed frame, its image or its robots.txt fetch would land in the request log) is never contacted and is reported as a discovery, and its in-pool canary image never matches; the rendered source's galleries exist only as `data-src` behind lazy loaders that wait for an event nothing sends, so the shadow-root gallery (with its decoy second image, which matches nothing), the in-scope iframe's gallery (credited to the embedding page) and the `about:srcdoc` frame's gallery can only be found by the frame/shadow gather; exactly the five planted findings are posted; the log scan stays clean.
- Nothing deleted, skipped or loosened: `browser.test.ts` (9), `browser-escape.test.ts` (7), `browser-light.test.ts` (7), `browser-renderer.test.ts` (19), `memory-only.test.ts` (2) and every other existing test pass unchanged.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 33 test files / 388 tests all passed (31 files / 365 before this WP), build ok. `asmita`: lint, type-check, `prisma validate` ok, 143 test files / 1173 tests all passed (unchanged by this WP; only its runbook gained a paragraph).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was again not updated: it is a protected agent-instruction file and the owner's approval for writing it did not come in this session (as for WP1–WP15; silence is not consent). Intended addition (apply on approval): after the browser-rendering sentence, that since crawler v3 WP16 a page's `<iframe>`/`<frame>` srcs are followed as ordinary page links and a render also gathers image candidates from every frame and open shadow root, loading the ones the page's own scripts never asked for.
- The gather was exercised only against the local fixture site (rule 7); no real framed or web-component site was rendered. Closed shadow roots stay invisible by design; galleries built entirely by canvas or script-side byte assembly (no candidate attribute anywhere) remain out of reach, as before.
- The Docker image, CI on Linux and the Worker smoke were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged); no image or CI change was needed — the worker's environment, flags and dependencies are unchanged.

**Deviations from the spec, with reasons**

- "gather image candidates from every frame and from open shadow roots": read as gathering the same candidates the static extractor reads (an unloaded `<img>`, srcsets, the lazy `data-*` attributes, video posters, inline background styles, image hrefs), not inventing a second extractor; the rules live in `extract.ts` and are shared, so both paths stay in agreement.
- The gather starts its loads in the main frame (`new Image()`), not in the frame a candidate came from: the request is identical either way (same routed path, robots verdict and caps), while a frame can be cross-origin, detached or unresponsive to `evaluate`; the bytes are still credited to the address that served them and the finding to the claimed page.
- "out-of-scope frames are not rendered (scope and robots checks as today)": taken as today's behaviour — an off-scope frame's navigation is refused in the route handler before any verdict or fetch. The gather adds no scope check of its own because it needs none: a refused frame keeps only the empty initial document, so it contributes no candidates. A frame that a page's script writes into without any navigation is page content, like an image the script creates in the main document, and its images are ordinary candidates under the same robots verdict and caps.
- Nofollow, the obvious-non-content skip and the image-src rule apply to frame srcs exactly as to anchors; the spec says only "as page links (kind page)", and those rules are what an ordinary page link already means in `extract.ts`.

### WP90. Review fixes (crawler v3) — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

WP90 is not a spec work package: this lane (branch `feat/crawler-v3`, cut after the three v3 lanes merged) finishes the defects a review found in the crawler v3 work. The instruction that defined it names eight review fixes; fixes 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 were already committed when the lane started (`36fc052`, `ea70499`, `4fc2184`, `315ca67`, `2b40f17`), and the working tree arrived with uncommitted partial work for fixes 4 and 7, which was continued, not thrown away. Everything landed in one commit per fix:

- **Fix 4 (WP10), commit `16685fa`:** an insert skips a row stored under a legacy page key. Frontier rows stored before WP10 carry the digest of the legacy key (the URL as written, tracking stripped); a link that spells such a row's page differently (https/http, bare/www., trailing slash, `/index.*`) would have inserted a duplicate live row after the deploy. `crawl-page-key.ts` gained `legacyPageKey`/`legacyPageKeys` (at most 20 spellings: 2 schemes × 2 host forms × the path bare, slash-ended and index-ended), and `reportCrawlPages` checks every incoming link and alias against the legacy digests too — in the same read as the WP11 lastmod check, on the memory, Kysely and Prisma paths; a link whose page has a legacy row is dropped and a lastmod or alias still reaches that row. No backfill: old rows age out with the 30-day retention.
- **Fix 7 (WP13), commit `22b28c9`:** only definite failures are permanent, and a row dies on the second one. A DNS name that does not exist or a definite TLS certificate failure (expired, wrong name, self-signed) is still never retried in the run, but is reported `error` carrying the new optional `failure: "permanent"` hint (strict Zod on both sides, only with status `error`) instead of `gone` on the first attempt. The app backs the row off 1 h like any error while remembering the first permanent failure on the row (new `CrawlPage.lastPermanentFailAt`, additive migration `20261119080000_crawl_page_permanent_fail`; a successful visit clears it) and marks it DEAD only on a permanent failure at least 24 h after the first, with the window measured from the first failure and never sliding. `UNABLE_TO_GET_ISSUER_CERT_LOCALLY` and `ERR_SSL_WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER` left the permanent class for the new temporary, retryable `tls_unverified` (a missing local issuer or a non-TLS answer on the TLS port is the crawler's environment, not the site). A robots.txt fetch that fails permanently is rethrown as before but no longer cached: the next request to the origin asks again, so one bad resolver answer cannot condemn every page of the origin for the rest of the run.
- **Fix 8 (WP11), commit `822b45d`:** backfilled priorities, one-shot lastmod jumps, seeds at 50. A new data migration (`20261119090000_crawl_page_priority_backfill`) backfills every row stored before the fix to the value a fresh report would store — sitemap 10, feed 20, a pagination-stored page 30, a depth-0 seed 50, any other page 50 + 10 per depth capped at 100 — and new seeds are inserted at 50 on all three backends (`FRONTIER_PRIORITY_SEED`). A successful visit now resets the row's priority to its kind/depth value (`visitPriority`), so a lastmod jump (priority 5) is one-shot: a bumped row runs first until it is crawled, never after; a failed visit keeps the bump. A reported lastmod is clamped to now before it is compared, so a site writing next year cannot outrank every honest page.

The 14 crawler tests that failed at the merged commit all pass: canonical (3), continuous (4), frames (5) and the lifecycle clean-run case were already fixed by the committed fixes 1-3 (verified by running them at the start of this session), and permanent-failures was rewritten to the fix-7 rule.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New or changed with the fixes: the memory path gained 6 frontier tests (every permanent-failure branch, the marker cleared by a visit, the hint refused off an error status, the seed at 50, the visit reset over fetched/not_modified/error, the future lastmod clamped to now), the Kysely path 2 and the Prisma path 2 (the marker written and read, DEAD on the second permanent failure 24 h on, the visit reset, the seed insert at 50); `failure-classes.test.ts` gained the `tls_unverified` mapping, its retry, and the robots no-cache case (33 tests now); `report-contract.test.ts` gained the hint's contract (4); `permanent-failures.test.ts` was rewritten to the new rule (still 2 tests, far denser: three probes per run, the row alive inside the 24 h window, DEAD on the second permanent failure a day later, nothing claimed after that); the mock mirrors the app's new rules so the integration suite exercises them.
- Existing tests changed only where behaviour changed on purpose (rule 6, each reason in its commit message): `permanent-failures.test.ts` (the old test demanded `gone`-on-first-attempt, which fix 7 removes), `priority.test.ts` (the seed row's claim-order place and `/gallery` back at 70 after its fetched visit — the old expectations pinned the seed at 100 and the bump surviving its own visit), the Kysely "no bump" assertion (told apart from the visit reset by the bump's value, 5), and the memory-path seed assertion (50).
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 46 test files / 529 tests all passed, build ok. `asmita`: lint 0 errors (3 pre-existing warnings in an untouched script), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 143 test files / 1225 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated, following the WP1-WP16 precedent: that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval, which did not come; silence is not consent. Intended addition (apply on approval): after the crawler sentence — "crawler v3 review fixes: only definite failures (a dead name, a definitely broken certificate) are permanent, reported `error` with a `permanent` hint, and a frontier row dies only on the second permanent failure at least 24 h after the first; the WP11 priorities were backfilled (seeds at 50) and a lastmod jump is one-shot; a link is checked against the legacy digests of pre-WP10 rows so no duplicate page row is stored". `crawler/README.md` and the runbook document all of it instead.
- The two new migrations (the `lastPermanentFailAt` column and the priority backfill UPDATE) have run only against the memory store and mocks, never a real Postgres (the runbook's go-live item now names all four crawler v3 migrations). The backfill's CASE expression in particular has never executed.
- The fix-4 and fix-7 commits were verified with the frontier and crawler test subsets at their exact trees plus the final full `check.sh` at the end state; a full `npm run check` was not run separately at each intermediate commit (the counts above are the final tree's).
- A concurrent detached session worked in this worktree during the WP (the documented WP8/WP13 pattern): it committed fix 4 (`16685fa`) and made comment-only documentation edits that matched this plan verbatim (`robots.ts`, `metrics.ts`, and `net/policy.ts`'s fix-7 wording, included in the final commit). Everything it contributed was audited hunk by hunk against the fix list before being kept.
- `tests/integration/frontier.test.ts` (link flood) failed three times in full-suite runs mid-session and has passed in every run since (isolation, a 9-file subset, and two full suites) with the tree in its committed state; the failures clustered exactly in the window in which the concurrent session was editing crawler sources, and the run-to-run variance was one page's report. Recorded as environment, not chased further; if it recurs, re-run before suspecting the code.
- No real site was crawled (rule 7); the Docker image build, CI on Linux and the Worker smoke (`SMOKE_CRAWLER=1`) were not run (pre-existing open items, unchanged).

**Deviations from the fix list, with reasons**

- The backfill formula in the fix text omits pagination-stored pages; the migration sets them to 30, because WP11's own contract gives pagination links 30 and re-backfilling them to the depth formula would silently undo it. Noted in the fix-8 commit message.
- "The app marks the row DEAD only on the second permanent failure at least 24 hours after the first" is measured from the FIRST permanent failure and the timestamp never slides: a second failure inside the window moves nothing, so a page that flaps cannot keep its window open forever.
- "After a row is fetched reset its priority": a `not_modified` answer resets too — a 304 is a successful visit that consumed the jump; resetting only on `fetched` would leave a bumped row that keeps answering 304 at the front of the queue forever, the exact defect fix 8 removes.
- The permanent-failure path keeps the ordinary error accounting (failCount, the 1 h/6 h/24 h backoff, dead after five) — the permanent kill is an additional, earlier death, not a replacement schedule.
- The fix-7 robots change costs one robots.txt probe per request to a dead origin instead of one per run; bounded by the page budget and the point of the fix (one bad resolver answer must not condemn the origin for the whole run).
- Fix 4's documentation (the README "Real addresses" row and the runbook's WP10 paragraph) landed in the fix-8 commit: the fix-4 commit was made by the concurrent session without doc updates, and the docs for all three fixes were written as one pass afterwards.
- The Kysely/Prisma test additions were restructured into separate fix-7 and fix-8 describes (two test pairs merged) so the two commits could carry their own tests cleanly.

## Sandbox

### WP25. Browser rendering safe to enable: sandbox self-test, fail closed, worker uid separation — 2026-11-19

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22) **and the Docker verification script ran green end to end.**

WP25 is not in the crawler spec; it was defined by this lane's task: make browser rendering safe enough to enable for real sites. Until now Chromium ran with `--no-sandbox` (twice over: the crawler passed the flag, and Playwright silently adds its own unless `chromiumSandbox: true` is set), so a renderer exploit ran as the crawler's user and could read the key. What was built:

- **Chromium's own sandbox ON, self-tested.** `chromiumArgs(sandboxed)` replaces the old constant: the sandboxed list carries no `--no-sandbox`, and the worker launches with `chromiumSandbox: true` — the option that withholds Playwright's implicit flag. Chromium's Linux sandbox needs no setuid helper, only unprivileged user namespaces, and it fails its launch hard when the runtime refuses them (measured: both the full browser and headless shell die with "No usable sandbox!" rather than degrade — so a successful launch is an honest sandbox verdict, never a silent fallback). The self-test is the launch itself plus one about:blank probe (bounded at 20 s, inside the parent's 30 s launch timeout), run the first time a run needs a browser; the verdict lands in the run summary as `browser.sandboxTested` / `browser.sandboxOn` / `browser.workerUid` — booleans and ids only, never a URL (the summary type gained booleans for exactly this).
- **Fail closed.** A refused self-test disables rendering for the run: `render = BROWSER` pages report `error` under the new `browser_sandbox_unavailable` code without rendering, `render = AUTO` pages fall back to their static result (both through the existing WP7 render-failure paths in `crawler.ts`, which is why the lane's changes there are two lines). The refusal is sticky: one `browser_rendering_disabled` log line per run, and no later render spawns anything. The only override is `CRAWLER_ALLOW_UNSANDBOXED_BROWSER=true` — local development only, strict literals (`TRUE`/`yes` fail startup), default false, documented in `.env.example`, the config table and the runbook; with it the run re-spawns the worker unsandboxed and records `sandboxOn: false`.
- **The worker never holds the key.** Its environment was already a fixed five-name allowlist; WP25 pins that with tests (env and argv carry no key, no key-file path, no `/proc` grant). In the container image the worker now also runs as a **separate user**: the image gains the `browser-worker` user (uid/gid 30000) and a 30-line setuid-root drop helper, `/usr/local/bin/asmita-browser-drop` — the `chrome-sandbox` pattern (drop all groups, switch to the compile-time uid, exec; no attacker-controlled code ever runs as root, and uid 0 is refused at compile time). The crawler spawns the worker through it (`CRAWLER_BROWSER_WORKER_HELPER` is image plumbing, read like `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`), so a renderer exploit lands on a uid that cannot read the key file (owned by the crawler's user, mode 0400) or the parent's `/proc/<pid>/environ`. The worker's ready message reports its own uid, so the summary proves the drop happened (`workerUid: 30000`).
- **Entrypoint.** Started as root, the image's entrypoint hands the mounted key file to the crawler's user (0400) and drops privileges once with `setpriv`; nothing keeps root afterwards; without `setpriv` it refuses to run as root at all. Started non-root (the image default, and how Cloudflare Containers runs it) it execs directly, passing through non-crawler commands (`node dist/sandbox-check.js` works without re-invoking the crawler). Design reasoning, recorded as the task asked: a non-root process cannot switch uids and Node's `fork` uid options need `CAP_SETUID`, so the only mechanism that also works in a non-root container is a baked setuid-root helper that drops before exec — the same choice Chromium itself made for its sandbox; the root window is three syscalls wide and runs no caller code. The worker's IPC channel survives the helper's exec chain: `spawn` wires `NODE_CHANNEL_FD` into the child's environment whatever the binary, and node's bootstrap picks the channel up after the `execvp` (verified with a live Uint8Array round-trip before the design was committed).
- **The glibc lesson.** The first container run failed in a way worth recording: glibc's secure-exec environment scrub deletes `TMPDIR` from the environment of every setuid-root binary — the helper lost exactly that one variable, Chromium fell back to `/tmp` for its shared-memory files, and the read-only root filesystem turned that into a renderer crash (SIGTRAP after "Creating shared memory in /tmp failed"). Fix: the spawn passes the temp directory as the helper's `argv[1]` and the helper sets it back into `TMPDIR` after the drop (an ordinary exec never scrubs it again), so the worker code itself never touches `process.env` and the "reads process.env only in main.ts" static test keeps its teeth.
- **Docker verification** (`crawler/scripts/docker-sandbox-check.sh`, run from the repository root; Docker was usable by this user, so it was RUN, not just written — Docker Engine 29.9.3). It builds the image and proves both modes against real containers, ending `ALL DOCKER SANDBOX CHECKS PASSED`: under Docker's **default seccomp profile** the standalone probe (`node dist/sandbox-check.js`, no key, no config, `--network none`) reports `sandboxOn: false` with `browser_sandbox_unavailable` and exits 1; the key file is unreadable as uid 30000 and readable as the crawler's uid 1000; an entrypoint started as root (`-u 0`) drops to the node user and exits 1 `config_invalid`; and the new integration test, run inside the container as uid 1000 with the repository mounted, takes its fail-closed branch (`[sandbox] unavailable: fail-closed verified`, 5/5). With **`crawler/docker/seccomp-userns.json`** (Docker's default profile, vendored from moby/profiles, Apache-2.0, plus one rule allowing `clone`/`clone3`/`unshare` — the default profile denies `clone(CLONE_NEWUSER)` to non-root users, which is exactly why `--no-sandbox` existed): the probe reports `sandboxOn: true, workerUid: 30000` and exits 0, and the same in-container test renders the fixture gallery sandboxed and finds its planted image (`[sandbox] available: rendered and found`, 5/5). The script's own recorded gotcha: the image bakes `NODE_ENV=production`, so its in-container `npm ci` needs `--include=dev` before vitest can run.

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New: `tests/unit/browser-sandbox.test.ts` (13: every branch of the sandbox decision; the sandboxed flag list never carries `--no-sandbox` and the override path's does; the override config is a strict literal defaulting to false and garbage fails startup naming the key; the worker env is exactly the five-name allowlist with no key, key-file path or `/proc` grant, argv likewise; the launch protocol requires `sandboxed`, the ready answer carries the mode and a non-negative uid, and the worker's refusal has its own code). `tests/unit/browser-renderer.test.ts` gained three tests through the real IPC boundary with a new no-sandbox fake worker (22 now: the sandbox-on launch recorded in the summary booleans; a refused sandbox fails closed, sticky, logged once, no respawn; the override re-spawns unsandboxed, recorded as `sandboxOn: false`). New `tests/integration/browser-sandbox.test.ts` (5, real Chromium): a strict no-override crawl branches on the runtime's verdict — fail-closed (BROWSER pages `error` under the code, the browser-only image never requested, AUTO pages keep their static matches, one refusal logged, clean log scan) or sandboxed render (planted image found) — plus an override crawl proving the escape hatch renders where the sandbox is refused and changes nothing where it works, and the `[sandbox]` markers the docker script greps.
- Changed on purpose (rule 6; reasons here and in the commit message): the shared fixture env sets `CRAWLER_ALLOW_UNSANDBOXED_BROWSER: "true"` because the check container runs the suite as root, where Chromium's sandbox can never start (Chromium refuses root without `--no-sandbox`); the browser integration tests exercise rendering behaviour, not the sandbox decision, which the dedicated tests and the docker script pin. `tests/unit/config-policy.test.ts` tolerates exactly one name in its ALLOW-scan — `CRAWLER_ALLOW_UNSANDBOXED_BROWSER`, mandated by the WP text — and gained a new static test that nothing under `src/net/` or `src/crawl/` mentions the override, keeping the guarantee that it can never touch the network policy. The old `CHROMIUM_ARGS` constant assertions moved to `chromiumArgs(true/false)`.
- The existing browser suites (browser, escape, light, quiet, frames, memory-only) pass unchanged: 38 tests through the unsandboxed override in the root check container, exactly as before the change.
- Package totals, from the final `check.sh` run (Node 22) — `crawler`: lint, type-check, 48 test files / 551 tests all passed, build ok (the new fourth entrypoint, `dist/sandbox-check.js`, included). `asmita`: lint, type-check, `prisma validate` ok, 143 test files / 1225 tests all passed (unchanged by this WP; only its runbook gained the sandbox sections).

**Not done or not verified**

- The crawler row in root `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated, following the WP1–WP90 precedent: that protected agent-instruction file needs the owner's approval, which did not come. Intended addition (apply on approval): after the browser sentence — "crawler sandbox WP25: Chromium's own sandbox is self-tested at the first browser launch and rendering fails closed without it (the only override, CRAWLER_ALLOW_UNSANDBOXED_BROWSER, is local development only); the container's browser worker runs as a separate uid that cannot read the key file, and the run summary records the verdict (browser.sandboxOn / browser.workerUid)".
- The seccomp behaviour was verified on Docker Engine 29.9.3 only; older engines' defaults, Cloud Run's and Cloudflare Containers' sandboxes were not surveyed beyond what the probe measures at runtime. Notably Docker 29's default profile still denies `unshare` outright and `clone(CLONE_NEWUSER)` to non-root users, so scenario A held exactly as the task wrote it; the older assumption "rendering runs under `--no-sandbox` in Docker but is unsafe" is now "rendering is disabled in default Docker until the userns profile is passed".
- The Cloudflare Containers secret still travels as an env var (Containers offer no file mounts); the uid separation covers the worker against it (a different uid cannot read the parent's `/proc/<pid>/environ`), and `deploy/crawler-cloudflare/src/index.ts` now documents that reasoning in code. Cloud Run should keep using `CRAWLER_SERVICE_KEY_FILE`.
- No real site was crawled (rule 7). The image was verified with `--read-only` in both sandbox modes; the seccomp profile is vendored, not generated, so it will age with the moby default it patches.

**Deviations from the task text, with reasons**

- "At startup the crawler runs a self-test": read as the first browser need of the run (the sandboxed launch plus the about:blank probe), not a Chromium launch in every pass including the many that never render — that would contradict the documented laziness ("a run whose sources need none never spawns the worker") and tax every hourly pass. The verdict is a property of the runtime, recorded when first observed; `dist/sandbox-check.js` answers it directly, key-free and network-free, for checking a deployment without enabling any browser source.
- "Docker's default seccomp profile (expect sandbox off)": on this host's Docker 29 the default is exactly that for the image's non-root user, so the expectation held as written; both scenarios were measured, not assumed.
- The run summary's `browser` object reports the worker's uid (`workerUid`) beyond the letter of "booleans and counts": a uid is an id, never an address, and it is the one production-visible proof that the container's uid separation is actually active (`30000` in the image, the runner's uid outside it, `-1` when no worker answered).
- The key-file `0400` crawler-owned invariant is enforced by the entrypoint only when the platform starts the container as root (Cloud Run mounts secret files root-owned; a non-root start relies on the platform's own mount permissions, and read-only bind mounts refuse the chown — the runbook tells the operator to mount the key as the crawler user's file).
## Web audit

### WP41. Leave nothing behind on a shared phone (app) — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

WP41 is one of the owner-approved web best-practice audit packages (20 November 2026, Saquib); like the video lane it is defined outside this spec, so this entry records what the audit asked for and what was built. Everything is in `asmita/`; no `crawler/` source changed, so the crawler contract, `crawler/README.md` and the runbook are untouched. The audit-facing record (what changed, decisions, what was not verified) is `asmita/docs/product/web-audit-progress.md`, new in this WP.

**What was built**

- S1 — sign-out now clears the browser, not just the cookies. Both logout routes (`src/app/api/auth/logout/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/auth/logout/route.ts`) send `Clear-Site-Data: "cache", "storage"` next to the Set-Cookie expiry, so with the session the browser drops the origin's HTTP cache and its storage — localStorage, sessionStorage, IndexedDB, service worker registrations. The three client sign-out paths — quick exit, the idle sign-out (`QuickExit.tsx`) and `CloseDashboardButton.tsx` — also clear the tab's sessionStorage before `location.replace` (new helper `src/lib/clear-tab-storage.ts`), because the keepalive logout may never finish; that client-side clear is what actually guarantees the submit draft (`asmita_submit_draft`, her typed URLs and hash metadata) and the eligibility answers (`asmita_eligibility`) are gone from the tab. Storage inventory, every key the app holds: those two sessionStorage keys (cleared client-side on every quick exit, idle sign-out and dashboard close, and by both logout routes' header) and the one localStorage key `asmita-admin-shortcuts` (the admin keyboard-shortcut preference, cleared by the admin logout's header). The `asmita_lang` language-preference cookie stays persistent on purpose (decision below).
- S2 — authenticated HTML pages (`/case/*`, `/my-cases`, `/review-sign`, `/submit`, `/track`, `/admin/*`) are served `Cache-Control: no-store` via `next.config.ts` headers (rules after the catch-all so they win the key). Verified on the production Worker bundle: `scripts/worker-smoke.mjs` now asserts no-store on `/submit`, so vinext honours the override. `QuickExit` also reloads on a back/forward-cache restore (pageshow with `persisted = true`): a bfcache restore puts a frozen, signed-out copy of a page back on screen with everything it held when it was left, including a filled form.
- S10 — the `asmita_eligible` cookie is a session cookie with no Max-Age (`src/lib/eligibility.ts`): the 24-hour window let a shared phone reopen `/submit` a day later without the age questions. It still lasts the whole browser session, and everything else about it is unchanged (the deletion cookie keeps its Max-Age=0; the server-side read in `(auth)/submit/page.tsx` is untouched).

**Decisions, recorded per the standing rule**

- The audit puts the pageshow listener "in the victim layout"; it lives in `QuickExit` instead — the component the victim layout renders on every signed-in page — because `/submit`, `/register` and `/track` sit in the `(auth)` route group and can hold a filled form behind the same restore. One implementation covers every page that has quick exit, including the pages the audit's own e2e covers.
- `asmita_lang` (one-year language preference) is left persistent: a UI preference is not case data or signed-in state, and clearing it would flip the site's language for the next visitor of a shared phone. `/register` keeps default caching (pre-session, not in the audit's page list). The admin sign-out button clears nothing client-side (the audit asks for client-side clears on the three survivor paths only; the admin route's header covers the admin UI's storage).
- No new or changed user-facing string: `en.json`, `hi.json` and `hi-review-status.json` are untouched (audit rule: every new string would need both locales).

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New `tests/unit/logout-clear-site-data.test.ts` (4 tests): both routes send `Clear-Site-Data: "cache", "storage"` and expire the session cookies (the admin cookie at both paths it was ever set); a forged logout clears nothing; the eligibility cookie carries no Max-Age while its deletion keeps Max-Age=0.
- `tests/e2e/quick-exit.spec.ts` +2 tests, both projects: quick exit from a filled `/submit` leaves `sessionStorage` empty and `page.goBack()` brings no form or case data back; a simulated bfcache restore (a synthetic `pageshow` with `persisted = true`, constructible in Chromium — checked) reloads the page, while an ordinary pageshow (`persisted = false`) does not.
- `scripts/worker-smoke.mjs`: asserts no-store on one authenticated page (`/submit` with the eligibility cookie) and Clear-Site-Data plus cookie expiry on both logout routes.
- E2E and Worker runs on this machine against a throwaway Postgres 17: the three specs sharing the changed paths (quick-exit, filing, reporting-audit) — 20 runs passed on both projects against the Node dev server (3.9 min); the worker smoke drove the production bundle in workerd and passed with the new assertions. The full Playwright run happens in the separate verify step.
- Package totals from the final `check.sh` run on the committed tree (Node 22): `crawler` — lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok (untouched by this WP, re-confirmed green). `asmita` — lint 0 errors (4 pre-existing warnings: three in the untouched `scripts/translate-faq.mjs` and the pre-existing `buttonRef` cleanup warning in `QuickExit.tsx`, present on the parent commit, its line number only moved), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 153 test files / 1254 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- The full Playwright suite runs in a separate verify step; this lane ran the three specs that share the changed paths plus the worker smoke. No manual testing on a real shared phone was done.
- A real back/forward-cache restore is not deterministic in a test runner; the e2e simulates it with a synthetic persisted pageshow event (the constructible `PageTransitionEvent` was verified in Chromium first).
- Clear-Site-Data is processed only on secure origins; production and staging are HTTPS. The client-side clears do not depend on it.
- The local TCP forwarder and throwaway Postgres used for the e2e and worker-smoke runs are host tooling, outside the repository (as for the video lane); they were deleted before the commit.
- Root `CLAUDE.md` was not updated (no crawler behaviour changed; protected agent-instruction file).

### WP53. Fix the branch's one Playwright failure: the deletion request's CSRF token (app) — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

WP53 is a repair package for this branch's verify run, defined outside this spec like the other web-audit WPs: the full Playwright run failed one test, `tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts:80` ("adult dashboard supports add URL, manual resolve, and deletion request, with no broken tools") — after "Schedule deletion" the POST to `/api/account/delete` came back not-ok, the form showed its error line, and no server-side error appears anywhere in the run log. The audit-facing record (what changed, what was not verified, decisions) is `asmita/docs/product/web-audit-progress.md` (WP53 section). No `crawler/` source changed.

**What was built** — `asmita/src/lib/client-csrf.ts` (`csrfFetch`, used by every mutating client call) no longer trusts every `/api/csrf` answer: a non-OK response, or a 200 body without a token, used to leave `token` undefined and send the POST with the literal header `x-csrf-token: undefined` — a silent server-side 403 `csrf_failed`, exactly the failing run's signature. A bad mint is retried once and otherwise rejects without sending anything. Mints are also serialized per tab: two overlapping `csrfFetch`es used to race, and the second mint's `Set-Cookie` could land before the first POST left, invalidating its token — the same silent 403; the queue holds the turn until the POST's `fetch()` has started (when the browser attaches the cookie). `DeleteAccountForm.tsx` now shows its existing error line when the mint fails instead of hanging on "Scheduling…".

**Tests** (all run, all passing)

- New `asmita/tests/unit/client-csrf.test.ts` (6 tests): the fresh token is sent; a non-OK mint is retried once and no garbage token ever leaves; a tokenless 200 body is treated as a failed mint with nothing sent; nothing is sent after two failed mints; concurrent mints are serialized so the second mint waits for the first POST to leave; the queue stays usable after a failed mint. All six fail against the old code (verified by running them against it before applying the fix) and pass against the new code.
- E2E on this machine against a throwaway Postgres 17: `smoke.spec.ts` + `quick-exit.spec.ts` — 14 passed, including the previously failing dashboard test and every test WP41 added.
- Package totals from the final `check.sh` run on the committed tree (Node 22): `crawler` — lint, type-check, 29 test files / 350 tests, build ok (untouched by this WP, re-confirmed green). `asmita` — lint 0 errors, type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, 154 test files / 1260 tests all passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- The failure did not reproduce: eight further full-suite runs on the unchanged code (one traced, three warm, four with a cold `.next`, plus a standalone instrumented twin of the failing test — roughly 1600 token mints logged, every mint/POST pair matched) all passed. The fix addresses the two mechanisms that can physically produce the observed signature (a resolved non-OK POST with a clean server log); which of them fired in the original run cannot be proven after the fact.
- The task's premise that WP41's changes broke the test could not be confirmed: none of WP41's changes can reach this request in the e2e environment. `next dev` already serves `Cache-Control: no-store` on every path (checked with curl against the running dev server), the failing test never logs out, restores a bfcache page or clears storage (so Clear-Site-Data, the client storage clears and the pageshow reload never fire), and the `asmita_eligible` cookie only gates `/submit`, which the test passes signed in. Every WP41 protection is untouched and its tests pass.
- The full Playwright suite runs in the separate verify step.

### WP63. Merge master into feat/watch-mode; resolve conflicts — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22).

WP63 is a merge-resolution package, defined outside this spec like the other lane packages: master had moved on (video fingerprints WP17/WP20/WP21, the SSCD layer WP28, crawler v3, the web-audit lane) and this branch's open PR could not merge. A merge of origin/master into this branch had been started and left with conflicts in six files. This WP resolved them keeping both sides' behaviour and tests, fixed the real breakage the merge caused, and added no feature work. No `crawler/` file was touched by the resolutions or the fixes — the crawler side of the merge was conflict-free and arrived whole.

**What was resolved** (merge commit `1f7eda0`)

- `asmita/src/app/start/page.tsx`: the branch's conditional watch card joined master's restructured path-cards grid, and master's new `/track` link follows the branch's minor-link paragraph below the grid, so nothing renders twice.
- `asmita/src/lib/crawl-pool.ts`: the branch's watch pool rule (`OPEN_CASE_STATUSES`, `isCaseOpen`, `poolCoverage` — live consents further cut to open cases or a running re-upload window) is the coverage master's WP20 video entries and WP28 SSCD embeddings are read through; master's pool entry types (`CrawlPoolImageEntry | CrawlPoolVideoEntry`, `sscd`) replace the branch's pre-WP20 image-only shape the rest of the merged file already uses.
- `asmita/src/lib/crawl-store.ts`: both new memory maps kept — master's `embeddings` (WP28) and the branch's `linkVerifications` (WP23) — in the seed object and the hot-reload backfills.
- `asmita/tests/unit/notice-template-seeds.test.ts`: all three tests kept — the branch's watch-only authorisation wording (WP22) and master's two WP28 authorisation-text tests.
- `asmita/tests/unit/video-hash-kysely-path.test.ts` and `video-hash-submission.test.ts`: master's WP20 expectations win — a VPDQ fingerprint resolves by pool id and is served as `{ kind: "video", frames }`, because WP20 supersedes the pre-WP20 rule 3 the branch's assertions still encoded and the merged `crawl-pool.ts` implements WP20 — with the branch's open-`Case` fixture kept, because the merged pool serves fingerprints only while the case is open or inside its re-upload window (WP24).

**Merge breakage fixed** (rule 6: every existing-test change and its reason is in the merge commit message)

- Type-level: the branch's memory-finding fixtures gained master's required `sscdSimilarity`/`method` fields (`null`/`"PDQ"` — they are ordinary PDQ findings): `crawl-watch.test.ts`, `link-verification.test.ts`, `watch-flow.test.ts`. The branch's select-shape assertions gained master's `videoFramesEncrypted` column: `crawl-watch-kysely.test.ts`, `crawl-watch-prisma.test.ts`, `link-verification-prisma.test.ts`.
- Behaviour-level: master's embeddings fixtures (`crawl-embeddings-kysely/prisma.test.ts`) and video pool memory test (`video-crawl-pool.test.ts`) gained the open `Case` row the branch's pool rule reads. `ensurePriorityCrawlPage` (branch, WP23) stores its depth-0 re-check row at `FRONTIER_PRIORITY_SEED` (50) on all three backends like master's seed rows, instead of the column default 100.
- The merge closed a new static import cycle `crawl-findings -> watch-ops -> crawl-consent -> crawl-embeddings -> crawl-findings`, whose initialisation order left `crawlUrlSchema` undefined while `crawl-embeddings` built its Zod schemas at module top level (8 embeddings tests failed with `Invalid element at key "pageUrl"`). ### WP72. Resolve the master merge on perf/hash-worker — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22, on the merge commit `3c8ddb7`). This was a merge-conflict resolution, not feature work: master had moved on (PR #86 `feat/csp-reporting-headers`, PR #93 `feat/watch-mode`, and the merges under them), and a merge of `origin/master` into `perf/hash-worker` had been started and left with conflicts. No feature code was added, no test was deleted or skipped, and `reviewedByLegal` was not set anywhere.

**Conflicts.** Exactly one file was conflicted: `asmita/src/lib/csp.ts`. Both sides had changed the same region: master's WP46 added `'report-sample'` to `style-src`; this branch's WP42 had inserted `worker-src 'self'` (the PDQ hashing Web Worker) just above it. The resolution keeps both: WP46's reporting (`style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' 'report-sample'`, with the `script-src` sibling, the reporting constants and `report-uri`/`report-to`, which merged clean) and WP42's worker-src entry.

**The real work was a WP38 × WP42 contradiction the earlier merge had baked in (verified, not assumed).** The branch tip (`807ea0a`, the merge-queue merge of 2026-11-20) already failed 3 tests on Node 22 (vitest run on the branch-tip tree before resolving: 2 in `csp.test.ts`, 1 in `hash-worker.test.ts`, 30 passed): WP38 (c2pa) and WP42 (hashing worker) are parallel branches that had been combined mechanically into TWO `worker-src` directives in one policy — `worker-src 'self'` early, `worker-src 'self' blob:` late. A CSP policy enforces only its FIRST `worker-src`, so on `/check-image` and `/c2pa/*` the blob: entry was silently ignored and the C2PA engine's dev-mode inline worker was blocked: WP38's behaviour was lost, not just its test text. The resolution is ONE conditional directive — `worker-src 'self' blob:` when `c2pa` (WP38's dev reader), `worker-src 'self'` everywhere else (WP42's hashing worker, never a blob) — which keeps both behaviours real; master's own c2pa entry moved up into it, nothing of either side dropped.

**Tests changed because behaviour changed on purpose** (rule 6, also in the merge commit message; none deleted, skipped or loosened — each changed assertion was written against the pre-merge world and is unsatisfiable together with the other side's tests in the same file):

- `csp.test.ts` (WP38-era, master's): two `worker-src` `toBeUndefined()` assertions for non-c2pa pages became `toBe("worker-src 'self'")` plus a `not.toContain("blob:")` check — WP42 added `worker-src 'self'` to every policy on this branch, and the old assertions contradict WP42's own test in the same file. Every WP38 scoping assertion (wasm-unsafe-eval on /check-image only, blob: workers on the c2pa pages only) is unchanged and still enforced, and both updated spots pin the absence of blob: outside the c2pa pages.
- `hash-worker.test.ts` (WP42-era, this branch's): the raw-source assertion `not.toContain("worker-src 'self' blob:")` pinned "no blob workers anywhere"; written before WP38's c2pa worker-src reached this file, it already failed at the branch tip. Replaced with a pin of the exact conditional — blob: exists in worker-src only behind the c2pa flag — the strongest true invariant after the merge.

**Everything else merged clean.** Only three other files were touched by both sides (`asmita/docs/product/web-audit-progress.md`, `asmita/tests/unit/csp.test.ts`, `crawler/docs/v2-progress.md`) and git merged them without conflict. Verified on the merged tree: the i18n JSONs (`en.json`, `hi.json`, `hi-review-status.json` — the WP72 example file) parse and keep every key of both sides (union check, no key either side added is missing, no duplicates); both progress docs keep every entry each side added (line-level check); `csp.test.ts`'s auto-merge is what carried both sides' contradictory tests, handled above.

**Checks (run, not assumed).** Full `check.sh` (Node 22, both packages) on the resolved tree, which is exactly the tree committed as the merge: `crawler` — npm ci, lint, type-check, **58 test files / 628 tests passed**, build ok (`dist/main.js`, `browser-worker.js`, `decoder-worker.js`, `sscd-worker.js` all emitted). `asmita` — npm ci, `prisma generate`, lint 0 errors, type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, **191 test files / 1805 tests passed**. The two directly affected files pass 36/36 (`csp.test.ts` 16, `hash-worker.test.ts` 20) against the branch tip's 3 failed / 30 passed.

**Not done or not verified**

- The heading/content misalignment in `web-audit-progress.md` described in the WP65 entry is still present (both sides' content is fully kept; restructuring the doc was out of scope for a merge).
- The roughly 140 files only master changed were taken as-is; they are covered by master's own checks plus this full run.
- The Playwright e2e suite and the worker smoke were not run (check.sh does not include them and the work package defines done as check.sh passing).

## WP78. Dependency audit green again — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed.** A lane work package outside `full-crawler-spec.md` (like WP70/WP72/WP69 before it); this entry follows the spec's Reporting section. No application code, no crawler code and no test was added, changed or deleted.

**What the check is.** Read from `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: the "Dependency audit" job (`security-audit`) — the only audit gate in CI — installs `asmita/` with `npm ci` and runs `npm run security:audit`, which is `npm audit --audit-level=high --omit=dev`. Only `asmita/` is covered: `crawler-ci.yml` has no audit step, `tools/` has no workflow at all, and the only "audit" match in `ai-review.yml` is prose in the review prompt.

**What was failing.** The exact command, run through the Node 22 helper, reported "8 vulnerabilities (5 moderate, 3 high)" — npm counts every package in a vulnerable chain — over two underlying advisories:

- effect 3.20.0, [GHSA-7589-6fvf-x4pw](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-7589-6fvf-x4pw) (high, ReDoS in `Schema.TemplateLiteral` compiled regex, vulnerable `<=3.22.0`), reached through `@prisma/config`, the prisma CLI's config loader. The old lockfile did not flag the package dev, so `--omit=dev` audited it anyway.
- fflate 0.7.3 — satori's nested copy — [GHSA-px8p-9vwx-vf98](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-px8p-9vwx-vf98) (moderate, `unzipSync` can enter an infinite loop parsing malformed ZIP64 archives, vulnerable `>=0.7.0 <0.7.5`), in the production chain `vinext` → `@vercel/og` → `satori` → `fflate` (OG image generation).

**What was built.** Two npm `overrides` in `asmita/package.json`, lockfile regenerated with the Node 22 helper; the lockfile diff is exactly two version bumps, nothing else moved:

- `"effect": "^3.22.5"` — effect 3.20.0 → 3.22.5 (first patched line is 3.22.1; 3.22.5 is its newest patch), now correctly flagged `devOptional` in the lockfile.
- `"satori": { "fflate": "0.7.5" }` — satori's nested fflate 0.7.3 → 0.7.5 (the patched version). Scoped to satori so `@vitest/ui`'s dev-only fflate 0.8.3 (declared `^0.8.2`, not in the vulnerable range) keeps its own copy; `@shuding/opentype.js`'s nested copy was already the patched 0.7.5.

New ops doc `asmita/docs/ops/dependency-audit.md` records the gate, the reasoning, the state after the fix and the remaining dev-tree advisories. The workflow, the audit command and its threshold are untouched; no `--force`, no `--legacy-peer-deps`, nothing silenced.

**Decisions recorded**

- Overrides rather than direct-dependency upgrades, because no upgrade path exists (checked against the registry, not assumed): prisma 7.10.0 and 7.10.1 — the newest stable — both pin effect `3.20.0` exactly; even the newest satori (0.63.2) still pins fflate `0.7.3`, and the newest vinext (1.4.6) still carries the same `@vercel/og` 1.0.3 chain. So the work package's first option ("upgrade the direct dependency") had no reachable fix; the override is its second option, and the smallest change.
- The fflate override is scoped to satori only (npm's nested-override form) so no dev tool's declared range is violated.
- picomatch [GHSA-fc5q-39xc-vjpf](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-fc5q-39xc-vjpf), named in the work package, needed nothing: the advisory was withdrawn upstream before this WP ran ("not a security issue"), npm's audit database no longer returns it for any version in the tree (verified against the bulk advisory endpoint, including the production copy picomatch 2.3.2 under `vinext` → `vite-plugin-commonjs` → `fast-glob` → `micromatch`), and the audit that failed listed only the two advisories above.
- The dev-tree advisories that `--omit=dev` excludes were left alone on purpose: the CI gate audits production dependencies only, and fixing the dev ones (vitest / `@vitest/mocker` < 4.1.12, minimatch 10.2.6 under typescript-estree < 11.0.1, basic-ftp 5.3.1 under `@lhci/cli`'s get-uri, lodash-es 4.17.23 pinned by chevrotain under lighthouse — every one verified dev-only, not prod-reachable) means dev-dependency churn with no effect on the gate. They are listed in the ops doc for a future work package.

**Checks (run, not assumed)** — all on Node 22 through the lane helpers:

- The exact CI sequence in `asmita/`, `npm ci` then `npm run security:audit`: **found 0 vulnerabilities**, exit 0 (the pre-fix run of the same command had failed).
- Full `check.sh`: `crawler` — npm ci, lint, type-check, **58 test files / 628 tests passed**, build ok; `asmita` — npm ci, `prisma generate`, lint, type-check, `prisma validate`, **208 test files / 2026 tests passed**. Printed ALL CHECKS PASSED.
- `npm run build` in `asmita/` with CI's dummy env values, exit 0. The `prisma generate` inside the install had already exercised `@prisma/config` against effect 3.22.5, and the whole suite passed on top of the upgraded packages.

**Not done or not verified**

- The Playwright e2e suite was not run: nothing user-facing changed (two lockfile version pins only), `check.sh` does not include it, and the work package defines done as the full checks plus the audit plus the build.
- No new tests were added: a version pin has no behaviour of its own to test; the existing suites (628 crawler, 2026 asmita tests) passing on the upgraded tree is the verification. The dev-tree advisories above are documented, not fixed.

**Commits.** `df99a27` "WP78: make the CI dependency audit pass again (effect, fflate)" (`asmita/package.json`, `asmita/package-lock.json`, new `asmita/docs/ops/dependency-audit.md`), then this entry. Not pushed, per the work package.
