### WP81. Fix the gitleaks secret-scan failure on fix/host-cookies-fetch-metadata (PR #85) — 2026-11-20

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22) plus the two verifications this WP is actually about: gitleaks 8.24.3 run locally with the exact CI invocation, and the branch's cookie/CSRF/logout Playwright specs via `e2e-some.sh`. A CI-fix work package, not in `full-crawler-spec.md` (the spec's "Rules that do not change" were followed; the crawler spec itself is untouched — no crawler behaviour changed). No application or crawler source changed, no test was changed, weakened, skipped or deleted, and `reviewedByLegal` was not set anywhere. Attempt 2 (2026-11-21) added one thing on top: the remote branch had moved while attempt 1 ran, so the fix commits were merged onto the branch's new tip (`7d3701c`) and every verification was re-run green on the merged tree — see the Attempt 2 paragraph.

**The real cause.** The required "Secret scanning (gitleaks)" check fails on PR #85 with 7 findings, all in the branch's first commit `b7f9220` (WP47, the `__Host-` cookie migration) and all the same two values: `synthetic-csrf-secret-0123456789abcdef` and `synthetic-jwt-secret-0123456789abcdef`, which the WP47 unit tests stub into `CSRF_SECRET`/`JWT_SECRET` (`tests/unit/cookie-migration.test.ts`, `tests/unit/csrf.test.ts`, `tests/unit/api-routes.test.ts`). jose refuses short HS256 keys, so the fixtures must be full-length strings; they are synthetic, test-only and cannot be production credentials, but gitleaks' `generic-api-key` rule cannot tell (entropy 4.35/4.47 against its 3.5 threshold). Not a leak — a false positive on test fixtures, reproduced locally with gitleaks 8.24.3 before touching anything: the exact CI command (`detect --redact -v --exit-code=2 --log-opts=--no-merges --first-parent b7f9220^..1123d37`, the range the failing job ran) reported the same 7 findings, exit 2.

**The fix.** One file: `.gitleaks.toml`. The repo allowlist gains two stopwords — a finding whose secret contains `synthetic-csrf-secret` or `synthetic-jwt-secret` is a known false positive. Narrow on purpose: a real secret never contains `synthetic-`, so nothing real is excused. **Verified, not assumed:** with the new config, gitleaks 8.24.3 still catches a planted high-entropy `api_secret` sitting in the same file where the synthetic fixture is ignored (negative test: exactly that one finding, exit 2); and the exact CI scan range reports **no leaks, exit 0** where the same command reported 7 before. After the fix commit `8ea64ad`, the range the next CI run will scan (`b7f9220^..HEAD`, 4 commits — the fix commit's own diff included) also reports **no leaks, exit 0**, so the commit that carries the stopwords is itself covered.

**Decision recorded** — an allowlist entry, not a code change or a history rewrite, is the fix:
- The offending commit is already pushed and sits first in every scan range gitleaks-action computes for this PR (`base^..head` over the branch's own commits, config read from HEAD), so changing the test literals would not clear the check: only the HEAD config can suppress the historical finding, or a force-push rewrite — which this WP does not do ("do not push", and the branch carries master merge commits a rewrite would have to redo).
- The tests stay as they are: the literals are the honest way to give jose a full-length key and the CSRF HMAC a real one; dodging the scanner with split strings or low-entropy filler would hide the fixture from every reader instead of declaring it, and would not fix the pushed commit either.
- The stopwords are scoped to the fixture naming convention (`synthetic-*-secret`), not to whole paths, so future real leaks in test files stay caught.

**Attempt 2 (2026-11-21): the fix had to be merged onto a moved branch — done, re-verified green.** While attempt 1 ran, the driver pushed `fb63915` to `origin/fix/host-cookies-fetch-metadata` (master's PR #80 review-labels content merged into the branch), so the driver's push of the fix HEAD (`0344249`, based on `1123d37`) was rejected non-fast-forward: correct work on a stale base could not land. Decision recorded: merge `origin/fix/host-cookies-fetch-metadata` (`fb63915`) into the lane — the same integration this branch already did in WP71 when master moved under it — rather than any history rewrite (forbidden here, and the branch carries merge commits a rewrite would redo). One conflict only, `crawler/docs/v2-progress.md` (both sides had appended entries at the end), resolved as the union: master's WP73 merge-lane entry kept in chronological order before the WP81 entry. Everything else merged clean — the incoming side is PR #80's finding review labels (admin findings UI, `crawl-findings`/`crawl-stats`/`crawl-store`, the labels route, four `finding-labels*` test suites and the `20261119010000_finding_review_labels` migration), files this branch never touched; `.gitleaks.toml` exists only on this side and was carried unchanged into the merge. On the merged tree the exact CI invocation (`detect --redact --exit-code=2 --log-opts=--no-merges --first-parent b7f9220^..HEAD`) scans 5 commits with the HEAD config and reports **no leaks, exit 0**, and the negative control still holds (a planted high-entropy `api_secret` is caught beside an ignored synthetic fixture), so the incoming merge does not disturb the fix; re-run at the final entry commit it scans 6 commits — the entry text itself — and still reports **no leaks, exit 0**.

**Playwright.** No Playwright spec is failing on this branch — every CI run of PR #85 shows only gitleaks and the separately-handled Dependency audit failing; CI's own e2e job passes on the branch head. To honour the work package's instruction anyway, the specs covering the branch's changed behaviour were run locally via `e2e-some.sh` (Node 22, Chromium, Postgres 17): `security-hygiene` (CSRF failures logged, admin logout clears every session cookie it ever set), `admin`, `admin-access`, `quick-exit` and `smoke` — **21 tests passed** (2.7 m, `E2E_SOME_RC=0`). Re-run on the merged tree after the attempt-2 merge: the same five specs, **21 tests passed** again (2.7 m, `E2E_SOME_RC=0`).

**Checks (run, not assumed).** Full `check.sh` (Node 22, both packages) on the fixed tree: `crawler` — npm ci, lint, type-check, **58 test files / 628 tests passed**, build ok (`dist/main.js`, `browser-worker.js`, `decoder-worker.js`, `sscd-worker.js` all emitted). `asmita` — npm ci, `prisma generate`, lint and type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, **211 test files / 2067 tests passed** (up from WP71's 195/1870: the master merges under this branch brought the later suites). `check.sh` printed **ALL CHECKS PASSED**. Re-run on the merged tree (attempt 2): `crawler` 58 files / 628 tests and build ok again, `asmita` lint, type-check and `prisma validate` clean and **215 test files / 2117 tests passed** (the merge brought PR #80's four `finding-labels*` suites and its fixture updates) — `check.sh` printed **ALL CHECKS PASSED** again.

**Not done or not verified**

- Not pushed (per the work package; the PR update, the CI re-run and the merge are the owner's step).
- The Dependency audit check also fails on this PR and is explicitly out of this WP's scope (handled separately, per the work package).
- `npm run build` for asmita and `scripts/worker-smoke.mjs` are not part of `check.sh` and were not run (CI's "Production build" and e2e jobs pass on this branch head).
- GitHub's native secret scanning / push protection is still off (an owner open item in `asmita/docs/security/open-items-2026-11.md`); this WP fixes the gitleaks-action check only.

**Commits.** `8ea64ad` "WP81: fix the gitleaks secret-scan failure on the synthetic test secrets" (`.gitleaks.toml` only), then the attempt-1 progress entry `0344249`. Attempt 2 added the merge `7d3701c` "WP81: merge the remote branch tip (master #80) under the gitleaks fix" and this entry update. Not pushed.

### WP82. Fix the gitleaks secret-scan failure on feat/crawler-sandbox (PR #92) — 2026-11-21

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22) plus the two verifications this WP is actually about: gitleaks 8.24.3 run locally with the exact CI invocation, and the branch's admin/crawler Playwright specs via `e2e-some.sh`. A CI-fix work package, not in `full-crawler-spec.md` (the spec's "Rules that do not change" were followed; the crawler spec itself is untouched — no crawler behaviour changed). No application or crawler source changed, no test was changed, weakened, skipped or deleted, and `reviewedByLegal` was not set anywhere.

**The real cause.** The required "Secret scanning (gitleaks)" check fails on PR #92 with exactly one finding: commit `7507c5f` (WP25, the browser sandbox), `crawler/tests/unit/browser-sandbox.test.ts` line 83 — the synthetic service-key fixture that test pins ("cwk_" + `0123456789abcdef` three times over) to assert the key never reaches the browser worker's environment or argv. It is published test data that cannot be a production credential, but the `generic-api-key` entropy rule (4.17 against its 3.5 threshold) cannot tell. Reproduced locally with gitleaks 8.24.3 — the version the failing job pins — before touching anything: the exact CI command from the job log (`detect --redact --exit-code=2 --log-opts=--no-merges --first-parent 7507c5f^..e9b3f54`) reported the same one finding, exit 2. The structural reason only the config can fix it: gitleaks-action scans branch history (`--no-merges --first-parent base^..head`, config read from HEAD), so the already-pushed WP25 commit keeps failing whatever the file says today — editing the test now would not clear the check, and a history rewrite is out (this WP does not push, and the branch carries master merge commits a rewrite would redo).

**The branch moved under the lane mid-WP — fast-forwarded, no rewrite.** While the lane ran, the driver merged master (PR #85, WP81's identical gitleaks fix, and PR #80's review labels) into `feat/crawler-sandbox` (`e9b3f54` → `7ecc688`) and CI re-ran: gitleaks failed on the new tip too (job 136666236295) — WP81's stopwords cover only the `synthetic-*-secret` fixtures, not this branch's `cwk_` one. The lane had no unique commits at that point, so it fast-forwarded to `7ecc688` (no merge commit, no history edit) and the fix was built and verified on that tip: the scan over `7507c5f^..7ecc688` reported the same one finding, exit 2, before the fix.

**The fix.** One file: `.gitleaks.toml`. The repo allowlist gains one regex on the exact fixture literal, beside the WP81 `synthetic-*-secret` stopwords that arrived with the master merge. Narrower than a path entry or a stopword on purpose: only that one byte-exact published string is excused, and any other `cwk_...` value stays a finding. **Verified, not assumed**, all with gitleaks 8.24.3: with the fix, the failing range `7507c5f^..7ecc688` reports **no leaks, exit 0** (one leak, exit 2, before); a negative control — a planted high-entropy `api_secret` staged beside the ignored fixture — is still caught, exactly one finding, exit 2; and after the fix commit `e9404d2`, the range the next CI run will scan (`7507c5f^..HEAD`, 3 commits — the fix commit's own diff, which names the literal in the toml, included) reports **no leaks, exit 0**.

**Decisions recorded:**

- An allowlist entry, not a test edit: no behaviour changed on purpose, so no test is out of date (rule 6 does not bite); the fixture is the honest way to give the worker-scrubbing assertions a realistic key, and dodging the scanner with split strings would hide it from every reader without fixing the pushed commit either.
- Not a history rewrite and not a push: the offending commit is already on `origin`; the WP forbids pushing, and the branch carries merge commits a rewrite would redo.
- The allowlist names the whole literal, never the file path, so a real leak in that test file tomorrow is still caught.

**Playwright.** No Playwright spec is failing on this branch — the only failing checks on PR #92 are gitleaks (this WP) and the separately-handled Dependency audit; CI's own e2e job passes on both the old and the moved branch head. To honour the work package's instruction anyway, the specs covering the branch's admin/crawler surface were run locally via `e2e-some.sh` (Node 22, Chromium, Postgres 17): `crawler-journey`, `admin`, `admin-access`, `security-hygiene`, `smoke` — **17 tests passed** (1.6 m, `E2E_SOME_RC=0`).

**Checks (run, not assumed).** Full `check.sh` (Node 22, both packages) on the fixed tree: `crawler` — npm ci, lint, type-check, **60 test files / 650 tests passed**, build ok (`dist/main.js`, `browser-worker.js`, `decoder-worker.js`, `sscd-worker.js`, `sandbox-check.js` all emitted). `asmita` — npm ci, `prisma generate`, lint 0 errors (4 warnings, all pre-existing in files this branch never touched: three unused underscore vars in `scripts/translate-faq.mjs`, one react-hooks warning in `QuickExit.tsx`), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, **215 test files / 2117 tests passed**. `check.sh` printed **ALL CHECKS PASSED**.

**Not done or not verified**

- Not pushed (per the work package; the PR's CI re-run is the owner's step — the green result is proven locally with the pinned 8.24.3 binary, and by PR #85's identical fix passing the same check in CI).
- The Dependency audit check also fails on this PR and is explicitly out of this WP's scope (handled separately, per the work package).
- `npm run build` for asmita and `scripts/worker-smoke.mjs` are not part of `check.sh` and were not run (CI's "Production build" and e2e jobs pass on this branch head).

### WP84. Fix the Playwright e2e (with Postgres) failure on feat/ai-lab (PR #96) — 2026-11-21

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22) — plus the verifications this WP is actually about: the full Playwright e2e suite through `e2e-some.sh`, and a local replication of the CI job's remaining steps (`build:vinext` + `prepare-production-wrangler.mjs`, then `worker-smoke.mjs`) against a fresh Postgres 17. A CI-fix work package, not in `full-crawler-spec.md`; the "Rules that do not change" were followed, the crawler spec is untouched (no crawler behaviour changed), no test was changed, weakened, skipped or deleted, and `reviewedByLegal` was not set anywhere.

**The real cause.** The job's Playwright run did not fail — CI's own log shows "Running 83 tests" and "78 passed (2.5m)". (The Postgres noise in the job tail — `SELECT 1 FROM "<table>" LIMIT 1` answered "relation does not exist" for every table — is the database-hardening spec probing the `anon`/`authenticated` roles, which hold no table privileges by design; those denials are that test passing.) The job dies one step later, in `npm run build:vinext && node scripts/prepare-production-wrangler.mjs`: the script's line 8 parses `wrangler.jsonc` with strict `JSON.parse`, and this branch had added two JSONC comments beside the new `ENABLE_LAB` vars ("AI Lab (docs/lab/charter.md): off until the owner turns it on") — `SyntaxError: Expected double-quoted property name in JSON at position 787 (line 26 column 5)`, exit 1, so `scripts/worker-smoke.mjs` (the step after) never ran. The invariant "wrangler.jsonc: no comments (prepare script parses strict JSON)" was fixed twice before for this same breakage — `8a1d617` on master, `f9b4c6c` on feat/escalation-drafts-reviewed — but it lived only in commit messages, which the AI Lab work could not have seen.

**The fix.** `asmita/wrangler.jsonc`: the two comment lines are removed, nothing else. `ENABLE_LAB` keeps its values (staging vars and production vars both "false"), and the note the comments carried already lives in `docs/lab/charter.md`, `docs/lab/progress.md`, `src/lib/lab/flags.ts` and `tests/unit/lab-flags.test.ts` — all about the value, not the comment. New `asmita/tests/unit/wrangler-jsonc.test.ts` (2 tests) makes the invariant executable where it is cheapest to break: `wrangler.jsonc` must `JSON.parse` and must define `env.production`, so a re-added comment now fails the fast "Lint, types, unit tests" job instead of the e2e job. **Verified with a negative control** (Node 22 through the n22 helper): with a comment re-planted beside `ENABLE_LAB`, both tests fail with the same `SyntaxError: Expected double-quoted property name` the CI job showed; with the file restored, both pass.

**Decisions recorded:**

- Comments removed, not a JSONC-tolerant parser added: the repo decided this exact question twice before (`8a1d617`, `f9b4c6c`), `scripts/prepare-production-wrangler.mjs` sits on the `deploy:production` path, and a string-aware comment stripper (the config legitimately contains `//` inside URL values) would be a bigger change to deploy-critical tooling than this WP needs. The rule is now enforced by a unit test instead of living in old commit messages.
- No test was out of date (rule 6 does not bite): the branch deliberately changed no behaviour any test observes — CI's Playwright suite passed on the broken tip, and what failed was build preparation, not a test.
- The Dependency audit check fails on this PR and on master alike and is out of scope here (handled separately, per the work package).

**Playwright.** All 26 e2e spec files — the CI job's exact suite — through `e2e-some.sh` (Node 22, Chromium, throwaway Postgres 17 on 5437): **83 tests, 78 passed, 5 skipped, 8.5 m, `E2E_SOME_RC=0`**, matching CI's own 78-passed run on the branch tip.

**The CI job's remaining steps, replicated locally (what actually failed / never ran).** In `node:22-bookworm` (v22.23.5, the exact CI Node): `npx prisma migrate deploy` and `npx prisma db seed` into a fresh `asmita_e2e` Postgres 17, then `npm run build:vinext` ("Build complete"), then `node scripts/prepare-production-wrangler.mjs` — **"Prepared .../dist/server/wrangler.production.json"** (the step that failed in CI; the fix's direct proof), then `node scripts/worker-smoke.mjs` — **"Worker smoke passed: Prisma runs in workerd, each request gets its own connection, the provenance check is off, and voice headers, session limits, the chat route, the CSP reporting endpoint and the WP46 security headers work."** (`SMOKE_RC=0`.)

**Checks (run, not assumed).** Full `check.sh` (Node 22, both packages) on the fixed tree: `crawler` — npm ci, lint, type-check, **60 test files / 650 tests passed**, build ok (`dist/main.js` emitted). `asmita` — npm ci, `prisma generate`, lint 0 errors (4 warnings, all pre-existing and untouched by this WP: three unused underscore vars in `scripts/translate-faq.mjs`, one react-hooks warning in `QuickExit.tsx`), type-check clean, `prisma validate` ok, **223 test files / 2156 tests passed** (the two new among them). `check.sh` printed **ALL CHECKS PASSED**.

Green is proven locally on the fixed tree — the exact failing step, the step after it, and the whole Playwright suite — and master's identical e2e job is green in CI; this branch's only deviation from master's config was the comment this WP removes.

## AI Lab beta tools (WP89–WP96, asmita/docs/lab/beta-spec.md)

The beta-tools work packages, recorded here per the lane instructions; the
working entries live in `asmita/docs/lab/beta-progress.md`.

### WP90. Detector panel at /lab/detectors (feat/lab-beta-dev) — 2026-11-21

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22), plus the WP's Playwright spec through `e2e-some.sh`. A beta-tools WP (`asmita/docs/lab/beta-spec.md`), not in `full-crawler-spec.md`: no crawler behaviour changed, the crawler spec is untouched, no test was weakened, skipped or deleted, and `reviewedByLegal` was not set anywhere.

**What was built.** One image in, every configured "AI-made or manipulated" detector answers side by side. `asmita/src/lib/lab/detectors/adapters.ts` reuses the E1 detector adapter interface from `tools/lab-bench/src/detectors` (ported, not imported — lab-bench is an offline package outside the Worker build): OpenAI vision (`OPENAI_API_KEY`), Hive (`HIVE_API_KEY`), Sightengine (`SIGHTENGINE_USER`/`SIGHTENGINE_SECRET`), a keyless adapter shown as "not configured", provider-prefixed option names so no two adapters can share a credential field, and the same defensive response parsing. `panel.ts` maps each 0..1 score to the panel's 0..100 with a verdict band (likely AI ≥ 70, likely camera < 30, unclear between), an error row per failing or hung detector (45 s cap) that never sinks the others, and a "detectors disagree" note when at least two scored rows fall in different bands. `POST /api/lab/detectors`: `ENABLE_LAB` gate (503 `lab_disabled`), CSRF, declared-length pre-check, per-IP 10/hour shared bucket, strict Zod consent field, JPEG/PNG/WebP magic-byte sniff, 10 MB cap, in-memory-only image (never stored or logged; sent only to the configured providers the WP names), and the site-wide `LAB_DETECTOR_DAILY_LIMIT` (default 200, 24 h window) spent only by requests that passed every earlier check. The page 404s while the Lab is off and carries the Beta badge, the limits sentence, the `/minor-support` pointer, `role="status"` result announcement, and en+hi strings (Hindi machine-translated, flagged in `hi-review-status.json`).

**Decisions recorded** (details in `asmita/docs/lab/beta-progress.md`): no sharp in the app, so no downscale — the image goes to the providers exactly as uploaded; no local ONNX detector in the panel (the WP names the three hosted ones); the tool asks no age, so the under-18 rule is met with the pointer, and the required consent tick names where the image goes; `LAB_DETECTOR_DAILY_LIMIT` is not added to `.env.example` or `wrangler.jsonc` — WP96 collects the new env vars.

**Checks (run, not assumed).** `check.sh` (Node 22): crawler — npm ci, lint, type-check, 60 test files passed, build ok; asmita — npm ci, prisma generate, lint (0 errors, 4 pre-existing warnings in untouched files), type-check clean, prisma validate ok, 225 test files / 2192 tests passed or skipped as before (36 new: `lab-detectors.test.ts` 25, `lab-detectors-route.test.ts` 11, every adapter mocked, no network). `e2e-some.sh tests/e2e/lab-detectors.spec.ts`: 2 passed (en journey with a generated gradient PNG and three honest "not configured" rows; hi journey at 320 px). `check.sh` printed **ALL CHECKS PASSED**.

**Commits.** `c03bba8` "WP90: AI Lab detector panel at /lab/detectors" (the tool, tests, i18n, and `asmita/docs/lab/beta-progress.md`), then this entry. Not pushed.

### WP91. Photo protection at /lab/protect (feat/lab-beta-dev) — 2026-11-21

**Status: complete. All checks passed** (`check.sh`, both packages, Node 22), plus the WP's Playwright spec through `e2e-some.sh`. A beta-tools WP (`asmita/docs/lab/beta-spec.md`), not in `full-crawler-spec.md`: no crawler behaviour changed, the crawler spec is untouched, no test was weakened, skipped or deleted, and `reviewedByLegal` was not set anywhere.

**What was built.** Before posting a photo, a visitor can add an invisible protective layer meant to make AI morphing harder. `asmita/src/lib/lab/protect/perturbation.ts` is the layer as one pure, dependency-free module: seeded uniform noise, an integer change in [-8, +8] per colour channel of every pixel (the WP's epsilon <= 8/255, exactly 8 in 8-bit units), clamped to [0, 255], alpha untouched, deterministic per uint32 seed with a fresh crypto-random seed per run. The `/lab/protect` page (404 while the Lab is off) runs the whole tool in the visitor's browser — `createImageBitmap` (EXIF rotation applied) -> Canvas -> `protectPixels` -> a lossless PNG download — with the before/after pair side by side, the "they look the same on purpose" note, and the strong limits notice the WP requires (experimental, may not stop every tool, does not protect copies already online). Beta badge, the one-sentence limits note, the `/minor-support` pointer, `role="status"` announcement with a focus move, JPEG/PNG/WebP and 10 MB enforced client-side plus a 25-million-pixel canvas cap, en+hi strings (Hindi machine-translated, flagged in `hi-review-status.json`).

**Decisions recorded** (details in `asmita/docs/lab/beta-progress.md`): no suitable model is available on the Worker — `onnxruntime-node` is native code workerd cannot load and the SSCD weights are never in the repo, so an `onnxruntime-web` fallback would have nothing to run either — so the WP's fallback ladder lands on the documented simpler perturbation; the Worker cannot decode JPEG/WebP either (no server-side image decoder), so per the WP's own line the page works in the browser and the photo never leaves the visitor's device. No API route exists, so there is nothing to rate-limit and `LAB_PROTECT_DAILY_LIMIT` has no server budget to spend — recorded for WP96's env-var collection, not added to `.env.example` or `wrangler.jsonc`. Measurement (aggregate only, the WP's ask): on 5 synthetic portraits the shipped layer moves the pinned SSCD embedding by a cosine distance of min 0.1601 / median 0.1712 / mean 0.1739 / max 0.1987, against min 0.0181 / median 0.2660 / mean 0.2667 / max 0.5034 between two different portraits — evidence the layer is not cosmetic, measured against SSCD only, never a guarantee.

**Checks (run, not assumed).** `check.sh` (Node 22): crawler — npm ci, lint, type-check, 60 test files / 650 tests passed, build ok; asmita — npm ci, prisma generate, lint (0 errors, 4 pre-existing warnings in untouched files plus 2 new `no-img-element` warnings on the tool's blob-URL previews, the codebase's standing pattern for plain `<img>`), type-check clean, prisma validate ok, 226 test files / 2200 tests passed (8 new: `lab-protect.test.ts` — the required bound that no channel moves more than epsilon = 8, clamping, alpha, noise spread, determinism, validation). `e2e-some.sh tests/e2e/lab-protect.spec.ts`: 2 passed (en journey: badge, under-18 pointer, generated gradient PNG upload, side-by-side pair, limits notice, and a real download whose saved bytes carry the PNG magic; hi journey at 320 px). `check.sh` printed **ALL CHECKS PASSED**.

**Commits.** `a6aeac4` "WP91: AI Lab photo protection at /lab/protect" (the tool, tests, i18n, and `asmita/docs/lab/beta-progress.md`), then this entry. Not pushed.
